# Découpage et refactoring des modules surdimensionnés

Chaque module de plus de 3 800 lignes a été confié à un analyste distinct, avec pour consigne
de cartographier ses sections internes réelles (bornes de lignes vérifiées), puis de proposer
un découpage **mécanique** : quels symboles bougent, vers quel fichier, et ce que cela casse.
La consigne incluait explicitement le droit de conclure qu'un module doit rester tel quel.

Contraintes que toute proposition doit respecter : `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`,
`mise run rustdoc` (qui refuse un lien intra-doc cassé), et le garde-fou `src/docs_coverage.rs`.

## Synthèse

## `launch` — src/sandbox/launch.rs (8880 lines)

**Verdict : **Découpage fortement recommandé****

### Cartographie actuelle

`src/sandbox/launch.rs` (8,880 lines: 1–6,171 production, 6,172–8,880 the single inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`) is the whole launch pipeline plus four unrelated verbs that happen to reach a cage. It is declared `mod launch;` at `src/sandbox/mod.rs:20` and re-exported at `src/sandbox/mod.rs:141-145` (`SessionHeader, app, attach, detach_log_path, effective_lock_target, gc, parse_session_header, run, run_mise, stop, superseded_reclaimable_hint, upgrade_mise_packages, upgrade_provision_steps`). Four more items cross the module boundary directly: `pub(super) session_housekeeping`/`shared_store_gc` (read by `src/sandbox/projects.rs:164,293,657,729`) and `pub(super) seccomp_argv`/`status_code` (read by `src/sandbox/task.rs:1154,1221`, `src/sandbox/taskpool.rs:512`, `src/sandbox/resolver.rs:221`, and named in a doc link at `src/sandbox/spec.rs:221`).

Internal sections, verified (ranges are doc-block start .. last line of the item):
1. Header + imports 1-48; `Prepared` 50-86 (the launch prerequisites: bwrap/nix/nix_store/layout/cwd/cfg/nixpkgs/engine_ref/userland/quiet_equip).
2. `run` 88-164; the launch-mode decision helpers `observation_flags` 166-176, `may_exec_replace` 177-191, `observe_feed_absent_reason` 192-226, `warn_observe_feed_absent` 227-244, `apply_launch_override` 245-260, `launch` 261-282, `warn_ask_under_detach` 283-300, `launch_foreground` 301-357.

17. Registration + cage execution: `register` 5551-5577, `session_runtime` 5578-5587, `run_supervised` 5588-5596, `run_status` 5597-5624, `run_captured` 5625-5650, `seccomp_argv` 5718-5733, `status_code` 5734-5742, `exec` 5743-5767, `supervise` 5768-5879, `cstring` 5880-5884.
18. `passthrough_env` 5885-5896, `keep_passthrough` 5897-5911; `WrapLayer` 5912-5945, `CommandWrap` 5946-5948, `wrap_cage_command` 5949-5961; `EnvLayer` 5962-6001, `extra_cage_env` 6156-6163.
19. Start-up composition `provision_chain` 6002-6021, `service_log_path` 6022-6030, `service_arg` 6031-6043, `service_lines` 6044-6086, `compose_startup_cmd` 6087-6132, `provision_only_cmd` 6133-6149, `shell_quote` 6150-6155; `missing` 6165-6170.
20. Tests 6172-8880 — 60 `#[test]`s plus 8 helpers, interleaved so that tests for a given section sit hundreds or thousands of lines from it (e.g. `wrap_flake_equip` at 5464 is tested at 8077-8273).

State sharing: `Prepared` is the only cross-cutting value — `run`/`app`/`launch*`/`build`/the two upgrade rolls/`sweep_current`/`equip_for_gc` all take it. `attach` and `stop` share **none** of it (they read `session::Registry` and `super::attach`; `attach`'s own doc at 2609-2620 says it "provisions nothing and re-resolves no config"). `gc` shares `Prepared` and three provisioning primitives (`prebuilt_ctx`, `mise_tools`, `seed_project_store`) but never calls `build`. The rolls call `build` + `run_captured` only. `run`/`app`/`run_mise` share everything.

### Justification

The file is not one machine; it is one machine (`Prepared` → `build` → cage) with four unrelated verbs bolted on, and the project's own stated criterion for carving a module out already covers two of them. `src/sandbox/mod.rs:114-117` says `projects` is "Kept apart from `launch` because it shares no state with the launch pipeline". `attach` (2609-2896) and `stop` (2904-3055) meet that test exactly: 499 lines that never touch `Prepared`, never call `build`, never resolve a config, and enter namespaces bwrap already made. `gc` (1794-2473) and the two `sbx upgrade` rolls (1023-1764 + 5651-5717) are batch/maintenance drivers that borrow the launch machinery rather than being part of it. Together those three clusters are ~2,700 production lines that a reader auditing "what reaches bubblewrap" must page past.

The split is cheap because Rust privacy runs downward: everything private in `launch/mod.rs` (`Prepared`, `prepare*`, `observation_flags`, `may_exec_replace`, `prebuilt_ctx`, `mise_tools`, `seed_project_store`) stays reachable from every child with no annotation at all, and each child's `use super::*;` inherits the parent's import block exactly the way today's `mod tests { use super::*; }` at 6174 already does. Only child→parent and child→sibling edges need `pub(super)`, and I have enumerated every one of them below.

What I am **not** proposing: carving up `build` itself. Its 1,728 lines are a single linear assembly whose ~40 locals (`wraps`, `extra_binds`, `gui_env`, `notify_wiring`, `brokers`, `egress_env`, `tcp_plan`, `signer_ring`, `fs_masks`/`fs_decoys`, `task_socket`) are consumed in three places at the end (`extra_cage_env` 4952, `binds::build_spec` 5028, the `LaunchGuard` literal 5183). Extracting the GUI-hole block (4618-4801) or the provisioning prologue (3489-3854) would move 8-10 values across a signature and split the one surface `src/sandbox/mod.rs:4-7` asks a security review to audit in one place. `build` moves **whole** into `launch/build.rs`; that sharpens the seam (the function that stands up a cage, alone with the helpers only it uses) instead of dissolving it. `launch/build.rs` therefore stays the largest file at ~2,700 lines, and that is the honest floor.

The real cost is not cyclic dependencies — Rust modules may reference each other freely, so there is no cycle risk in any arrangement here — it is three mechanical sweeps: (a) ~174 `super::X` references inside the moved ranges must be rebased to `crate::sandbox::X` (counted per module below); (b) ~20 intra-doc links must gain a path prefix, and `mise run rustdoc` fails loudly on each one it misses rather than silently dropping it; (c) two source-scanning tests and two `docs_coverage.rs` allowlist entries name the file by path and must be repointed. All three are enumerable, and the compiler/rustdoc/test guards catch every miss.

### Découpage proposé

#### `src/sandbox/launch/mod.rs` (~1415 lignes)

**Rôle.** The launch entry points and the prerequisites everything else in the directory shares: `Prepared`, the `prepare*` chain, the launch-mode decision, and the `run`/`app`/`mise` verbs. Also holds the three provisioning primitives `build` and the gc sweep both need, precisely because a parent's private items are visible to every child without an annotation.

**Ce qui bouge.** STAYS (nothing moves in): header+imports 1-48; `Prepared` 50-86; `run` 88-164; `observation_flags` 166-176; `may_exec_replace` 177-191; `observe_feed_absent_reason` 192-226; `warn_observe_feed_absent` 227-244; `apply_launch_override` 245-260; `launch` 261-282; `warn_ask_under_detach` 283-300; `launch_foreground` 301-357; `AppOutcome` 759-775; `ARGV0_SHELLS` 777-779; `ends_with_shell_payload` 781-801; `app` 803-933; `network_posture_name` 935-943; `launch_foreground_learning` 945-988; `available_apps` 990-1000; `run_mise` 1002-1021; `prebuilt_ctx` 2344-2355; `launch_interactive_shell` 2475-2491; `launch_pty_supervised` 2492-2555; `prepare` 3057-3066; `prepare_with` 3067-3080; `launch_cwd` 3081-3088; `prepare_in` 3089-3100; `PreparedConfig` 3101-3115; `prepare_config` 3116-3144; `prepare_engines` 3145-3216; `effective_lock_target` 3217-3256; `seed_project_store` 5255-5276; `collect_roots` 5277-5300; `mise_tools` 5350-5378; `missing` 6165-6170. Gains `mod build; mod cage; mod detach; mod equip; mod reclaim; mod roll; mod session; mod startup;` and the re-export block `pub(crate) use detach::{SessionHeader, detach_log_path, parse_session_header}; pub(crate) use reclaim::{gc, superseded_reclaimable_hint}; pub(crate) use roll::{upgrade_mise_packages, upgrade_provision_steps}; pub(crate) use session::{attach, stop}; pub(in crate::sandbox) use cage::{seccomp_argv, status_code}; pub(in crate::sandbox) use reclaim::{session_housekeeping, shared_store_gc};` so `src/sandbox/mod.rs:141-145`, `src/sandbox/projects.rs:164,293,657,729`, `src/sandbox/task.rs:1154,1221`, `src/sandbox/taskpool.rs:512` and `src/sandbox/resolver.rs:221` are all untouched. Tests that stay: `mod tests` header 6174-6178; `only_a_trailing_shell_script_takes_an_argv0_filler` 6251-6299; `const REV` 6300; `a_launch_that_cannot_show_the_observe_feed_says_so` 6302-6365; `config_declared_observation_blocks_the_exec_replace_shortcut` 6366-6414; `the_guardless_launch_paths_ask_the_predicate_and_not_the_observe_flag` 6415-6474; the five `effective_lock_target` tests 7319-7454; `collect_roots_unions_base_then_packages_then_tools_then_fonts` 7509-7563.

**Risque.** Visibility: none for anything staying here — children see it all. Rustdoc: `[`app`]` at 3150 and `[`prepare*`]` at 3067/3107/3089/3478 stay in-module; `[`build`]` at 3478 (`build`'s own doc has moved away, so the reference on `prepare` prose) and `[`may_exec_replace`]`/`[`observation_flags`]` links in `launch_foreground`'s neighbourhood stay resolvable only if the parent keeps `use build::build;` — write `[`build::build`]` instead, which is unambiguous. `LaunchGuard::observed_events` (3363) is called from `launch_foreground_learning` at 987, so it must become `pub(super)` in `build.rs` together with `LaunchGuard`'s `notify_sink` field (read by `detach.rs` at 464). Tests: the source-scanning test at 6415-6474 does `include_str!("launch.rs")` and searches for `\nfn launch_foreground(` and `\nfn detached_child(`; after the split it must iterate `[("launch_foreground", include_str!("mod.rs")), ("detached_child", include_str!("detach.rs"))]` — its per-file cut on `"\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {"` still works unchanged. Its doc prose at 6423-6426 cross-references `the_wraps_nest_around_the_composed_startup_and_not_the_bare_command`, which now lives in `build.rs`; update the sentence (CLAUDE.md's doc-consistency rule, not a compile break). `docs_coverage.rs:914-915` must become `("sandbox/launch/mod.rs", "missing")` and `("sandbox/launch/mod.rs", "prepare_config")` — the stale-entry assertion at `src/docs_coverage.rs:1082-1092` fails loudly otherwise. The test helper `resolved` (7036-7045) is used from three future modules (7336/7369/7397/7409/7439 here, 7131/7230 in roll.rs, 7766 in equip.rs): promote it to `src/testutil.rs` beside the existing `pub(crate) fn resolved` at line 160, as e.g. `resolved_channels(global, project)`. No cycle risk.

#### `src/sandbox/launch/session.rs` (~740 lignes)

**Rôle.** The two verbs that address a cage someone else built: `sbx session attach` and `sbx session stop`, with the four confirmation renderers they and the foreground GUI hint share. It shares no state with the launch pipeline — the exact criterion `src/sandbox/mod.rs:114-117` already uses to keep `projects` out of `launch`.

**Ce qui bouge.** `render_attaching` 2557-2570; `launch_display_name` 2571-2589; `render_gui_stop_hint` 2590-2608; `attach` 2609-2705; `attach_argv` 2706-2732; `supervise_attach` 2733-2828; `run_attach_direct` 2829-2896; `render_no_active_sessions` 2897-2903; `stop` 2904-2977; `render_stop_outcome` 2978-3014; `stop_session` 3015-3041; `stop_exit_code` 3042-3055. Tests: `session_verb_confirmations_are_plain_text_when_uncolored` 6854-6906; `a_stop_that_left_something_running_outranks_an_id_that_matched_nothing` 6907-6917; `a_stop_that_signalled_nothing_keeps_the_session_record` 6918-6957; `launch_display_name_prefers_the_app_then_the_program_basename` 6958-6983; `session_verb_confirmations_color_their_outcome_and_identifier_spans` 6984-7035; helper `argv_strings` 8498-8504; `attach_argv_with_no_command_is_the_interactive_rc_shell` 8505-8519; `attach_argv_with_a_command_runs_it_positionally_through_bash` 8845-8869; `attach_argv_rejects_a_command_argument_with_an_interior_nul` 8870-8880.

**Risque.** Visibility: `attach` and `stop` keep `pub(crate)` and are re-exported from `mod.rs`, so `src/sandbox/mod.rs:142-143` is unchanged. `launch_display_name` and `render_gui_stop_hint` must become `pub(super)` — `launch_pty_supervised` (2510, 2544) stays in the parent and calls both. `cstring` (5880-5884) must become `pub(super)` in `cage.rs`: `attach_argv` calls it at 2716-2735. Rustdoc: `[`supervise`]` at 2735 must become `[`super::cage::supervise`]`; `[`mod@super::attach`]` at 2619 must become `[`mod@crate::sandbox::attach`]` — left as `super::attach` inside this child it would resolve to the *function* `launch::session::attach` and fail the `mod@` disambiguator, so `mise run rustdoc` catches it. 16 `super::X` references in 2557-3055 (`super::attach::*`, `super::seccomp::*`) rebase to `crate::sandbox::X`. Naming: call the file `session.rs`, not `attach.rs` — `src/sandbox/attach.rs` already exists and holds the setns mechanism this verb drives. The two `session_verb_confirmations_*` tests assert across all four renderers at once, which is why `render_gui_stop_hint` and `launch_display_name` move here rather than staying beside `launch_pty_supervised`: keeping them together lets both tests move intact. No cycle risk.

#### `src/sandbox/launch/reclaim.rs` (~767 lignes)

**Rôle.** The `sbx gc` verb: what a reclaim run decides and reports. `src/sandbox/gc.rs` already holds the store-level mechanics (`collect`, `prune_*_roots`, `sweep_runtime_dirs`, `human_bytes`); this is the policy layer above it, which is why it is `reclaim.rs` and not a second `gc.rs`.

**Ce qui bouge.** `gc` 1794-1848; `session_housekeeping` 1849-1882; `runtime_housekeeping` 1883-1917; `shared_store_gc` 1918-2012; `report_optimise` 2013-2034; `inline_flake_gcroot_names` 2035-2055; `gc_live_session_refusal` 2056-2082; `sweep_current` 2083-2286; `superseded_reclaimable_hint` 2287-2343; `equip_for_gc` 2356-2473. `prebuilt_ctx` 2344-2355 deliberately does NOT move — it is called from both `equip_for_gc` (2386) and `build` (3545), so it stays in `mod.rs` where both children see it for free. Tests: `gc_refuses_when_the_session_registry_cannot_be_read` 6475-6519; `a_lapse_in_trust_does_not_look_like_a_removed_inline_flake` 6520-6573.

**Risque.** Visibility: `gc` and `superseded_reclaimable_hint` keep `pub(crate)` and are re-exported. `session_housekeeping` (1853) and `shared_store_gc` (1936) are `pub(super)` today and are called from `src/sandbox/projects.rs:164,293,657,729` — inside a child of `launch` that keyword would mean 'visible in `launch`' and break all four call sites. They must become `pub(in crate::sandbox)` and be re-exported by `mod.rs` with `pub(in crate::sandbox) use reclaim::{session_housekeeping, shared_store_gc};`. Rustdoc: the module is self-contained on links — `[`sweep_current`]` (1796, 2293) and `[`shared_store_gc`]` (1800) resolve in-module, and `[`packages::project_gcroot_names`]`/`[`packages::flake_inline_packages`]` at 2044-2046 use explicit link-reference definitions that need rebasing to `crate::sandbox::packages::…`. `src/sandbox/projects.rs:10` carries the prose `Kept apart from [`super::launch`]` — the link still resolves (the module still exists) but the sentence should name the new shape. 39 `super::X` references across 1794-2343 and 2356-2473 (`super::gc::*` ×19, `super::projectstore::*`, `super::prebuilt::*`, `super::fonts`/`gpu`/`audio`/`guidata`/`portal`, `super::packages::*`) rebase to `crate::sandbox::X` — the largest sweep after `build.rs`. `equip_for_gc` calls `mise_tools` (2423) and `seed_project_store` (2469), both of which stay in `mod.rs`, so no annotation is needed for them. No cycle risk.

#### `src/sandbox/launch/roll.rs` (~1060 lignes)

**Rôle.** The two `sbx upgrade` sub-rolls that need a cage: `upgrade_mise_packages` and `upgrade_provision_steps`, their group types, the task-pool roll, and the aligned report the three share. Batch drivers over `build` + `run_captured`, not part of a launch.

**Ce qui bouge.** `GroupHome` (enum + impl) 1023-1061; `MiseGroup` 1062-1069; `mise_package_groups` 1070-1119; `withheld_mise_packages` 1120-1151; `upgrade_mise_packages` 1226-1485; `ProvisionGroup` 1486-1493; `provision_groups` 1494-1524; `upgrade_provision_steps` 1525-1659; `step_bundles` 1660-1671; `provision_roll_recap` 1672-1688; `roll_task_pool` 1703-1764; and the report formatters that today sit 4,000 lines away — `mise_transitions` 5651-5663, `mise_up_to_date` 5664-5669, `roll_line` 5670-5680, `mise_roll_recap` 5681-5717. Tests: `mise_transitions_extracts_the_version_rolls_from_captured_output` 6772-6801; `mise_up_to_date_detects_the_no_op_roll` 6802-6810; `mise_roll_recap_names_what_rolled_and_tallies_the_rest` 6811-6835; helpers `mise_pkg` 7046-7058, `nix_pkg` 7059-7067, `app_overlay` 7068-7120; `provision_groups_takes_only_the_apps_whose_bundles_install` 7121-7179; `an_install_roll_runs_the_steps_alone_and_never_the_app` 7180-7214; `the_install_roll_recap_names_what_ran_and_tallies_the_rest` 7215-7226; `mise_package_groups_covers_the_baseline_and_each_app_generically` 7227-7318.

**Risque.** Visibility: `upgrade_mise_packages` (1244) and `upgrade_provision_steps` (1539) keep `pub(crate)` and are re-exported, so `src/cli/upgrade.rs:280,317,701,707` is untouched. Needs from siblings: `build::build`, `cage::run_captured`, `equip::{MISE_ROLL_FLAG, mise_upgrade_cmd}`, `startup::provision_only_cmd` — all `pub(super)`. `prepare_in` (3093) and `Prepared.quiet_equip`/`Prepared.cfg` stay in the parent and need nothing. Rustdoc: `[`mise_package_groups`]` at 1232 and 1496 stay in-module; `[`GroupHome::runtime`]`/`[`GroupHome::label`]` (1025, 1052) stay in-module; `[`mise_upgrade_cmd`]` referenced from `wrap_mise_equip`'s doc at 4068 lives in `equip.rs` — that link is *inside* the `build` body comment (a `//` comment, not a doc comment), so it does not affect rustdoc, but `[`mise_up_to_date`]` at 5654 stays in-module. Only 7 `super::X` references across the moved ranges (`super::packages::mise_packages` 1073/1105, `super::binds::project_runtime_id`, `super::task::TaskEngine`, `super::taskpool::pool_dir` in `roll_task_pool`). Test helper `resolved` used at 7131 and 7230 comes from the promoted `crate::testutil` helper. No cycle risk.

#### `src/sandbox/launch/cage.rs` (~415 lignes)

**Rôle.** Turning a finished `SandboxSpec` into a running process, and recording that it ran: the seccomp-prefixed argv, the four ways to start it (exec-replace, fork-and-wait, capture, pty-supervise) and the session registration. The one part of `launch` three sibling modules outside it already call.

**Ce qui bouge.** `register` 5551-5577; `session_runtime` 5578-5587; `run_supervised` 5588-5596; `run_status` 5597-5624; `run_captured` 5625-5650; `seccomp_argv` 5718-5733; `status_code` 5734-5742; `exec` 5743-5767; `supervise` 5768-5879; `cstring` 5880-5884. Tests: `no_variable_reaches_the_world_readable_argument_list` 6574-6654; `a_spec_with_no_environment_gains_no_descriptor` 6655-6673; `session_runtime_maps_each_launch_runtime_to_its_owned_form` 6836-6853; `exec_refuses_a_private_tty_spec` 8329-8353.

**Risque.** Visibility is the main cost and it is already documented by the current keywords. `seccomp_argv` (5723) and `status_code` (5736) are `pub(super)` today, meaning 'visible in `sandbox`'; inside a child of `launch` that keyword shrinks to 'visible in `launch`' and breaks `src/sandbox/task.rs:1154,1221`, `src/sandbox/taskpool.rs:512`, `src/sandbox/resolver.rs:221`. Declare them `pub(in crate::sandbox)` and add `pub(in crate::sandbox) use cage::{seccomp_argv, status_code};` to `mod.rs`; a `use` re-export is itself a link target, so the doc link `[`super::launch::seccomp_argv`]` at `src/sandbox/spec.rs:221` keeps resolving with no edit — verify that with `mise run rustdoc` rather than assuming it. `register`, `exec`, `supervise`, `run_supervised`, `run_status`, `run_captured`, `cstring` → `pub(super)` (callers: `mod.rs` 315/351/966/971/2523/2548, `detach.rs` 452/494/512, `roll.rs` 1371/1615, `session.rs` 2716). Rustdoc: `[`run_status`]` at 5625 and `[`supervise`]` in-module; `[`detach_log_path`]` and `[`detached_child`]` in `register`'s doc (5560-5561) → `[`super::detach::detach_log_path`]` / `[`super::detach::detached_child`]`; `[`RecordGuard`]` at 5555 resolves through the parent's import and survives `use super::*`. 20 `super::X` references (`super::seccomp`, `super::netns::holder_wrap`, `super::cgroup::wrap`, `super::argv::compose`) rebase. `exec_refuses_a_private_tty_spec` uses `super::super::cgroup::Limits` at 8346 — inside this child's test module `super::super` becomes `launch`, so that one line must become `crate::sandbox::cgroup::Limits`. No cycle risk.

Its 325 lines of tests are the second-largest test cluster in the file and are entirely self-contained (they build `BundleProvision`/`ServiceSpec` values and read the emitted script), so they move with no edits beyond the module header. **Rôle.** The `--detach` daemon and the format of the log it leaves behind: fork, `setsid`, the readiness pipe, the session-log header, and the trust-drop notes. Self-contained process plumbing whose writer and reader are deliberately kept side by side (594-604 states exactly that).

**Ce qui bouge.** `DETACH_READY` 359-361; `launch_detached` 363-421; `detached_child` 423-518; `detach_parent` 520-559; `detach_log_path` 561-567; `SESSION_LOG_HEADER_OPEN`/`_CLOSE` 569-576; `SESSION_LOG_TRUST_DROP_OPEN` 578-584; `SessionHeader` 586-592; `parse_session_header` 594-615; `open_detach_log` 617-652; `note_trust_drops` 654-681; `trust_drop_notes` 683-716; `fail_detached` 718-723; `signal_detach_ready` 725-733; `redirect_stdin_to_null` 735-747; `redirect_to_log` 749-757. **Risque.** Visibility: `detach_log_path` (565), `SessionHeader` (588) and `parse_session_header` (605) keep `pub(crate)` and are re-exported by `mod.rs`, so `src/cli/session.rs:396,412,438,439,447,519` is untouched. `launch_detached` → `pub(super)` (called from `launch` at 274). Needs `pub(super)` from siblings: `build::build` (446), `cage::{register, exec, run_status}` (452, 494, 512); `observation_flags` and `may_exec_replace` (486, 491) stay private in the parent and are visible for free. Rustdoc: `[`build`]` at 367/368 and 656 → `[`super::build::build`]`; `[`SESSION_LOG_HEADER_CLOSE`]` (581), `[`parse_session_header`]` (582), `[`open_detach_log`]` (586), `[`redirect_to_log`]` (658), `[`trust_drop_notes`]` (664) all stay in-module; `[`super::notify_sink`]`, `[`Needles`]`, `[`super::locks::read_locked`]` in `trust_drop_notes`'s doc (683-700) rebase to `crate::sandbox::…`. Only 9 `super::X` code references (`super::notify_sink::NotifyWiring`, `super::locks::read_locked`, `super::redact::*`, `super::observe_feed::Observation`). Test coupling: `detached_child` is one of the two functions the source-scanning guard at 6415-6474 reads, so that guard must be taught to `include_str!("detach.rs")` for it — see the `mod.rs` risk note. No cycle risk.

### Autres observations

**A duplicated driver that only becomes visible once the two rolls sit together.** `upgrade_mise_packages` (1226-1485) and `upgrade_provision_steps` (1525-1659) are the same 130-line control flow twice: compute groups → compute the dot-leader `width` from `home.name()` (1310-1315 vs 1563-1568) → per group, skip `NetworkPolicy::Isolated` with an identical `roll_line(name, width, "network \"none\" — skipped")` (1322-1334 vs 1576-1588) → `cfg.warnings.clear()` + `prep.cfg = cfg` → `build` → on `Err` print "failed to launch" and `failed += 1` (1354-1366 vs 1600-1612) → `run_captured` → `drop(guard)` → report → tally → recap coloured by `failed > 0 / ran.is_empty()` (1470-1484 vs 1648-1658). Only the group type, the cage command, and the success line differ. A `trait RollGroup { fn name(&self); fn label(&self); fn runtime(&self); fn cfg(&self); fn command(&self, prep) -> Vec<OsString>; fn report(&self, code, out) -> String; }` with one `fn run_groups(...)` driver would collapse it. I would do this **after** the move, not as part of it: it is a behaviour-preserving refactor of ~260 lines that is far easier to review when both copies are in one 1,060-line file than when they are 300 lines apart in an 8,880-line one.

**Three duplicated cage-command preambles, safely collapsible inside `cage.rs`.** `run_status` (5601-5616), `run_captured` (5631-5641), `exec` (5751-5766) and `supervise` (5779-5786) each repeat the identical trio `seccomp_argv(spec)?` → `netns::holder_wrap(bwrap, argv, spec.netns_dummy.as_ref())` → `cgroup::wrap(&holder_prog, holder_argv, limits, &spec.cage_slug)`. A single `fn cage_command(bwrap, spec, limits) -> io::Result<(PathBuf, Vec<OsString>, Vec<File>)>` states it once; the returned `Vec<File>` is load-bearing (`seccomp_argv`'s doc at 5718-5722 says the memfds must outlive the exec), so the signature has to hand them back rather than drop them. This is a genuine four-way collapse, it is entirely local to the proposed `cage.rs`, and the existing tests at 6574-6673 and 8329-8353 already cover the argv it produces.

**Two near-identical prebuilt walks that should NOT be collapsed.** `build` (3545-3606) and `equip_for_gc` (2400-2445) both iterate `prebuilt::DIRECT_ORDER` then `RESOLVE_ORDER` over `prep.cfg.packages`, pushing roots. `src/sandbox/prebuilt.rs:643` already notes the pairing. But the resolve halves differ on the security-relevant point: `build` calls `provision_resolve` (which *runs* the profile's resolve command in a hermetic cage and may touch the network), `equip_for_gc` calls `provision_resolve_pinned` and its comment at 2432-2434 says gc "must never run the resolve command or touch the network". Factoring them into one function parameterised by a `run_command: bool` would put that guarantee behind a boolean argument. Leave them apart; the duplication is the cheaper of the two costs.

**Vestigial visibility, tightened by the move rather than worked around.** `LaunchGuard` (3308) and all thirteen of its fields are `pub(crate)`, but grep across `src/` finds no user outside `launch.rs`; moving it into `build.rs` as `pub(super)` is a real narrowing. Conversely `AppOutcome` (763) must stay `pub(crate)` — `src/cli/app.rs:72,74` reads `.learned` and `.code` without naming the type.

**Section ordering is already broken today, which is part of the argument.** Four functions that exist only to serve `upgrade_mise_packages` (`mise_transitions` 5651, `mise_up_to_date` 5664, `roll_line` 5670, `mise_roll_recap` 5681) sit 4,000 lines below it, wedged between `run_captured` and `seccomp_argv`; `wrap_mise_equip` (5419) sits 4,200 lines from the `MISE_EQUIP_VERB` (1160) whose pairing its doc explains; `equip_announcement` (1163) sits with the roll but is only ever called by `build` (4052). The proposal puts each beside the thing it belongs to, which is why several modules take two disjoint line ranges rather than one contiguous block.

**Suggested order of execution**, so each step is independently reviewable and `mise run ci` is green between them: (1) promote the test helper `resolved` to `src/testutil.rs`; (2) `git mv src/sandbox/launch.rs src/sandbox/launch/mod.rs` with no other change (proves the path-only edits — `docs_coverage.rs:914-915` and the two `include_str!` targets at 6429 and 7641); (3) `startup.rs` (zero `super::` rewrites, largest self-contained test block); (4) `session.rs`; (5) `detach.rs`; (6) `equip.rs`; (7) `roll.rs`; (8) `reclaim.rs`; (9) `cage.rs`; (10) `build.rs` last, since it is the residue. Run `mise run rustdoc` after every step — it is the only guard that catches a doc link that stopped resolving, and it fails the build rather than dropping the link silently.
