## `proc-enforce` — src/sandbox/proc_enforce.rs (5750 lines)

**Verdict : **Découpage fortement recommandé****

### Cartographie actuelle

`/home/user/ops-cli/src/sandbox/proc_enforce.rs` (5750 lines; 1–2940 code, 2941–5750 tests) is the host-side half of the seccomp user-notification gate. An in-cage shim (`proc-shim/`) installs a `NEW_LISTENER` filter on itself, passes the listener fd out over a bind-mounted UDS, and `execvp`s the payload; a supervisor thread in the launching sbx process then decides every notified syscall.

Internal sections, with verified line ranges:

- 1–115 module header (`//!`), entirely about **exec** enforcement: the shim rationale, the ancestor invariant, the open-then-validate ordering, bypass resistance, the `#!` gap.
- 117–130 imports. 132–156 `ASK_PENDING_CAP` / `ASK_TIMEOUT` / `SWEEP_EVERY` / `SHIM_CAGE_PATH` / `NOTIF_SOCK_CAGE_PATH`.
- 158–190 seccomp ioctl ABI: `IOC_WRITE`, `IOC_READ`, `SECCOMP_IOC_MAGIC`, `seccomp_ioc`, `notif_recv_code`, `notif_send_code`, `notif_id_valid_code`.
- 192 a section banner (`// ── the in-cage shim ──`) with nothing under it.
- 194–266 `ProcOverlay` / `OverlayInner` — the live `--session` rule overlay, `pub(crate)`, consumed by `proc_control.rs`.
- 268–509 lifecycle: `Wiring`, `ProcEnforce` (+`refusals`), `Refusal`, `impl Drop`, `start`, `start_for_task`, `start_inner`.
- 511–528 `OPEN_LENS_FLAG` + `wrap_command`.
- 530–615 `Undecidable` + `unmatched_word` — the "decisions we could not base on a name" counters and their teardown report.
- 619–674 `exec_verdict`; 1032–1101 `caller_chain`, `refusal_errno` — the exec decision.
- 676–741 `OpenName`, `open_name`, `UNNAMEABLE_OPEN_SAID` — naming a notified open.
- 743–1030 the supervisor state machine: `Deciding`, `supervise`, `close_supervision`, `accept_handoff`, `recv_loop`, `handle_notif`. Inside `handle_notif`, 879–961 is the **open** branch and 962–1030 the **exec** branch.
- 1103–1216 answering the kernel: `is_notif_listener`, `notif_id_valid`, `respond_continue`, `respond_errno`, `ADDFD_UNAVAILABLE`, `respond_with_fd`.
- 1218–1662 serving an open from the supervisor's descriptor: `serve_open`, `Creation`, `serve_creation`, `errno_describes_the_file`, `reopen_probe`, `PARKED_OPEN_CAP`/`PARKED_OPENS`/`FIFO_RETRY`/`park_open`.
- 1664–1819 reading the parked target: `notif_of`, `open_target_mem`, `read_u64`, `OPEN_HOW_VER0`, `open_flags`, `open_mode`, `open_resolve`. 1821–1831 `send_resp`.
- 1833–1979 the ask registry: `PendingExec`, `Parked`, `park`/`answer`/`answer_all`/`list`/`sweep`, `answer_parked`.
- 1981–2062 `read_path_bytes`, `read_exec_path`, `open_args`, `exec_args`.
- 2064–2827 the **content lens**: `FileId`, `ScanCache`, `CageMounts`, `Statx`/`mount_id`, `OPENAT2_UNAVAILABLE`/`probe_in_cage_root`, `vouched_probe`, `policy_scan_ceiling`, `OpenLens`, `probe_and_vouch`, `splice_first_link`, `proc_self_behind_a_link`, `open_is_refused`, `OpenOutcome`, `OpenReport`, `caller_ids_in_cage`, `caller_umask`, `umask_of`, `innermost_ids`, `caller_proc_path`, `open_target_path`.
- 2829–2939 fd plumbing: `poll_readable`, `CmsgBuf`, `recv_fd`, `recv_fd_raw`.
- 2941–3133 `mod open_path_tests` (9 tests); 3134–5750 `mod tests` (~65 tests plus the harnesses `run_with_open_lens` 3160, `materialized_shim` 4874, `spawn_shim` 4891, `run_under_supervisor*` 4946–5051, `DecidingParts` 5469–5512, `UNREADABLE` 5518).

External surface consumed elsewhere: `Refusal` (task.rs, task_control.rs, cli/task.rs), `ProcOverlay`/`PendingExec` (proc_control.rs), `ProcEnforce`/`start`/`start_for_task`/`wrap_command` (launch.rs, task.rs), `SHIM_CAGE_PATH` (task.rs).

### Justification

The decisive fact is not size, it is subject. This file holds **two different lenses** that share nothing but one notification descriptor.

Counting non-test lines: the **open/content lens** (`[fs] scan`, whose pure half is `src/open_policy.rs`) is ~1490 lines — `OpenName`/`open_name` 676–741, the open branch of `handle_notif` 879–961, `serve_open`..`park_open` 1218–1662, `open_flags`/`open_mode`/`open_resolve` 1711–1819, `open_args` 2020–2039, and the whole 2064–2827 block. The **exec gate** (`[proc]`, pure half `src/proc_policy.rs`) is ~364 lines: `exec_verdict` 619–674, the exec branch 962–1030, `caller_chain`/`refusal_errno` 1032–1101, `PendingExec` 1833–1979, `exec_args` 2041–2062. The 115-line module header describes only the second one; the open lens appears in it exactly twice, in passing (lines 85, 107), and has no module-level prose anywhere.

The seam between them is unusually exact, which is what makes this a mechanical split rather than a hopeful one. `Deciding` (745–756) has seven fields; the entire open branch of `handle_notif` (879–961) touches exactly two of them — `cx.open` (lines 900, 912, 933) and, via `open_name`, `cx.undecidable`. It never touches `ring`, `notifier`, `pending`, `policy` or `overlay`. So the open half can be lifted behind a single call `handle_open(notif_fd, req, dirfd, path_addr, lens: Option<&OpenLens>, undecidable: &Undecidable)` with **no back-reference to `Deciding` at all** — no cycle, not even a conceptual one.

Underneath both lenses there is a genuinely policy-free mechanism layer that is currently interleaved with them: the ioctl ABI and the four answer forms (158–190, 1103–1216, 1821–1831), the fd handoff (2829–2939), and the parked-target reads plus per-syscall register decoding (1664–1819, 1981–2062). Nothing in that layer calls into either lens — it is a clean leaf, and it already carries its own tightly-scoped unit tests (`ioctl_codes_match_the_kernel_abi`, the four `each_*_form_*` tests, `the_shortest_open_how_the_kernel_accepts_is_the_whole_first_version`, `the_handoff_control_buffer_is_aligned_for_the_header_it_is_read_as`).

Answering the framing directly: policy-decision / mechanism / reporting **are** separable here, but not symmetrically. The mechanism separates cleanly. The *open* policy decision separates cleanly. The *exec* policy decision does not earn a module — it is 126 lines and every one of its tests needs the `DecidingParts` harness (5469–5512) that lives with the shim-driven tests, so it stays in `mod.rs` beside `handle_notif`. Reporting separates only halfway: the `Undecidable` counters and the teardown report are a real unit, but the `diag::warn` calls are deliberately emitted at the site that knows (549–554: "Counted at the read and not by its caller, deliberately"), and a reporting module that hoovered those up would dissolve a documented decision.

What must **not** be split: the supervisor state machine `supervise`/`accept_handoff`/`recv_loop`/`handle_notif`/`close_supervision` (743–1030, ~288 lines) is one machine with one invariant — the single listener never blocks — and the `Spec`-side seam is elsewhere entirely. It stays whole in `mod.rs` together with the lifecycle it is documented by.

The crate already has this exact shape twice: `src/sandbox/proxy/` (16 flat children, `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` in `proxy/tests.rs`) and `src/sandbox/control/` (children plus `pub(crate) use capture::*;`). Re-exporting the seven `pub(crate)` items from `mod.rs` means **zero call-site changes outside the module** — `crate::sandbox::proc_enforce::Refusal` and friends keep their paths, and `src/proc_policy.rs:6` / `src/open_policy.rs:6`, which link `[`crate::sandbox::proc_enforce`]`, keep resolving.

### Découpage proposé

#### `src/sandbox/proc_enforce/open_lens.rs` (~970 lignes)

**Rôle.** Decide one notified open by the bytes behind it: name the path, reach the inode the cage's own walk would reach, vouch for it against the cage's mounts, scan it against the compiled OpenPolicy, and say what to report. The impure counterpart of the pure src/open_policy.rs, which today has no module of its own.

**Ce qui bouge.** 676–741 `OpenName`, `open_name`, `UNNAMEABLE_OPEN_SAID`. 879–961, the open branch of `handle_notif`, extracted verbatim as `pub(super) fn handle_open(notif_fd: libc::c_int, req: &libc::seccomp_notif, dirfd: libc::c_int, path_addr: u64, lens: Option<&OpenLens>, undecidable: &Undecidable)` — `cx.open` becomes `lens`, `cx` in the `open_name` call becomes `(lens.is_some(), undecidable)`. 2064–2460 `FileId`, `SCAN_CACHE_MAX`/`ScanCache`, `CAGE_MOUNTS_MAX`/`CageMounts`, `STATX_MNT_ID`/`Statx`/`mount_id`, `OPENAT2_UNAVAILABLE`/`probe_in_cage_root`, `vouched_probe`, `policy_scan_ceiling`, `OpenLens`+`impl`, `probe_and_vouch`. 2509–2716 `open_is_refused`, `OpenOutcome`+`impl`, `OpenReport`. 1529–1555 `errno_describes_the_file` (both askers — `OpenOutcome::failed` here and `serve_open` in open_serve.rs — keep the one definition its doc insists on; open_serve imports it, one direction only). Tests that move: `the_statx_layout_matches_the_kernels` 4039–4056, `a_mount_this_process_cannot_see_is_never_taken_for_one_it_can` 4057–4077, `a_process_that_cannot_be_read_answers_no_rather_than_yes` 4078–4095, `the_second_resolution_reaches_the_same_inode_and_refuses_a_marked_path` 4096–4132, `a_reported_open_path_carries_no_byte_that_could_forge_a_line_the_operator_reads` 4263–4317, `an_errno_about_this_process_is_never_reported_as_the_files` 4615–4642, `a_refusal_never_carries_an_errno_about_the_supervisor` 4643–4649.

**Risque.** Visibility: `OpenLens` drops from `pub(crate)` (it has no user outside this module) to `pub(super)`; `OpenLens::new`, `open_is_refused`, `policy_scan_ceiling`, `handle_open`, `OpenName`+3 variants, `OpenOutcome`+its 4 fields+`ALLOWED`, `OpenReport`+its 3 fields, `vouched_probe` (called by `serve_creation` at 1473), `errno_describes_the_file`, `caller_umask` all become `pub(super)`. `OpenLens`'s own fields stay private — every reader of `policy`/`cache`/`root`/`mounts` lands in this file. Rustdoc: `[`read_exec_path`]` at 685 → `super::target::read_exec_path`; `[`PendingExec::park`]` at 2704 → `super::pending::PendingExec::park`; `[`vouched_probe`]` at 1228 moves with `serve_open`, see open_serve. Code: `super::sanitize` at 2609 and 2628 must become `crate::sandbox::sanitize` — `super` is now `proc_enforce`, not `sandbox`, and this is the silent-breakage class (it would fail to compile, so it is loud, but it must be done at every one of the three sites). Cycles: none — after the `handle_open` signature change this module depends only on `target`, `notify`, `cagepath`, `report`, `crate::open_policy`; nothing here reaches back into `mod.rs`. Cost note: at ~970 lines this is still the largest child. Splitting it further (probe/cache machinery vs the scan decision) would force `OpenLens`'s four fields to `pub(super)`, exposing the struct's internals across files for no gain; do not.

#### `src/sandbox/proc_enforce/open_serve.rs` (~615 lignes)

**Rôle.** Answer an open the lens permitted — from the supervisor's own descriptor rather than by letting the syscall re-resolve the path. This is the half that makes an allow sound, and it is mechanism, not policy: it decides nothing about content, only how a verdict already formed is delivered to the cage.

**Ce qui bouge.** 1218–1380 `serve_open`; 1382–1394 `Creation`; 1396–1527 `serve_creation`; 1557–1570 `reopen_probe`; 1572–1662 `PARKED_OPEN_CAP`, `PARKED_OPENS`, `FIFO_RETRY`, `park_open`. Tests that move: `a_restricted_openat2_is_left_to_the_kernel_to_walk` 3910–3960 and `a_short_openat2_is_left_to_the_kernel_to_refuse` 3961–3995 (both call `serve_open(-1, …)` directly), `a_creation_that_could_not_be_handed_over_is_taken_away_again` 4318–4381 (calls `serve_creation`).

**Risque.** Visibility: `serve_open`, `serve_creation` and `Creation` + its four variants become `pub(super)` (called from `open_lens::handle_open`); `park_open` becomes `pub(super)` only because a doc link at 1853 names it — see below; `reopen_probe`, `PARKED_OPEN_CAP`, `PARKED_OPENS`, `FIFO_RETRY` stay private. Imports needed: `super::notify::{respond_errno, respond_continue, respond_with_fd, notif_of, notif_id_valid}`, `super::target::{open_flags, open_mode, open_resolve, OPEN_HOW_VER0}`, `super::cagepath::{open_target_path, caller_umask}`, `super::open_lens::{OpenLens, vouched_probe, errno_describes_the_file}`. Rustdoc: `[`vouched_probe`]` at 1228 → `super::open_lens::vouched_probe`. `OPEN_HOW_VER0` must be `pub(super)` for `a_short_openat2_is_left_to_the_kernel_to_refuse` at 3984. Cycles: depends on open_lens one way only — `serve_creation` takes `&OpenLens`, nothing in open_lens calls back into here (`handle_open` does, but `handle_open` lives in open_lens; if that reads badly, put `handle_open` here instead and the direction inverts cleanly — either is acyclic, do not do both).

#### `src/sandbox/proc_enforce/cagepath.rs` (~370 lignes)

**Rôle.** Answer one question in one place: what host path names the object a cage's syscall named. Resolution through /proc/<pid>/root, /cwd, /fd/<n>; the self / thread-self rewrite; the /dev/stdout-class link chase; and the two /proc/<pid>/status parsers those need. Touches no policy and no OpenLens.

**Ce qui bouge.** 2462–2507 `splice_first_link`, `proc_self_behind_a_link`; 2718–2827 `caller_ids_in_cage`, `caller_umask`, `umask_of`, `innermost_ids`, `caller_proc_path`, `open_target_path`. Tests that move: from `mod open_path_tests`, `an_absolute_path_is_read_through_the_targets_own_root` 3092–3101, `a_relative_path_follows_the_descriptor_it_was_opened_against` 3102–3117, `an_absolute_path_never_takes_over_the_prefix` 3118–3133; from `mod tests`, `self_and_thread_self_are_rewritten_to_the_caller_and_nothing_else_is` 3544–3584, `a_link_is_spliced_where_it_sits_and_not_only_at_the_end` 3585–3619, `the_dev_links_arrive_at_the_callers_own_entry` 3620–3644, `the_umask_line_is_read_as_the_octal_it_is_written_in` 3645–3659, `the_innermost_number_is_the_one_the_cage_uses` 3704–3731.

**Risque.** Lowest-risk module in the set: eight free functions, no shared state, no statics, eight unit tests that already run without a cage. Visibility: `open_target_path` and `caller_umask` become `pub(super)` (used by open_serve.rs at 1289, 1454, 1483); `caller_proc_path`, `proc_self_behind_a_link` become `pub(super)` (used by `open_is_refused` at 2523 and 2536); `splice_first_link`, `caller_ids_in_cage`, `umask_of`, `innermost_ids` stay private. Rustdoc: `[`read_exec_path`]` at 2809 → `super::target::read_exec_path`; `[`innermost_ids`]` at 2734 and `[`PathBuf::push`]` at 2805 resolve unchanged. Cycles: none — this is a leaf under open_lens and open_serve.

#### `src/sandbox/proc_enforce/target.rs` (~620 lignes)

**Rôle.** Read a parked target: its memory, under the open-then-validate ordering the module header proves at lines 37–49, and its syscall arguments, decoded per syscall number. Pure ABI knowledge — where each of the five notified calls keeps its dirfd, path pointer, flags, mode and resolve word — with no opinion about what any of it means.

**Ce qui bouge.** 1674–1698 `open_target_mem`; 1700–1709 `read_u64`; 1711–1722 `OPEN_HOW_VER0`; 1724–1756 `open_flags`; 1758–1784 `open_mode`; 1786–1819 `open_resolve`; 1981–1999 `read_path_bytes`; 2001–2018 `read_exec_path`; 2020–2039 `open_args`; 2041–2062 `exec_args`. Tests that move: `each_open_form_is_read_from_its_own_registers` 2946–2968, `a_syscall_that_is_not_an_open_is_left_to_the_exec_path` 2969–2979, `each_exec_form_is_read_from_its_own_registers` 3057–3076, `a_syscall_that_is_neither_an_open_nor_an_exec_is_decided_by_neither` 3077–3091, `each_open_form_keeps_its_mode_where_its_own_abi_puts_it` 3732–3813, `each_open_form_states_the_path_walk_it_asked_for` 3838–3909, `each_open_form_keeps_its_flags_where_its_own_abi_puts_them` 4163–4202, `openat2_reads_its_flags_from_the_struct_it_points_at` 4203–4247, `the_shortest_open_how_the_kernel_accepts_is_the_whole_first_version` 4248–4262, `a_target_named_in_bytes_no_name_can_carry_is_not_read_as_a_name_with_them_replaced` 4382–4414, `a_parked_target_this_supervisor_cannot_read_reads_as_no_path_at_all` 5737–5749.

**Risque.** Visibility: `read_path_bytes`, `read_exec_path`, `open_args`, `exec_args`, `open_flags`, `open_mode`, `open_resolve`, `OPEN_HOW_VER0` become `pub(super)`; `read_u64` stays private; `open_target_mem` should become `pub(super)` even though it has no cross-file caller, because three doc links name it (header line 38, `handle_notif` line 875, `notif_of` line 1668) and a link is easier to keep sound against a nameable path. Rustdoc: `[`ProcPolicy`]` at 2015 and `[`ProcPolicy::unmatched`]` at 2048 must become `crate::proc_policy::ProcPolicy…` — this file otherwise has no reason to import `ProcPolicy`, so there is no `use` to carry them; `[`caller_chain`]` at 2003 → `super::caller_chain`. Depends on `super::notify::notif_id_valid` (from `open_target_mem`). Cycles: none. Note the doc/test symmetry this creates: eleven tests whose whole subject is 'which register holds what' land in the file that holds the answer, which is what makes a future sixth syscall number a one-file change.

#### `src/sandbox/proc_enforce/notify.rs` (~420 lignes)

**Rôle.** The kernel interface, and nothing else: the ioctl request codes derived from the struct sizes, the four ways to answer a notification (continue, errno, ADDFD, raw send), the id-validity probe, and the SCM_RIGHTS handoff that receives the listener fd. No policy, no /proc, no path.

**Ce qui bouge.** 158–190 the ioctl banner, `IOC_WRITE`, `IOC_READ`, `SECCOMP_IOC_MAGIC`, `seccomp_ioc`, `notif_recv_code`, `notif_send_code`, `notif_id_valid_code`; 1103–1216 `is_notif_listener`, `notif_id_valid`, `respond_continue`, `respond_errno`, `ADDFD_UNAVAILABLE`, `respond_with_fd`; 1664–1672 `notif_of`; 1821–1831 `send_resp`; 2829–2939 `poll_readable`, `CmsgBuf`+`impl`, `recv_fd`, `recv_fd_raw`. Tests that move: `ioctl_codes_match_the_kernel_abi` 3141–3159, `the_handoff_control_buffer_is_aligned_for_the_header_it_is_read_as` 3814–3837, `a_descriptor_received_over_the_handoff_socket_is_close_on_exec` 4754–4782 together with its `send_fd` helper 4783–4824.

**Risque.** Visibility: `notif_recv_code` (used by `recv_loop` at 902), `notif_id_valid`, `respond_continue`, `respond_errno`, `respond_with_fd`, `notif_of`, `poll_readable`, `recv_fd` become `pub(super)`; `seccomp_ioc`, `notif_send_code`, `notif_id_valid_code`, `is_notif_listener`, `recv_fd_raw`, `CmsgBuf`, `ADDFD_UNAVAILABLE` stay private (the `recv_fd_raw` test is in this file's own `mod tests`, so its deliberate split from `recv_fd` — argued at 2887–2900 — survives intact). `send_resp` is `pub(crate)` today with zero callers outside the module (only 1141 and 1149); make it private on the way over. docs_coverage: four of the eight `UNDOCUMENTED_MODULE_ITEMS` entries land here — `seccomp_ioc`, `notif_recv_code`, `notif_send_code`, `notif_id_valid_code` — and must be re-keyed from `"sandbox/proc_enforce.rs"` to `"sandbox/proc_enforce/notify.rs"` (docs_coverage.rs:922–925), or the staleness assertion at docs_coverage.rs:1082–1090 fails. Rustdoc: `[`open_target_mem`]` at 1668 → `super::target::open_target_mem`. Cycles: none — this is the bottom of the stack and imports nothing from its siblings.

#### `src/sandbox/proc_enforce/pending.rs` (~215 lignes)

**Rôle.** The ask-parked execve registry: shared mutable state reached from three threads (the receive loop that parks and sweeps, the control server that answers, teardown that drains), with its own flood cap, timeout and dup-per-entry discipline. It is a pub(crate) type consumed by proc_control.rs, and the one place in this module where lock ordering is load-bearing.

**Ce qui bouge.** 132–150 `ASK_PENDING_CAP`, `ASK_TIMEOUT`, `SWEEP_EVERY`; 1833–1979 the `// ── ask-mode parking ──` banner, `PendingExec`, `Parked`, `impl PendingExec` (`new`, `park`, `answer`, `answer_all`, `list`, `sweep`), `answer_parked`. Test that moves: `a_parked_path_carries_no_byte_that_could_forge_a_row_on_the_wire` 3022–3056.

**Risque.** Visibility: `park` and `sweep` go from private-in-impl to `pub(super)` (called at 1027 and 846); `SWEEP_EVERY` and `ASK_TIMEOUT` become `pub(super)` — `SWEEP_EVERY` because `recv_loop` reads it, `ASK_TIMEOUT` only because `recv_loop`'s doc links it at 838. `Parked` and its `notif_fd` field must become `pub(super)` for the same reason: `close_supervision`'s doc at 782 links `[`Parked::notif_fd`]` and `mod.rs` has no `use` that would bring a private `Parked` into scope. This is the split's one genuinely irritating cost — two doc links widening visibility on types that have no code caller outside the file. Rustdoc: `[`close_supervision`]` at 1850 → `super::close_supervision`; `[`park_open`]` at 1853 → `super::open_serve::park_open` (and that forces `park_open` to `pub(super)`); `[`super::proc_control::ExecRing::push_verdict`]` at 1877 → `crate::sandbox::proc_control::ExecRing::push_verdict`, the `super`-depth shift. Code: `super::sanitize(path)` at 1888 → `crate::sandbox::sanitize`. docs_coverage: the `("sandbox/proc_enforce.rs", "Parked")` entry at docs_coverage.rs:921 re-keys to `"sandbox/proc_enforce/pending.rs"`. Cycles: none; depends on `super::notify` only. `PendingExec` must be re-exported from `mod.rs` so `proc_control.rs:213/229/684/724` keep compiling unchanged.

#### `src/sandbox/proc_enforce/overlay.rs` (~120 lignes)

**Rôle.** The live `--session` rule overlay, folded onto the resolved config policy at every decision. Shared between the supervisor's decide path and the control server that writes it, and the one lock in the tree that recovers from poisoning on an argument sandbox/locks.rs explicitly declines to supply.

**Ce qui bouge.** 194–266 the `// ── the live `--session` rule overlay ──` banner, `ProcOverlay`, `OverlayInner`, `impl ProcOverlay` (`new`, `remember`, `decide`, `snapshot`). Test that moves: `a_poisoned_overlay_still_decides_and_still_takes_a_rule` 5527–5560.

**Risque.** Smallest of the set and the least dependent: it imports only `crate::proc_policy::{ProcPolicy, ProcRule, Verdict}` and `crate::sandbox::locks::{read_locked, write_locked}` and is referenced by nothing else in the module except `start_inner` (line 423) and `Deciding.overlay`. No intra-doc link crosses out of it — `[`ProcRule::new`]` 206, `[`remember`](ProcOverlay::remember)` 208 and `[`decide`](ProcOverlay::decide)` 209 all resolve locally. docs_coverage: `("sandbox/proc_enforce.rs", "OverlayInner")` at docs_coverage.rs:920 re-keys to `"sandbox/proc_enforce/overlay.rs"`. One prose reference outside rustdoc's reach must be hand-updated: src/sandbox/locks.rs:26–27 says "`ProcOverlay` in `sandbox/proc_enforce.rs`" — a code span, not a link, so nothing will fail the build if it is missed, which is exactly why it should be fixed in the same commit. `ProcOverlay` must be re-exported from `mod.rs` for proc_control.rs:30. Cycles: none. Honest note: at ~120 lines this is the most optional module in the set — its justification is that it is a lock discipline documented at length and cited from another module by path, not its size.

#### `src/sandbox/proc_enforce/report.rs` (~135 lignes)

**Rôle.** What a finished run owes its user about the decisions it could not base on a name: the three counters, the word a mode's unmatched default deserves, and the teardown report that says a kind twice only when it happened more than once.

**Ce qui bouge.** 530–615 `Undecidable`, `impl Undecidable::report`, `unmatched_word`. Test that moves: `the_teardown_report_names_a_kind_that_happened_more_than_once_and_not_one_that_happened_once` 5703–5736.

**Risque.** Visibility: `Undecidable`, its three fields `exec`/`open`/`caller`, `report`, and `unmatched_word` all become `pub(super)` — the fields because they are bumped from three call sites that end up in three different files (`exec_verdict` 664 in mod.rs, `open_name` 730 in open_lens.rs, `caller_chain` 1066 in mod.rs), which is the counting-at-the-read-site discipline the type's own doc argues for at 548–554 and which the split preserves rather than disturbs. Rustdoc: `[`handle_notif`]` at 550 → `super::handle_notif` and `[`ProcEnforce`]` at 556 → `super::ProcEnforce`; both are private items in mod.rs, which is fine because `mise run rustdoc` runs with `--document-private-items` (mise.toml:101), so no `private_intra_doc_links` warning fires — but the paths themselves must still be corrected or the links resolve to nothing and `-D warnings` fails. Cycles: none — this module imports only `crate::proc_policy::{ProcPolicy, Verdict}`; the reference back to mod.rs is in prose only. Scope discipline: do NOT also move the `crate::diag::warn` call sites here. They are deliberately at the read (549–554), and hoisting them would dissolve a stated design decision.

#### `src/sandbox/proc_enforce/tests.rs` (~1700 lignes)

**Rôle.** The tests that need a real cage: everything that materializes the shim, hands over a live listener fd, and drains notifications until a payload exits. They exercise the supervisor as a whole, so they belong beside mod.rs rather than under any one lens.

**Ce qui bouge.** The harnesses `run_with_open_lens` 3160–3239, `materialized_shim` 4874–4890, `spawn_shim` 4891–4906, `run_under_supervisor` 4946–4957, `run_under_supervisor_n` 4958–4968, `run_under_supervisor_full` 4969–4985, `run_under_supervisor_notified` 4986–5051, `DecidingParts` 5469–5512, `UNREADABLE` 5518; and every test that uses one of them: 3240–3543, 3660–3703, 3996–4038, 4133–4162, 4415–4519, 4520–4614, 4650–4710, 4825–4873, 4907–4945, 5052–5225, 5226–5468, 5561–5702. Plus `ending_supervision_denies_what_is_still_parked_before_closing_their_descriptor` 2980–3021 (its subject is mod.rs's `close_supervision`) and the two `wrap_command` tests 4711–4753. `mod open_path_tests` 2941–3133 is dissolved: four tests to target.rs, three to cagepath.rs, one to pending.rs, one here.

**Risque.** This is where the work actually is, and where the split can go quietly wrong. Everything the harnesses reach must be visible from a descendant module — which `pub(super)` satisfies, since a `pub(super)` item in `proc_enforce::notify` is visible throughout `proc_enforce` including `proc_enforce::tests`; and `handle_notif`, `Deciding` and `close_supervision` stay private in mod.rs, which a child module can reach directly. `DecidingParts` (5469–5512) is the constraint that decides several placements: it constructs `Deciding` from seven owned pieces and is used by tests at 4479, 5080, 5168, 5574, 5600, 5617, 5675, 5689 that would otherwise scatter — those tests all stay here. Set the file up like src/sandbox/proxy/tests.rs (declared `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` from mod.rs, opening with `use super::*;` plus explicit `use super::{notify::*, target::*, …}`). docs_coverage treats a file named `tests.rs` as test-only and skips it whole (docs_coverage.rs:997–1000), so nothing there needs prose. Residual risk: a test silently landing in the wrong file is caught by the compiler here (every one of them calls the symbol it names), so the failure mode is loud rather than a coverage hole.

### Autres observations

**A real doc bug the split would expose.** At 511–516 the doc block that opens "Prepend the shim invocation to a command…" — plainly written for `wrap_command` — runs straight into "The flag that asks the shim for the open lens…" and lands on `const OPEN_LENS_FLAG` (516). `wrap_command` (518) is left with no doc at all, which is exactly why `("sandbox/proc_enforce.rs", "wrap_command")` sits in `UNDOCUMENTED_MODULE_ITEMS` at docs_coverage.rs:927. Split the block in two and the allowlist entry can be deleted rather than re-keyed.

**docs_coverage is a hard gate on the split.** All eight entries at docs_coverage.rs:920–927 must be re-keyed in the same commit — `OverlayInner`→overlay.rs, `Parked`→pending.rs, `notif_id_valid_code`/`notif_recv_code`/`notif_send_code`/`seccomp_ioc`→notify.rs, `start_inner`/`wrap_command`→`sandbox/proc_enforce/mod.rs`. The keys are `src/`-relative paths built at docs_coverage.rs:993–1002, and the staleness assertion at 1082–1090 fails on an entry naming an item that is now gone, so a partial rename fails the test twice over.

**Two `super::` hazards specific to a directory module.** (1) Code: `super::sanitize` at 1888, 2609, 2628 must become `crate::sandbox::sanitize` once those lines live one level deeper; the four `super::` uses inside `start_inner` (415, 418, 420, 434, 436, 437) are untouched because `start_inner` stays in `mod.rs`, where `super` is still `sandbox`. (2) Docs: `[`super::proc_control::ExecRing::push_verdict`]` at 1877 shifts with `PendingExec`; the header's `[`super::observe_feed`]`/`[`super::proc_control`]` (4) and `[`super::seccomp`]` (75) do not, since the header stays in `mod.rs`.

**Do not document the `mod` declarations.** mise.toml:94–98 records that this project has already been bitten by it: an outer doc comment on a `mod X;` merges with the child's own `//!` header and moves the whole block's link resolution into the *declaring* module's scope. Write bare `mod open_lens;` etc. and put every `//!` inside the child file.

**Rejected: an `exec_decide.rs`.** `exec_verdict` (619–674), `caller_chain` (1032–1101) and `refusal_errno` (1079–1101) are only 126 lines, they need `Deciding` (a child→parent `use super::Deciding` back-edge, legal but the one place the layering would stop being one-way), and every test that drives them — `an_execve_whose_target_cannot_be_read…` 5561–5592, `a_caller_whose_program_is_not_a_name…` 5626–5702 — needs the `DecidingParts` harness that stays in tests.rs. Leave them in `mod.rs` beside `handle_notif`, which lands `mod.rs` at roughly 760 lines: header 1–115, `SHIM_CAGE_PATH`/`NOTIF_SOCK_CAGE_PATH` 152–156, the lifecycle 268–528, the supervisor 743–878 and 962–1030, the exec decision 619–674 and 1032–1101, plus `mod` declarations and the `pub(crate) use` re-exports that keep the external surface at its current paths.

**Dead structure.** Line 192 is a section banner, `// ── the in-cage shim ──`, with nothing under it — the shim is a separate binary in `proc-shim/`. Delete it rather than carry it into a child.

**Over-broad visibility, independent of the split.** `send_resp` (1822) is `pub(crate)` with exactly two callers, both in this file (1141, 1149) and both of which end up in the same new module. `OpenLens` (2393) is `pub(crate)` and has no user outside `proc_enforce` at all. Both can be tightened while moving, at no cost to any call site.

**A latent bug the split surfaces rather than causes.** `PARKED_OPENS` (1580) is a process-wide `AtomicUsize` against a process-wide `PARKED_OPEN_CAP` of 64, but a single sbx process can run more than one supervisor: `start_for_task` (387) exists precisely because "a session serving two invocations at once" is a real state (377–386). Two concurrent supervisors therefore share one flood cap, so a cage that parks 64 FIFO opens starves its sibling's opens into `EACCES`. `ADDFD_UNAVAILABLE` (1154), `OPENAT2_UNAVAILABLE` (2258) and `UNNAMEABLE_OPEN_SAID` (741) are also process-global; for the first two that is correct (they record a kernel property), for `UNNAMEABLE_OPEN_SAID` it means the second launch in a process says nothing. Worth a separate look — the cap plausibly belongs on `OpenLens`, which is already per-launch and already holds the other per-launch state (`cache`, `mounts`).

**Ordering, if this is done incrementally.** notify.rs → target.rs → cagepath.rs are pure leaves and can each land alone, each a self-contained commit with its own tests, before any of the larger moves. open_lens.rs and open_serve.rs should land together (the `handle_open` extraction is what makes both clean). pending.rs, overlay.rs and report.rs are independent of all of the above and of each other. tests.rs should be last, after every `pub(super)` it needs already exists.

---

## `config-mod` — src/config/mod.rs (5846 lines), and how it relates to src/config/{types,schema,load,validate}.rs

**Verdict : **Découpage utile****

### Cartographie actuelle

`src/config/mod.rs` (5846 lines) is the config crate's root: it declares the eleven submodules, owns the layering/gating engine, and holds the resolved data model that the rest of the crate consumes. Its own internal sections, verified by line:

- 1–90 module doc, `mod`/`use` block, re-exports (incl. three `#[cfg(test)] use` lines at 40, 54, 62 that exist solely so `tests.rs` can reach `schema::RawCmd`, `secrets::validate_*`, `load::merge_profile_apps` through `use super::*`), and the four file/label constants.
- 92–197 the reserved-env-key denylist: `is_reserved_env_key` (132), `is_proxy_env_key` (192).
- 199–251 broker types: `BrokerTarget` (206) + `impl` (213), `BrokerBinding` (231).
- 253–487 `struct Resolved` — 60-odd fields, almost entirely doc comment.
- 489–501 `AppHomeScope`; 503–517 `BundleProvision`; 519–659 `struct ResolvedApp`.
- 661–1222 `impl Resolved`: `merge_app` (669–794), `apply_override_channel` (811–855), `apply_override` (857–1221), `validate_override` (1201–1221).
- 1224–1347 override scalars: `OverrideScalars` (1229), `build_override_scalars` (1247), `override_fatal_error` (1335).
- 1349–1612 the `[plugin.<name>]` attachment pass: `apply_plugin_host_config_to_secrets` (1363), `apply_to_header_secrets` (1384), `apply_plugin_host_config` (1419), `apply_to_tasks` (1473), `apply_to_sources` (1490), `host_config_for` (1514), `validated_programs` (1557), `declared_vars_of` (1599).
- 1614–1638 `warn_mise_nix_packages`.
- 1640–2593 `fn resolve` — one 954-line linear function with ~60 locals: read every global field, then every project field through `Gate`, then apps, brokers, plugin tables, then build the struct literal.
- 2595–3194 per-layer readers and merge primitives: `network_stats_of` (2598), `network_default_methods_of` (2607), `builtin_app_default_methods` (2617), `resolve_app_default_methods` (2626), `warn_unknown_proc_keys` (2650), `warn_unknown_notify_keys` (2666), `warn_if_baseline_sets_default_methods` (2682), `warn_if_app_sets_stats` (2699), `overlay_limits` (2713), `is_zone_name` (2733), `apply_seccomp` (2751), `apply_devices` (2793), `apply_fs` (2826), `union_forward` (2913), `warn_unknown_keys` (2935), `warn_unknown_app_keys` (2982), `apply_ssh_agent` (3008), `union_ssh_agent` (3048), `union_devices` (3061), `parse_forward_entry` (3075), `sort_forward` (3123), `mark_limit_origins` (3131), `global_limits` (3151), `enforce_secret_posture` (3163), `warn_l4_l7_conflicts` (3184).
- 3196–3976 the app resolver: `resolve_apps` (3201), `is_valid_app_name` (3260), `resolve_app` (3277–3935, 666 lines), `apply_app_secret` (3940), `global_app_source` (3964), `project_app_source` (3971).
- 3975–4248 env/bind primitives: `apply_env` (3978), `bind_mode` (4012), `apply_binds` (4036), `expand_bind_path` (4221); with `parse_tcp_endpoint` (4087) and `resolve_brokers` (4112) wedged between `apply_binds` and `expand_bind_path`.
- 4250–5014 the declared-tool pipeline: `apply_packages` (4257), `apply_fresh_releases` (4312), `apply_tools` (4343), `apply_prebuilt_libs` (4488), `apply_resolvers` (4577), `apply_flakes` (4652), `parse_backend` (4709), `classify_backend` (4730), the four `*_RESOLVE_SENTINEL` consts (4826/4831/4837/4844), `is_injection_free_url` (4856), `strip_fetch_scheme` (4870), `is_valid_deb_url` (4885), `is_valid_appimage_url` (4896), `is_valid_binary_url` (4917), `is_valid_tarball_url` (4933), `is_valid_deb_github_locator` (4949), `is_valid_deb_apt_locator` (4980), `upsert_package` (4991).
- 5016–5155 the trust gate: `struct Gate` (5023) + `impl` (5030–5122), `refuse_untrusted` (5124), `untrusted_reason` (5135), `TRUST_DROP_MARKER` (5150), `is_trust_drop` (5153).
- 5157–5247 leaf helpers: `unknown_notify_event` (5159), `mode_from_parent` (5175), `parse_http2_hosts` (5190), `parse_duration` (5220), `DURATION_MAX_SECS` (5247).
- 5249–5418 egress groups: `type NetGroups` (5252ish), `group_ref` (5262), `group_refs` (5268), `classify_entries` (5287), `take_net_groups` (5334), `build_net_groups` (5355), `is_valid_group_name` (5412) — interleaved with `is_valid_uri_scheme` (5400).
- 5419–5577 charset validators and two one-liners: `is_valid_nixpkgs_source` (5425), `is_valid_env_key` (5434), `is_valid_package_name` (5442), `is_valid_attr` (5468), `is_bare_nix_attr` (5487), `is_valid_mise_token` (5501), `is_valid_flake_ref` (5521), `upsert` (5556), `dropped_binds_warning` (5566).
- 5579–5846 `#[cfg(test)] mod bad_fields_cost_a_field` (5586–5839, the only inline test module) then `mod tests` (5841) and `mod catalogue_tests` (5846).

Two structural facts govern any change here. First, every sibling except `view.rs`/`manage.rs`/`safety.rs` opens with `use super::*` (`types.rs:5`, `validate.rs:14`, `load.rs:7`, `secrets.rs:6`, `tasks.rs:42`, `tests.rs:5`), and they reach mod.rs's *private* items through it — `validate.rs` alone uses `is_zone_name`, `is_valid_uri_scheme`, `is_valid_nixpkgs_source`, `classify_entries`, `parse_http2_hosts`, `mode_from_parent`, `unknown_notify_event`, `parse_duration`, `sort_forward`, `parse_forward_entry`, `NetGroups`, `AppHomeScope`. Second, `view.rs`, `manage.rs`, `overrides.rs` and `fspolicy.rs` do *not* glob-import; they write explicit `super::` paths (`view.rs:1669` `super::union_forward`, `:1718` `super::overlay_limits`, `:1770` `super::enforce_secret_posture`, `:1078` `super::untrusted_reason`; `manage.rs:690` `super::parse_forward_entry`; `overrides.rs:312,341` `super::warn_unknown_keys`, `:381` `super::take_net_groups`). Both patterns keep working across a move as long as mod.rs retains a `use <newmod>::{…}` binding for the name, because a private `use` in `config` is nameable by every descendant and resolvable through `super::`. The existing `#[cfg(test)] use secrets::{…}` at line 54 is proof the pattern already carries load in this crate.

### Justification

This is not one cohesive state machine. It is one 954-line state machine (`fn resolve`, 1640–2593) surrounded by six or seven subjects that share nothing but `&mut Vec<String> warnings` and `TrustState`. Concretely: the app resolver (`resolve_app`, 3270–3935) takes twelve explicit parameters and touches no local of `resolve`; the package-locator grammar (`parse_backend`/`classify_backend`/the six URL validators/the five charset validators) is a set of pure leaf predicates whose main consumers are *outside* `config` entirely (`src/sandbox/{deb,tarball,binary,appimage,prebuilt,nixhub}.rs`, `src/store.rs:1668`); the `[plugin.<name>]` attachment pass (1349–1612) is a self-contained two-entry-point walk. None of these is entangled with the layering engine — they are called from it and return.

The cost is bounded and mechanical, and lower than it looks, for one reason: the file's intra-doc links cluster along exactly the seams I propose. Of the 60-odd bare links in the file, `apply_packages` (×6), `apply_tools` (×5), `is_valid_deb_url` (×5), `TARBALL_RESOLVE_SENTINEL` (×3), `apply_flakes`, `apply_resolvers`, `parse_backend`, `strip_fetch_scheme`, `is_valid_tarball_url` all sit inside the tool block and stay intra-module if it moves as one piece; `refuse_untrusted`, `Gate::refuse`, `untrusted_reason`, `TRUST_DROP_MARKER`, `dropped_binds_warning` all sit inside the gate block; `apply_plugin_host_config`(×2) and `apply_plugin_host_config_to_secrets`(×2) inside the plugin block. The `Resolved::merge_app` (×9 as `[…](Resolved::merge_app)`), `Resolved::apply_override`, `Resolved::validate_override` links are method-qualified and resolve wherever the `impl` block lives. Cyclic dependency is not a risk category in Rust — modules in one crate may reference each other freely — so the only real hazards are (a) a name that stops being glob-visible and (b) a bare doc link crossing a new boundary. Both are enumerable, and I enumerate them per module below.

Why "worthwhile" and not "strongly recommended": `fn resolve` stays 954 lines under every proposal. It is a single linear pass over ~60 accumulator locals, and there is no way to break it up that does not either invent a `ResolveState` builder (which would put a mutable god-struct where explicit locals are today) or thread twenty `&mut` parameters through sub-functions. Leave it whole and say so. Likewise `merge_app` (669–794) reads nearly every field of `Resolved` and belongs beside it. So the split takes the file from 5846 to roughly 2450 lines, not to 1200 — a real improvement, not a transformation. The three highest-value, lowest-risk moves are #1 (apps), #2 (tools) and #6 (gate); they are independent of each other and can land as three separate reviewable commits. Modules #3–#5 and #7 are worth doing but carry less benefit per unit of diff, and nothing is lost by deferring them.

### Découpage proposé

#### `src/config/apps.rs` (~1055 lignes)

**Rôle.** The per-app overlay resolver: the `ResolvedApp` data model and the parallel layering engine that produces one, global-app-under-project-app, gated by the project's verdict. It is a sibling engine to `fn resolve`, not a part of it — `resolve` calls `resolve_apps` once (mod.rs:3201) and passes every baseline it needs by reference.

**Ce qui bouge.** `AppHomeScope` (489–501), `ResolvedApp` (503–659, taking the doc-comment repair described in other_observations), `builtin_app_default_methods` (2614–2624), `resolve_app_default_methods` (2626–2641), `warn_if_app_sets_stats` (2695–2705), `warn_unknown_app_keys` (2968–2994), `resolve_apps` (3196–3252), `is_valid_app_name` (3254–3268), `resolve_app` (3270–3935), `apply_app_secret` (3937–3962), `global_app_source` (3964–3969), `project_app_source` (3971–3973). Header is `use super::*;` like every other sibling. STAYS in mod.rs: `Resolved::merge_app` (661–794) — it mutates and reads nearly every `Resolved` field and defines that struct's precedence rules; `BundleProvision` (503–517), which both `Resolved.provisions` and `ResolvedApp.provisions` carry; and `resolve`'s call site plus the `warn_mise_nix_packages` loop over `apps` at 2513–2515.

**Risque.** Visibility: mod.rs must add `pub(crate) use apps::{AppHomeScope, ResolvedApp, is_valid_app_name};` — `crate::config::AppHomeScope` is named at src/sandbox/launch.rs:866,867,1102,1103,1510,1511,7070 and src/cli/app.rs; `crate::config::ResolvedApp` at src/sandbox/launch.rs:7072, src/cli/upgrade.rs, src/testutil.rs; `config::is_valid_app_name` at src/cli/app.rs:375,856,936, src/cli/config.rs:2361,2568, src/main.rs:240,755, src/store.rs:1175 (and a doc link at src/store.rs:1084). Plus a private `use apps::{resolve_apps, warn_unknown_app_keys, warn_if_app_sets_stats, builtin_app_default_methods, resolve_app_default_methods};` so `resolve` and the glob-importing siblings still see them — `validate.rs` names `AppHomeScope` in `validate_home_scope` (validate.rs:203) purely through `use super::*`, and `tests.rs` constructs `ResolvedApp` 18 times and `AppHomeScope` 11 times the same way. Doc links: `warn_unknown_app_keys`'s doc (2968–2969) contains `[`warn_unknown_keys`]`, which stays in mod.rs — it resolves through `use super::*`, but rewrite it as `[`super::warn_unknown_keys`]` to be safe under `-D rustdoc::warnings`. `ResolvedApp`'s field docs use `[`merge_app`](Resolved::merge_app)` (×9) and `[`Self::cmd`]` — both survive the move unchanged; `ResolvedApp.binds` names `[`load()`]`, which resolves via mod.rs's `pub(crate) use load::{… load …}`. `resolve_app` carries `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` (3269) — move the attribute with it. Inline tests: none in any moved range (mod.rs's only `#[cfg(test)] mod` is at 5586). Cycles: none possible; `apps.rs` calls back into mod.rs (`Gate`, `apply_*`, `validate_*`) and mod.rs calls `resolve_apps`, which is legal and normal in Rust.

#### `src/config/tools.rs` (~885 lignes)

**Rôle.** The declared-tool pipeline: turn `[packages]`/`[flakes]`/`[tarball]`/`[deb]`/`[appimage]`/`[binary]` into `Vec<Package>`, plus the locator grammar that decides what a package value may contain. Keeping the fold and the grammar in ONE module is deliberate — they cross-reference each other in a dozen doc comments, and the grammar's whole argument (`is_valid_attr`'s 'this charset is the whole barrier on one path', `strip_fetch_scheme`'s 'one definition, six validators') is only legible beside the callers it constrains.

**Ce qui bouge.** The contiguous block 4250–5014: `apply_packages`, `apply_fresh_releases`, `apply_tools`, `apply_prebuilt_libs`, `apply_resolvers`, `apply_flakes`, `parse_backend`, `classify_backend`, `TARBALL_RESOLVE_SENTINEL`/`DEB_RESOLVE_SENTINEL`/`APPIMAGE_RESOLVE_SENTINEL`/`BINARY_RESOLVE_SENTINEL`, `is_injection_free_url`, `strip_fetch_scheme`, `is_valid_deb_url`, `is_valid_appimage_url`, `is_valid_binary_url`, `is_valid_tarball_url`, `is_valid_deb_github_locator`, `is_valid_deb_apt_locator`, `upsert_package`; plus the charset validators 5438–5552 (`is_valid_package_name`, `is_valid_attr`, `is_bare_nix_attr`, `is_valid_mise_token`, `is_valid_flake_ref`); plus `warn_mise_nix_packages` (1614–1638), whose only two callers are `resolve` (2515) and `resolve_app`. STAYS: `is_valid_nixpkgs_source` (5419–5429) — it governs the nixpkgs *channel*, not a package locator, and its only caller is `validate::validate_nixpkgs`.

**Risque.** Visibility: mod.rs must add `pub(crate) use tools::{is_valid_deb_url, is_valid_appimage_url, is_valid_binary_url, is_valid_tarball_url, is_valid_deb_github_locator, is_valid_deb_apt_locator, is_valid_attr, is_bare_nix_attr};` — all eight are reached as `crate::config::…` from src/sandbox/appimage.rs:164,248, src/sandbox/deb.rs:182,692,774,1263, src/sandbox/tarball.rs:39, src/sandbox/binary.rs:34, src/sandbox/prebuilt.rs:1176, src/sandbox/nixhub.rs:497,715, src/store.rs:1668. A private `use tools::{apply_packages, apply_tools, apply_flakes, apply_resolvers, apply_prebuilt_libs, apply_fresh_releases, parse_backend, upsert_package, warn_mise_nix_packages, is_valid_package_name, is_valid_mise_token, is_valid_flake_ref, TARBALL_RESOLVE_SENTINEL};` keeps `tests.rs` working — it calls `parse_backend`, `apply_packages`, `apply_tools`, `apply_flakes`, `apply_resolvers`, `upsert_package`, `warn_mise_nix_packages`, `is_valid_attr`, `is_bare_nix_attr`, `is_valid_mise_token`, `is_valid_package_name` and `TARBALL_RESOLVE_SENTINEL` through `use super::*` alone (e.g. tests.rs:6020 `package_name_and_attribute_validators`, :11078 `every_fetching_backend_refuses_plaintext_by_default`), and `types.rs` names `is_valid_flake_ref` in `Backend::Flake`'s doc. Doc links: every bare link in this block points inside it (`[`apply_packages`]` ×6, `[`apply_tools`]` ×5, `[`is_valid_deb_url`]` ×5, `[`TARBALL_RESOLVE_SENTINEL`]` ×3, `[`apply_flakes`]`, `[`apply_resolvers`]`, `[`parse_backend`]`, `[`strip_fetch_scheme`]`, `[`is_valid_attr`]` ×2, `[`is_valid_tarball_url`]`) — which is exactly why the block must not be split further. Links pointing out are already absolute or qualified: `[`Resolved::allow_insecure_http`]` ×2, `[`crate::store::provision_unfree`]` ×2, `[`Package`]`, `[`Backend::FlakeInline`]` (the latter two via `use super::*` → `pub(crate) use types::*`). Links pointing IN from outside stay valid through the `pub(crate) use`: src/sandbox/deb.rs:150 writes `[`is_valid_deb_url`](crate::config::is_valid_deb_url)`. `apply_tools` and `apply_resolvers` carry `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` (4342, 4576) — move them. Inline tests: none. Cycles: none.

#### `src/config/override_apply.rs` (~430 lignes)

**Rôle.** The authoritative application of a one-shot override onto an already-resolved config — the half that `src/config/overrides.rs` deliberately does not do. That module's header (overrides.rs:45–48) already states the seam in prose: 'This module only *collects and merges* the inputs into one overlay; the authoritative application onto a resolved configuration is [`super::Resolved::apply_override`]'. This turns that paragraph into a file boundary. Do NOT fold it into overrides.rs — that would dissolve the stated seam and push that file past 3100 lines.

**Ce qui bouge.** A second inherent `impl Resolved { … }` block holding `apply_override_channel` (796–855), `apply_override` (857–1221) and `validate_override` (1201–1221), plus their private support: `OverrideScalars` (1224–1240), `build_override_scalars` (1242–1330) and `override_fatal_error` (1332–1347). `merge_app` (661–794) and the closing of mod.rs's own `impl Resolved` stay behind. `const OVERRIDE_SOURCE` (mod.rs:84) stays in mod.rs (it also labels `Resolved::apply_override_channel`'s validator notes and nothing else), reached via `use super::*`.

**Risque.** Visibility: none changes. All three are `pub(crate) fn` methods on `Resolved`; Rust permits inherent `impl` blocks for a type in any module of the defining crate, and a method path (`Resolved::apply_override`) is independent of which file the block lives in. That is also what keeps overrides.rs:46–48's two doc links (`[`super::Resolved::apply_override`]`, `[`super::Resolved::apply_override_channel`]`) resolving with no edit. Doc links inside the move: `[`build_override_scalars`]`, `[`Resolved::apply_override`]`, `[`Resolved::validate_override`]`, `[`Resolved::apply_override_channel`]` are intra-cluster or method-qualified; `[`crate::sandbox::effective_lock_target`]` and `[`Provenance::Override`]` are absolute / glob-resolved. The one thing to watch is that `apply_override` calls fifteen mod.rs helpers (`apply_env`, `apply_binds`, `apply_packages`, `apply_seccomp`, `apply_devices`, `apply_fs`, `apply_ssh_agent`, `union_forward`, `union_devices`, `union_ssh_agent`, `overlay_limits`, `mark_limit_origins`, `enforce_secret_posture`, `canonicalize_binds`, `sbx_control_plane_roots`) plus five `validate::*` — all arrive through `use super::*`, but `apply_packages` now lives in tools.rs, so mod.rs's private `use tools::{apply_packages, …}` (module #2) is a prerequisite. Inline tests: none — the override tests live in tests.rs:7515–8126 and reach `apply_override` through `Resolved`, so they need no edit. Cycles: none. Naming note: the file cannot be called `override.rs` (reserved word) and must not be called `overrides.rs`.

#### `src/config/plugin_config.rs` (~265 lignes)

**Rôle.** The single pass that attaches each `[plugin.<name>]` table to every plugin instance the config reaches — a wire credential's resolver chain and signer, a task's `[inject]`, an app's secrets, a broker binding — validating each against the manifest that instance carries. Two entry points (`apply_plugin_host_config_to_secrets` before the posture clear, `apply_plugin_host_config` after) that `resolve` calls at 2470–2478 and 2528–2536, and six private helpers that nothing else touches.

**Ce qui bouge.** The contiguous block 1349–1612: `apply_plugin_host_config_to_secrets` (1349–1373), `apply_to_header_secrets` (1375–1402), `apply_plugin_host_config` (1404–1471), `apply_to_tasks` (1470–1488), `apply_to_sources` (1487–1506), `host_config_for` (1508–1555), `validated_programs` (1538–1594), `declared_vars_of` (1596–1612).

**Risque.** Visibility: mod.rs must add `pub(crate) use plugin_config::validated_programs;` — it is called as `crate::config::validated_programs` from src/cli/plugins.rs:700 and :2278. The other seven are private and used only by `resolve`, so a plain `use plugin_config::{apply_plugin_host_config, apply_plugin_host_config_to_secrets};` suffices. Doc links: `[`apply_plugin_host_config`]` ×2 and `[`apply_plugin_host_config_to_secrets`]` ×2 are intra-cluster. `validated_programs`' doc names `[`is_valid_attr`]`, which module #2 moves to tools.rs — this is the one genuine cross-boundary bare link in the whole proposal; it resolves through `use super::*` + mod.rs's re-import, but write it `[`super::is_valid_attr`]` (or `[`crate::config::is_valid_attr`]`) so it cannot rot. `[`crate::store::provision_unfree`]` is absolute. Note that `resolve`'s comment at 2467–2470 references `apply_plugin_host_config_to_secrets` inside a `//` (not `///`) comment, so rustdoc does not check it — but update the text anyway, since it names a function the reader now has to go find. Inline tests: none; the coverage is tests.rs:8810–9160 (`a_signer_named_by_an_app_or_a_task_gets_its_plugin_table`, `a_plugin_table_supplies_only_the_variables_the_manifest_reads`, …) and reaches these through `resolve`. Cycles: none.

#### `src/config/broker.rs` (~180 lignes)

**Rôle.** The broker binding: where a broker plugin's host resource lives, and the fail-closed fold from `[broker.<name>]` tables into the bindings a launch acts on. Currently the type pair sits at the top of the file (199–251) and the resolver 3900 lines below (4085–4207) with no way to read one beside the other.

**Ce qui bouge.** `BrokerTarget` enum + `impl BrokerTarget::describe` (199–224), `BrokerBinding` (226–251), `parse_tcp_endpoint` (4085–4104), `resolve_brokers` (4106–4207). STAYS: `resolve`'s project-layer `[broker.*]` gating loop (2059–2110) — it folds a trusted project's `allow` onto the global table and refuses `socket`/`secret`, which is layering work, not binding work, and it mutates `broker_cfg`/`broker_origin` locals of `resolve`.

**Risque.** Visibility: mod.rs must add `pub(crate) use broker::{BrokerBinding, BrokerTarget};` — `crate::config::BrokerTarget` is matched at src/sandbox/launch.rs:4242,4250,4254,4267 and used six times in src/sandbox/broker.rs; `BrokerBinding` once in src/sandbox/broker.rs; `tests.rs` names `BrokerTarget` twice through `use super::*`. `resolve_brokers` and `parse_tcp_endpoint` are private, so `use broker::resolve_brokers;` in mod.rs is enough for `resolve`'s call at 2523. Dependencies pointing back: `resolve_brokers` calls `expand_bind_path` (staying in mod.rs at 4209–4248) and `crate::config::secrets::parse_secret_ref` — the first via `use super::*`, the second already absolute. Doc links: none bare crossing out; `BrokerBinding.secret`/`.host` reference `crate::plugins::HostConfig` explicitly. Name collision check: `crate::plugins::broker` already exists but is a different path and is always written fully qualified (tests.rs:2470 `crate::plugins::broker::BrokerPlugin`), so a `config::broker` module introduces no ambiguity. Inline tests: none; coverage is tests.rs:2417–2623. Cycles: none.

#### `src/config/gate.rs` (~165 lignes)

**Rôle.** The trust gate and the complete vocabulary of a dropped-for-want-of-trust warning. This is the split with the best ratio of clarity to lines: `TRUST_DROP_MARKER`'s own doc says 'there is more than one producer: [`untrusted_reason`] phrases it one way and [`dropped_binds_warning`] another', yet those three items are currently 430 lines apart (5150, 5135, 5566), and the `Gate` that routes most refusals to them is 550 lines from `resolve`'s first use of it.

**Ce qui bouge.** `struct Gate<'a>` (5016–5028) and `impl Gate<'_>` with `refuse`/`take`/`take_validated`/`union` (5030–5122), `refuse_untrusted` (5124–5133), `untrusted_reason` (5135–5142), `TRUST_DROP_MARKER` (5144–5150), `is_trust_drop` (5152–5155), `dropped_binds_warning` (5563–5577).

**Risque.** Visibility: mod.rs must add `pub(crate) use gate::{is_trust_drop, untrusted_reason};` — `crate::config::is_trust_drop` is called at src/sandbox/launch.rs:708,3910 and doc-linked at :689; `config::untrusted_reason` at src/sandbox/launch.rs:5313 and src/cli/upgrade.rs:974, and `super::untrusted_reason` at src/config/view.rs:1078,1256,1292 (view.rs does not glob-import, so it needs the name bound in `config` — a plain `use` would suffice, but the external callers force `pub(crate)` anyway). Add a private `use gate::{Gate, refuse_untrusted, dropped_binds_warning};` so `resolve` (mod.rs), `resolve_app` (apps.rs) and the four `apply_*` guards in tools.rs keep reaching them, and so tests.rs:10342 `every_dropped_security_field_warning_is_recognised_as_a_trust_drop` and :4043 keep resolving `is_trust_drop`/`dropped_binds_warning` through `use super::*`. Doc links: `[`refuse_untrusted`]`, `[`Gate::refuse`]`, `[`untrusted_reason`]`, `[`dropped_binds_warning`]`, `[`TRUST_DROP_MARKER`]` are all intra-cluster and move together. One external doc link points in — src/sandbox/launch.rs:689 `[`crate::config::is_trust_drop`]` — and the `pub(crate) use` keeps it alive. Inline tests: none. Cycles: none. `Gate` has no dependency on anything in mod.rs except `TrustState` (already imported from `crate::trust`), so this file is nearly free-standing.

#### `src/config/netgroups.rs` (~160 lignes)

**Rôle.** Reusable egress groups: the `[network.groups]` vocabulary a `@<name>` reference resolves against — how a group is taken out of the one layer allowed to define it, how it is pre-classified, and how an entry list expands a reference. Currently interleaved with unrelated leaf validators (`is_valid_uri_scheme` sits between `build_net_groups` and `is_valid_group_name`).

**Ce qui bouge.** `type NetGroups` (5249–5252), `group_ref` (5254–5266), `group_refs` (5268–5275), `classify_entries` (5277–5322), `take_net_groups` (5324–5352), `build_net_groups` (5354–5390), `is_valid_group_name` (5406–5418). STAYS: `is_valid_uri_scheme` (5392–5405), which is the `[open]` key grammar and belongs with the other leaf validators in mod.rs; and `resolve`'s group plumbing at 1671–1682 (build the map, warn a project that declared one), which is layering.

**Risque.** Visibility: mod.rs must add `pub(crate) use netgroups::{group_ref, group_refs, is_valid_group_name};` — reached as `config::is_valid_group_name` at src/cli/net.rs:2557,2976 and src/cli/app.rs:585, `config::group_refs` at src/cli/bundle.rs:535 (doc-referenced at src/cli/mod.rs:340), and `group_ref`/`group_refs` from load.rs through `use super::*`. Add a private `use netgroups::{NetGroups, classify_entries, take_net_groups, build_net_groups};` — `validate.rs` names `NetGroups` and calls `classify_entries` purely through `use super::*` (validate.rs:426 `validate_network`, :527 `validate_network_table`), `tests.rs` calls `build_net_groups` (tests.rs:348) and constructs `NetGroups` (tests.rs:182 `make_groups`) the same way, and `overrides.rs:381` writes the explicit path `super::take_net_groups(&mut merged.network)`. That explicit `super::` path resolves through mod.rs's private `use`; if you prefer not to lean on that, change the one call site to `super::netgroups::take_net_groups`. Doc links: `[`NetGroups`]` (in `build_net_groups`' doc) and `[`Slot::Allow`]` (same doc; `Slot` arrives via mod.rs's `use crate::allowlist::{…, Slot}` and the glob) are fine; `take_net_groups`' doc names `[`validate::validate_network_table`]`, which resolves from netgroups.rs through `use super::*` — rewrite as `[`super::validate::validate_network_table`]` for safety. Inline tests: none; coverage is tests.rs:192–583. Cycles: none.

### Autres observations

**A real doc-comment defect the split would force you to fix.** `ResolvedApp`'s doc comment is broken at src/config/mod.rs:503–522. Line 503 reads `/// An app's resolved overlay over the sandbox baseline: the command to run plus the extra` — and then line 504 starts `BundleProvision`'s own doc (`/// One bundle's install step, as the fold hands it to a launch…`), so `ResolvedApp`'s opening sentence is rendered as part of `BundleProvision`'s documentation, and `ResolvedApp`'s doc (519–522) now begins mid-sentence with the lowercase fragment `/// environment, binds, packages, network posture, and credentials it declares — each`. `BundleProvision` was clearly inserted into the middle of `ResolvedApp`'s doc block. rustdoc does not complain (both links resolve), so `mise run rustdoc` is silent about it. Fix while moving `ResolvedApp` to `apps.rs`: line 503 rejoins 519–522, and `BundleProvision` keeps 504–510.

**A second `#[cfg(test)]` sibling is already precedent for splitting the suite.** `src/config/catalogue_tests.rs:4–8` states the rule in prose: 'Kept apart from [`super::tests`], which exercises the resolution engine… A contributor adding a shipped profile has no reason to open the engine's suite, and one changing the engine has no reason to page through these.' That is the same argument for subdividing `tests.rs`.

**Should tests.rs (11317 lines, 341 `#[test]`s) follow the split? Yes — but as a separate change, after the source split, and on the same topic axis.** It costs nothing today (it is already out of mod.rs, and `cargo test --lib config::tests::<filter>` already targets it), so it is not urgent; and the real work is not moving tests but extracting fixtures, which is independent of everything above. Specifics if you do it:
- The clusters are visible but *not contiguous*, which is the main cost. Egress groups 65–583; app overlay / brokers / limits / `merge_app` 584–3986; env+binds 3988–4799; packages/backends/resolvers/libs 4800–6069; nixpkgs/network/gui/forward/fs/devices/ssh_agent 6070–7062; secrets+resolver plugins 7088–10167 — but one-shot overrides are wedged inside that span at 7515–8326, and notify+overrides reappear at 10797–11317. Any split has to re-sequence, not just cut.
- Fixture builders are scattered across at least six sites and shared across clusters: 65–190 (`validate_network` shim, `raw`, `declare_groups`, `net_table_defaults`, `net_field`, `make_groups`), 584–826 (`ro_bind`, `rw_bind`, `raw_packages`, `raw_nixpkgs`, `raw_network*`, `raw_gui`, `raw_forward*`, `same_forwards`, `raw_devices`, `raw_ssh_agent`, `raw_seccomp`, `raw_limits`, `raw_app`, `raw_with_app`, `a_header_secret`), 2382–2500, 3988–3992 (`pkg`, `get`), 7088–7200, 7363–7377 (`vhs`, `resolve_no_plugins`, `zoned`), 8328–8430, 8682–8810. These want one `src/config/tests/fixtures.rs` that every topic file does `use super::fixtures::*;` against.
- One sharp edge: tests.rs:60–78 defines a **shadowing shim** `fn validate_network` that deliberately shadows the glob-imported real function so 'the many bare 3-argument calls read as before'. If the suite becomes `mod tests { mod fixtures; mod groups; mod apps; … }`, that shim must live in `fixtures` and be glob-imported by exactly the files that want the shadowing — and any file that calls the real four-argument `validate_network` must not import it. Get this wrong and tests silently compile against the wrong function.
- Suggested shape, mirroring the source split one-for-one so a contributor changing `apps.rs` knows to open `tests/apps.rs`: `fixtures.rs`, `groups.rs`, `apps.rs`, `tools.rs`, `binds_env.rs`, `postures.rs`, `secrets.rs`, `plugins.rs`, `overrides.rs`, `invariants.rs` (the last for the cross-cutting `every_*_names_itself_in_its_refusal` family at 10342–10796, which deliberately spans every field and should not be filed under any one topic).

**A deliberate anti-DRY seam to respect.** `expand_bind_path`'s doc (mod.rs:4209–4220) ends with 'Do not merge the two behind one helper', about its intentional divergence from the resolver-plugin `allow_paths` expander over a literal `$` past the head. Whatever else moves, that function keeps its doc verbatim.

**A collapsible near-duplication, offered but not recommended.** The global-layer field reads in `resolve` (1806–1960: network/proc/notify/gui/forward/limits/seccomp/devices/fs/ssh_agent, each 'validate → assign → stamp origin') are structurally parallel to the global-app-layer reads in `resolve_app` (3402–3560), and the project halves (`resolve` 2190–2470 vs `resolve_app` 3640–3800) already share `Gate`. A `Gate`-shaped 'trusted-by-location layer' helper could collapse perhaps 120 lines. I would not do it: the two differ in the mode-inheritance parent they pass and in `T` vs `Option<T>` slots, and forcing them together would need a generic that obscures which of the two a reader is looking at — the exact readability the current duplication buys.

**No dead code found** in mod.rs. Every private item has at least one caller inside `config`; the closest thing to an orphan is `is_valid_uri_scheme` (5400), whose sole caller is `validate::validate_open` — reached only through `use super::*`, which is worth knowing before anyone 'tidies' it."

---

## `cli-net` — src/cli/net.rs (5168 lines)

**Verdict : **Découpage utile****

### Cartographie actuelle

`src/cli/net.rs` (5168 lines) is the whole `sbx net` verb family: the handlers, arg parsers, view models and pure presenters for `rules / groups / allow / unallow / deny / undeny / mute / unmute / pending / stats / logs / live`. Its own header (lines 1–12) says the cross-cutting plumbing (`persist_egress_rule`, `open_rule_write`, `session_pids_*`, `pending_session_context`, `format_log_time`, `net_mode_word`, `interval_seconds`) deliberately stays at the crate root and is reached via `crate::` — that seam is already clean and I am not touching it.

Code occupies 1–3525; `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` is 3526–5168 (1642 lines, ~32% of the file). Internally it is SIX independent subsystems plus three dispatchers and one shared helper, laid out in an order that no longer matches the subsystems:

1. Dispatchers — `net_cmd` 28–67, `net_pending` 69–83, `net_groups` 2253–2263.
2. PENDING / control-plane answering — `collect_pending` 85–103, `egress_rule_for` 105–124, `pending_port` 126–137, `net_pending_list` 139–216, `WatchArgs` 218–223, `parse_watch_args` 225–251, `net_pending_watch` 253–300, `PendingGroup` 530–540, `group_pending` 542–568, `render_pending` 570–643, `drain_sessions` 645–678, `net_pending_answer` 727–917, `net_pending_answer_all` 919–955, `collapse_hosts` 957–971, `render_drain` 973–1041, `write_unsupported_note` 1043–1079, and — stranded 2200 lines from its only caller (`net_pending_answer` at 817) — `net_pending_drain_and_save` 3274–3449. Tests 3711–3822, 4728–4825, 4969–5069.
3. LIVE (open flows) — `LiveArgs` 302–308, `parse_live_args` 310–334, `collect_flows` 336–353, `format_flow_age` 355–364, `render_live` 366–429, `flush_live_json` 431–464, `net_live` 466–528. Tests 3823–3929.
4. SHARED — `write_session_header_line` 680–703 and `write_session_header` 705–725, called from `render_pending` (617), `render_live` (411), `render_drain` (1025), `render_logs` (1756), `net_logs_follow` (1658), `render_inject` (3223). The only helper used by more than one subsystem.
5. STATS — `net_stats` 1081–1190, `render_stats` 1192–1248. Tests 5070–5168.
6. LOGS + capture — `LogView` 1250–1275 through `capture_json` 2079–2110 (an unbroken 861-line run). Tests 3930–4727 and 4826–4968.
7. RULES (list, config write, live-overlay inject, removal) — `net_rules` 2112–2251, `net_rules_manual` 2722–2815, `render_net_rules` 2817–2942, `net_add_rule` 2944–3050, `net_remove_rule` 3052–3102, `net_inject_session` 3104–3188, `render_inject` 3190–3272, `removal_words` 3451–3463, `persist_egress_removal` 3465–3524. Tests 3553–3663.
8. GROUPS — `net_groups_list` 2265–2349, `net_groups_export` 2351–2440, `keep_replaced_groups` 2442–2497, `render_replaced_group` 2499–2533, `net_groups_import` 2535–2643, `net_group_entry_issue` 2645–2654, `render_net_groups` 2656–2720. Tests 3530–3552 and 3664–3710.

Coupling between clusters 2/3/5/6/7/8 is essentially nil: I grepped every private symbol and found no call crossing a cluster boundary except the three dispatchers and `write_session_header{,_line}`. No shared mutable state, no state machine, no borrowed lifetimes crossing a boundary (`PendingGroup<'a>` and `capture_of<'a>` are internal to their own clusters). Every test also falls cleanly inside exactly one cluster; not one test straddles two.

### Justification

This is not one cohesive state machine — it is six near-independent verb subsystems concatenated, with a two-function shared surface. The evidence for the seam being real rather than cosmetic: (a) not a single private symbol is called across the six clusters; (b) the 1642-line test block partitions with zero straddling tests; (c) the current layout already fails to keep a cluster together — `net_pending_drain_and_save` (3274–3449) sits between the rule-write functions and 2200 lines from `net_pending_answer` (its only caller, at line 817), and `write_session_header*` (680–725) sits buried inside the pending block. The repo already has this shape elsewhere (`src/config/`, `src/sandbox/control/`, `src/allowlist/`), so the pattern needs no invention.

I stop short of "strongly recommended" because the cost is real and not zero: 12 items must widen from private to `pub(super)`, six new `use crate::{…}` headers must be derived exactly right (an over-copied import is an `unused_imports` warning, and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` is a hard gate), and the module header at lines 1–12 has to be rewritten into a map of the children while keeping the "plumbing stays at the crate root" paragraph that is still true. Nothing here is broken today; the win is navigation, review-diff locality, and `cargo test --lib cli::net::logs` granularity.

THE ONE HARD CONSTRAINT — verified, and it dictates the shape: two source-scanning guards read `src/cli` with a NON-recursive `std::fs::read_dir` filtered to `extension == "rs"` (`src/cli/mod.rs:968-975` in `dispatch_heads`, and `src/help.rs:3728-3735` in `dispatched_alias_pairs`). Nothing under a `src/cli/net/` directory is ever scanned. Consequences:
- `src/cli/mod.rs:1101-1116` asserts that every help path with child pages has a dispatcher head. `["net"]`, `["net","pending"]` (pages at `src/help.rs:2515,2536,2582`) and `["net","groups"]` (pages at `src/help.rs:2260,2275`) all qualify, so moving `net_cmd`, `net_pending` or `net_groups` into a child file fails `every_routed_subcommand_is_named_by_its_parents_page` with "has subcommand pages but the scan found no dispatcher for it".
- `src/help.rs:3678` `DISPATCHERS` hard-codes `("net.rs", "net_cmd", &["net"])`, and `body_lines` (`src/help.rs:3699-3712`) panics unless `fn net_cmd(`/`pub(crate) fn net_cmd(` starts at column 0 in a file literally named `net.rs`. Moving it also breaks the reverse check at `src/help.rs:3813-3820` for the declared alias `(&["net"], "log", "logs")` (`src/help.rs:3108`, dispatched at `src/cli/net.rs:52`).

So the split MUST be `src/cli/net.rs` (kept as the module root, Rust-2018-style, edition is 2024 per `Cargo.toml:4`) plus a `src/cli/net/` directory — NOT `src/cli/net/mod.rs`. `net.rs` retains the three dispatchers and the shared session-header writer. Declare children as bare `mod pending;` etc. with NO outer `///` on the `mod` line: `mise.toml:95-98` documents that a doc comment on a `mod` declaration merges with the child's own header and moves resolution into the declaring module's scope, which `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc --no-deps --document-private-items` (`mise.toml:102`) then flags. `src/config/mod.rs:17-27` and `src/sandbox/control/mod.rs:35-36` are the in-repo precedent for exactly that.

Cyclic-dependency risk is nil in the Rust sense (module cycles compile), and the design has none anyway: it is a strict tree. Children reach `write_session_header{,_line}` via `use super::…` — legal without any visibility change, because a private item is visible in its defining module AND all descendants. Only the 12 dispatched entry points widen, to `pub(super)`.

### Découpage proposé

#### `src/cli/net.rs (KEPT — the module root, rewritten, not a new file)` (~160 lignes)

**Rôle.** The verb tree and the one line every listing shares. Holds the three dispatchers the source-scanning guards require to live in a file named net.rs directly under src/cli, plus the session-header writer that is the only helper more than one subsystem uses.

**Ce qui bouge.** STAYS: module header 1–12 (rewritten into a map of the six children; keep its final paragraph about crate-root plumbing, which is still accurate); `net_cmd` 28–67; `net_pending` 69–83; `net_groups` 2253–2263; `write_session_header_line` 680–703; `write_session_header` 705–725. ADDS: `mod groups; mod live; mod logs; mod pending; mod rules; mod stats;` with no outer doc comments. Trimmed `use` set: `std::ffi::OsString`, `std::path::PathBuf`, `std::process::ExitCode`, `crate::{config, diag, help, sandbox, style}`. No `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` — nothing left here has a direct test (the header writer is asserted through `render_pending`/`render_live`/`render_drain`/`render_logs`).

**Risque.** The critical file. `body_lines` (src/help.rs:3699) requires `pub(crate) fn net_cmd(` to remain at column 0 and to close on a `}` at column 0 — keep it first and unwrapped. `dispatch_heads` (src/cli/mod.rs:966) requires all three dispatchers to keep the `match args.first().and_then(|a| a.to_str())` idiom (lines 34, 76, 2258) and to keep the `sbx <path>` synopsis in their doc comments, or it panics with "this dispatcher names no command path". Intra-doc link to fix: line 2256 `[`net_groups_list`]` becomes `[`groups::net_groups_list`]`. Line 705's `[`write_session_header_line`]` needs no change (target stays beside it). The 12 child entry points must be imported here or called path-qualified.

#### `src/cli/net/pending.rs` (~1235 lignes)

**Rôle.** The `ask`-posture control plane: listing, watching, answering one parked request by id, bulk-draining, and the drain-and-save path — plus their presenters. Everything that writes an irreversible answer to a live session lives in one file.

**Ce qui bouge.** `collect_pending` 85–103, `egress_rule_for` 105–124, `pending_port` 126–137, `net_pending_list` 139–216, `WatchArgs` 218–223, `parse_watch_args` 225–251, `net_pending_watch` 253–300, `PendingGroup` 530–540, `group_pending` 542–568, `render_pending` 570–643, `drain_sessions` 645–678, `net_pending_answer` 727–917, `net_pending_answer_all` 919–955, `collapse_hosts` 957–971, `render_drain` 973–1041, `write_unsupported_note` 1043–1079, `net_pending_drain_and_save` 3274–3449. Tests move with them: 3711–3822 (render_pending, parse_watch_args ×2), 4728–4825 (egress_rule_for, and the stand-in-socket drive of `net_pending_answer`), 4969–5069 (render_drain, collapse_hosts).

**Risque.** Visibility: `net_pending_list`, `net_pending_watch`, `net_pending_answer` become `pub(super)` (called from `net_pending`, which stays in net.rs). Imports needed: `std::{ffi::OsString, io::IsTerminal, path::{Path,PathBuf}, process::ExitCode, time::Duration}`, `crate::{egress_data_dir, egress_write_target, interval_seconds, pending_session_context, persist_egress_rule, precheck_local_save, session_app_of, session_pids_for_app, session_pids_for_project, split_scope}`, `crate::{config, diag, help, sandbox, style}`, `use super::write_session_header`. Do NOT import `write_session_header_line` — unused here, and `-D warnings` fails on it. Rustdoc: none of this cluster's `[`…`]` links leave it; the two `[`egress_rule_for`]` references (lines 851, 3383) are `//` comments, not rustdoc, and land in this same file. The stand-in-socket test at 4749 uses `crate::testutil::{EnvVar, TmpDir, env_lock}` and `std::os::unix::net::UnixListener` — those `use` lines are inside the test fn and move verbatim.

#### `src/cli/net/logs.rs` (~890 lignes)

**Rôle.** `sbx net logs` — the view model (`LogView`), the arg parser, the reader and filters, the one-shot render, the `--follow` stream, the per-event line, the captured-traffic block, and both JSON emitters. One file, because every function here branches on `LogView` and the event path and the capture path interleave inside `render_logs` and `net_logs_follow`.

**Ce qui bouge.** The unbroken run 1250–2110: `LogView` 1250–1275, `impl LogView` 1277–1284, `parse_log_args` 1286–1361, `collect_logs` 1363–1388, `event_passes_filters` 1390–1395, `filtered_log_events` 1397–1418, `display_log_path` 1420–1429, `snapshot_evicted` 1431–1444, `net_logs` 1446–1508, `net_logs_follow` 1510–1681, `flush_stream` 1683–1694, `verdict_color` 1696–1705, `status_color` 1707–1715, `render_logs` 1717–1803, `render_log_line` 1805–1882, `render_sightings` 1884–1907, `capture_of` 1909–1919, `render_capture` 1921–1974, `render_bytes` 1976–2005, `is_mostly_text` 2007–2021, `log_event_json` 2023–2077, `capture_json` 2079–2110. Declares `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` pointing at the file below.

**Risque.** Visibility: only `net_logs` becomes `pub(super)`. Imports: `std::{ffi::OsString, io::IsTerminal, path::{Path,PathBuf}, process::ExitCode}`, `crate::{egress_data_dir, format_log_time, pending_session_context, session_pids_for_app}`, `crate::{diag, help, sandbox, style}`, `use super::{write_session_header, write_session_header_line}` (both are used: 1756 and 1658). THE ONE CROSS-MODULE RUSTDOC FIX IN THE WHOLE SPLIT: line 1365, inside `collect_logs`'s doc, says "the log's analogue of [`collect_pending`]" — retarget to `[`super::pending::collect_pending`]`. It resolves under `--document-private-items` and does not trip `private_intra_doc_links` because `collect_logs` is itself private. The other four links in this cluster (1369 `[`snapshot_evicted`]`, 1372 `[`filtered_log_events`]`, 1400 and 1436 `[`collect_logs`]`) stay intra-file and need no edit.

#### `src/cli/net/logs/tests.rs` (~950 lignes)

**Rôle.** The log/capture test block, split out purely for size — 941 lines of tests would otherwise put logs.rs at ~1800. Uses the same `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` file form as src/config/mod.rs:5840 → src/config/tests.rs.

**Ce qui bouge.** 3930–4727 (the `log_event` and `log_capture` fixtures plus every capture, parse, filter, status, proto, ws, render and eviction test) and 4826–4968 (the two muted-ring tests). Body only — the `mod tests {` wrapper at 3527 and its closing brace are replaced by the file itself; `use super::*;` becomes the file's first line.

**Risque.** Lowest-value item in the proposal — skip it if you prefer inline tests everywhere, and accept an ~1800-line logs.rs. Note the fixtures `log_event` (3930–3958) and `log_capture` (4036–4056) are used by tests on both sides of the 4728–4825 pending block, so they must move as a pair with the whole test body — do not try to split this file further along the capture/event line, because `asking_for_traffic_from_a_session_that_does_not_capture_says_so` (4057–4099) calls `render_logs` AND `log_capture`, and separating them would force a test fixture to cross a module boundary. `src/docs_coverage.rs:996-999` skips any file named `tests.rs` whole, so nothing here needs doc comments. No visibility changes.

Rustdoc: line 3466's `[`persist_egress_rule`]` keeps resolving because this module imports it from the crate root. Line 3455's "Its `[proc]` twin" is a code span, not a link — safe. OUTSIDE EDIT REQUIRED: `src/cli/proc.rs:125` says "Its egress twin is `cli::net::removal_words`" — a plain code span, so it will not fail rustdoc, but it becomes a stale pointer; update it to `cli::net::rules::removal_words`, since CLAUDE.md requires a renamed/moved symbol to carry its doc references with it.

No rustdoc links leave this cluster once `net_groups`'s `[`net_groups_list`]` (line 2256, staying in net.rs) is requalified to `[`groups::net_groups_list`]`. `keep_replaced_groups`'s doc (2442-2455) mentions `cli::bundle::keep_replaced_bundles` in a code span, not a link — safe.

#### `src/cli/net/live.rs` (~355 lignes)

**Rôle.** `sbx net live` — the top-style view of the egress tunnels open right now: arg parsing, the flow gather, the age formatter, the frame presenter and the NDJSON snapshot emitter.

Tests 3823–3929 (`parse_live_args_defaults_and_overrides`, `parse_live_args_rejects_bad_input`, `format_flow_age_is_compact`, `render_live_groups_open_flows_by_session`).

**Risque.** Visibility: `net_live` becomes `pub(super)`. Imports: `std::{ffi::OsString, io::IsTerminal, path::{Path,PathBuf}, process::ExitCode, time::Duration}`, `crate::{egress_data_dir, interval_seconds, pending_session_context, session_pids_for_app}`, `crate::{diag, help, sandbox, style}`, `use super::write_session_header` (used at 411). No rustdoc links in or out. Note `render_live` takes `now_ms` from the caller so its test is deterministic — that seam is preserved verbatim.

### Autres observations

Four concrete cleanups found while mapping, all independent of the split (do them before or after, not during — a move-only commit is far easier to review):

1.
