DUPLICATED INTERVAL PARSER. `src/cli/net.rs:1309-1321` (inside `parse_log_args`) re-implements `crate::interval_seconds` (`src/main.rs:251-263`) byte-for-byte, including all three error strings: "`--interval` needs a value in seconds", "invalid interval `{}` — expected a whole number of seconds", "interval must be at least 1 second". The file already imports `interval_seconds` and uses it correctly twice — `parse_watch_args` at line 234 and `parse_live_args` at line 320. Replace the whole arm with `Some("-i") | Some("--interval") => v.interval_secs = interval_seconds(it.next())?,`. 13 lines to 1, with no behaviour change and no test change (`parse_log_args_reads_every_flag_and_rejects_bad_input` at 4253 asserts those exact messages and keeps passing).

2. THREE NEAR-IDENTICAL GATHER FUNCTIONS. `collect_pending` (85–103), `collect_flows` (336–353) and `collect_logs` (1363–1388) are the same 15-line shape: call `sandbox::control::{list_all|flows_all|log_all}`, `retain` on `session_pids_for_app` when `app` is set, then pair with `pending_session_context`. They differ only in the control call and, for `collect_logs`, two extra bool args. A generic `fn scoped<T>(data_dir: &Path, app: Option<&str>, list: impl FnOnce(&Path) -> Vec<T>, pid_of: impl Fn(&T) -> u32) -> (Vec<T>, Vec<(u32, PathBuf, String)>)` would collapse them. I would NOT put it in the crate root — it is a `cli::net` concern; it belongs in `src/cli/net.rs` beside `write_session_header`. Worth doing only if you take the split, because otherwise the three stay adjacent-ish and the abstraction buys little. Note each carries a distinct, load-bearing doc comment (`collect_logs`'s 1363–1372 explains why the muted ring is always folded in) — that prose must survive on the call sites, not be collapsed into the generic.

3. SEVEN COPIES OF THE CWD BLOCK. `src/cli/net.rs:1119-1123, 2169-2175, 2562-2568, 3006-3011, 3027-3032, 3078-3083, 3308-3313` each hand-roll `match std::env::current_dir() { Ok(d) => d, Err(e) => { diag::error(&format!("sbx: cannot read the current directory: {e}")); return ExitCode::FAILURE; } }`. `crate::config_cwd()` (`src/main.rs:266-272`) already does this and is used 9 times in `cli/app.rs`, `cli/config.rs` and `cli/upgrade.rs`. CAVEAT that makes this not a pure win: `config_cwd` uses a bare `eprintln!`, while `diag::error` (`src/diag.rs`) routes through `highlight(msg, &Palette::for_stream(…))`, so backticked spans in the message get colored on a TTY. The message text is identical, but switching would silently drop that highlighting on seven paths. Either accept that, or change `config_cwd` to call `diag::error` (which would change the 9 existing call sites' output too, so it needs its own look).

4. AN ARGUMENT-PARSING FAMILY THAT NEVER FORMED. `net_pending_list` (144–170), `net_stats` (1090–1109) and `net_rules` (2123–2167) each hand-roll the same `--app`/`-a` + `--json` walk with slightly different error text ("sbx: `--app` needs an app name" vs "sbx: net stats: `--app` needs an app name" vs "sbx: net rules: `--app` needs an app name"), while `parse_watch_args`, `parse_live_args` and `parse_log_args` are pure, testable parsers returning `Result<T, String>`. The three inline walkers are the odd ones out and are the only arg paths in the file with no direct unit test. I am flagging this rather than proposing it: converting them means choosing one error-prefix spelling, which is user-visible text and possibly asserted in `tests/`. It is a deliberate follow-up, not part of a mechanical split.

Nothing in this file is dead. I checked every private symbol; all 68 have at least one non-test caller.

---

## `cli-config` — src/cli/config.rs (5004 lines)

**Verdict : **Découpage utile****

### Cartographie actuelle

`/home/user/ops-cli/src/cli/config.rs` (5004 lines) is the whole `sbx config` verb family: the dispatcher, the six management verbs, and the two rendering documents (`sbx config show` and `sbx config show --app <name>`). Code runs to line 3105; `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` is 3107–5004 (1897 lines, ~38% of the file). Integration coverage lives separately in `/home/user/ops-cli/tests/config.rs` (5481 lines).

Internal sections, verified by grep/sed:

1. Dispatch + `show` handlers — 22–237: `config_cmd` (22–60), `set_show_source` (62–87), `config_show` (89–204), `config_show_app` (206–237). The only `diag::`-using code above line 2349 (last hit is 228).
2. Provenance/line formatters shared by both documents — 240–422: `provenance_tag` (240–248), `provenance_parts` (250–268), `app_provenance_tag` (270–276), `app_provenance_parts` (278–293), `opt_provenance_tag` (295–302), `bind_mode_tag` (304–312), `struct NetTransport` (314–324), `write_net_transport` (326–422).
3. Baseline `sbx config show` document — 424–1702: 22 `*_section` functions (`env_section` 424 … `apps_section` 1292–1642) plus `render_config` (1644–1702), whose body is the ordered sequence of section calls and whose comment at 1655–1657 declares that order load-bearing. `network_section` (1044–1187) and `apps_section` (1292–1642) are the two big ones.
4. Shared network-posture preamble — `write_net_posture_head` (929–1042), documented at 932–939 as deliberately written once because `render_config` and `render_app_detail` state the same posture and differ only in the rule listing.
5. Per-app detail document — 1704–2253: `posture_shown` (1704–1726), `render_app_detail` (1728–2217, 490 lines), `collection_list` (2219–2241), `package_name_tag` (2243–2253).
6. Shared entry formatters — 2255–2347: `service_line` (2255–2276), `package_line` (2278–2311), `channel_text` (2313–2332), `channel_origin_kind` (2334–2347). Each is called from both documents (`service_line` at 506/1392/2187; `package_line` at 522/1404/2154; `channel_text` at 613/614/2162).
7. Key-editing verbs — 2349–3105: `app_prefixed_key` (2349–2365), `config_usage` (2367–2374), `config_get` (2376–2424), `reject_app` (2426–2438), `scope_is_gated` (2440–2450), `admit_config_write` (2452–2533), `resolve_key_target` (2535–2587), `config_set` (2589–2649), `enum ListEdit` (2651–2658), `config_list_edit` (2660–2736), `config_unset` (2738–2789), `config_path_cmd` (2791–2840), `render_resolution_layers` (2842–2877), `config_edit` (2879–3023), `report_write_trust` (3025–3096), `is_security_key` (3098–3105).

The decisive structural fact: sections 2–6 (render) and section 7 (edit) have **zero call edges in either direction**, and their imports are disjoint. `trust::` appears only at 2486–3058; `PathBuf` only at 2554; the `crate::cli::confirm::{…}` renderers only at 2631–3061; `ScopeArgs`/`split_scope`/`config_cwd` only at 2382–2909. Conversely `crate::allowlist::DEFAULT_*` appears only at 380–418, `net_mode_word` only at 978/1454/1486, `short_rev` only at 1418/2298/2327. The two halves meet only in `config_cmd`'s match arms and in the shared `use` block.

### Justification

The file contains two programs that do not talk to each other, and the seam is provable rather than aesthetic: no call edge crosses it (verified by grepping every function name against the whole file), and the import sets are disjoint (`trust::` and `cli::confirm::*` live entirely below 2349; `allowlist::DEFAULT_*`, `net_mode_word` and `short_rev` entirely above it). The `#[cfg(test)]` module partitions along the same line with no shared fixture: `sample_config_view()` (3238–3349) is called only by `render_config` tests (3354–4147), and the four edit-verb tests (3112–3236, 3946–3962) share nothing with them. After the split three of the five files contain no `diag::`, no `std::process`, no filesystem call — they are pure `&View -> String` presenters, which is exactly what `render_config`'s own doc at 1644–1647 claims ("a pure presenter over `config::view` … a future front-end can render the same model differently"). The split makes a stated design property physical instead of conventional.

The cost is small and enumerable: ~19 items go from private to `pub(super)` (crate-invisible outside `cli::config`), two rustdoc links need a `use crate::config;` carried into the new files, and one prose reference in `src/main.rs:601` needs its path updated. `src/cli/mod.rs:8` needs no edit, because the crate already uses `mod.rs`-style directory modules (`src/config/`, `src/sandbox/`, `src/plugins/`, `src/allowlist/`, `src/sandbox/control/`).

Why "worthwhile" and not "strongly recommended": the split does not make the file small. `render.rs` still lands at ~2760 lines (~2160 after the test-fixture cleanup in `other_observations`), because the baseline document genuinely is one cohesive thing — `render_config` (1644–1702) is a single ordered list of section calls and the code says at 1655–1657 that the order is load-bearing, so cutting the sections into "collections" vs "whole-view" groups would put the document's structure in two places to save nothing. That residual is the honest floor. If appetite is limited, take **only the render/edit cut** (modules 1+2 below merged as `mod.rs`, module 5 as `edit.rs`): that is one file move, ~9 visibility changes, zero rustdoc risk, and it removes 908 lines and the entire `trust`/`confirm`/`ScopeArgs` import surface from the render side.

### Découpage proposé

#### `src/cli/config/mod.rs` (~265 lignes)

**Rôle.** Family front door: the verb dispatcher, the `config show` / `config show --app` handlers (the only two consumers of the render modules), and the family-wide usage helper. Keeps `src/cli/mod.rs:8` (`pub(crate) mod config;`) untouched, matching the crate's existing mod.rs-style directories (src/config/, src/sandbox/, src/plugins/, src/allowlist/).

**Ce qui bouge.** Stays/moves here: the `//!` module doc (1–8, rewritten for the new layout), `config_cmd` (22–60), `set_show_source` (62–87), `config_show` (89–204), `config_show_app` (206–237), and `config_usage` (2367–2374) hoisted up from the edit half. Adds `mod app_detail; mod edit; mod format; mod render;`. Imports narrow to `std::ffi::OsString`, `std::io::IsTerminal`, `std::path::Path`, `std::process::ExitCode`, `crate::{config, diag, help, style}`. Inline test module keeps `set_show_source_rejects_a_conflicting_second_flag` (3964–3972), which needs `use crate::config;` for `config::Source` — already present.

**Risque.** `config_usage` must become `pub(super)` (edit.rs calls it at 15 sites: 2391, 2395, 2434, 2570, 2582, 2604, 2608, 2683, 2687, 2752, 2756, 2807, 2814, 2900, 2907). `render::render_config` and `app_detail::render_app_detail` must become `pub(super)` for the calls at 197 and 236, and the seven edit verbs listed in edit.rs below. No rustdoc link in 22–237 points at a moved symbol. No cycle: this is the parent module; edit.rs referencing `super::config_usage` is an ordinary `super::` hop, and Rust permits mutual intra-crate module references regardless.

#### `src/cli/config/format.rs` (~425 lignes)

**Rôle.** The line-level formatters both documents share — the provenance hue table, the entry lines (`service:`, `packages:`, channels, bind mode), and the network-posture preamble. Its existence is already asserted by the code: the doc at 250–253 calls `provenance_parts` "the one place the level→hue mapping lives, so … [the two uses] cannot drift", and 932–939 says `write_net_posture_head` is "written once because both views state the same posture". This module is that statement made structural.

**Ce qui bouge.** `provenance_tag` (240–248), `provenance_parts` (250–268), `app_provenance_tag` (270–276), `app_provenance_parts` (278–293), `opt_provenance_tag` (295–302), `bind_mode_tag` (304–312), `struct NetTransport` (314–324), `write_net_transport` (326–422), `write_net_posture_head` (929–1042), `service_line` (2255–2276), `package_line` (2278–2311), `channel_text` (2313–2332), `channel_origin_kind` (2334–2347). Test `channel_origin_kind_tracks_the_real_store_origin_labels` (3574–3592) moves here and carries `use crate::store;` (currently line 3110) with it. Imports: `crate::{config, style}`, `crate::{net_mode_word, short_rev}` (short_rev is used at 2298 and 2327; net_mode_word at 978 inside write_net_posture_head), `crate::allowlist::DEFAULT_*` stays as the fully-qualified `crate::allowlist::…` it already is (380, 394, 406, 418).

**Risque.** `pub(super)` needed on: `provenance_tag`, `provenance_parts`, `opt_provenance_tag`, `app_provenance_tag`, `app_provenance_parts`, `bind_mode_tag`, `write_net_posture_head`, `service_line`, `package_line`, `channel_text` (10 items). `NetTransport`, `write_net_transport` and `channel_origin_kind` stay private — their only callers (1027, 2322) and their only test move here with them. Rustdoc: the two intra-doc links at 251 (`[`provenance_tag`]`) and 280 (`[`provenance_parts`]`) have both endpoints inside this file, so they keep resolving unchanged — this is the main reason the six provenance helpers must move together rather than be split render/app-detail. Clippy risk is only `unused_imports` if the `use` block is copied wholesale rather than trimmed; `-D warnings` will catch it. No cycle (leaf module: depends on crate root and `config::view` only).

#### `src/cli/config/render.rs` (~2765 lignes)

**Rôle.** The baseline `sbx config show` document: 22 section renderers plus `render_config`, which is the ordered sequence of section calls. Pure — after the move this file contains no `diag::`, no filesystem call and no `ExitCode`.

**Ce qui bouge.** `env_section` (424–445), `binds_section` (447–467), `open_section` (469–492), `service_section` (494–509), `packages_section` (511–526), `mise_section` (528–551), `tools_section` (553–604), `channels_section` (606–616), `proc_section` (618–649), `notify_section` (651–691), `timezone_section` (693–708), `desktop_sections` (710–768), `forward_section` (770–792), `limits_section` (794–820), `grants_section` (822–890), `brokers_section` (892–927), `network_section` (1044–1187), `secrets_section` (1189–1209), `redact_section` (1211–1228), `plugins_section` (1230–1261), `tasks_section` (1263–1290), `apps_section` (1292–1642), `render_config` (1644–1702). Tests: 3238–3572 (including the `sample_config_view` fixture at 3238–3349), 3594–3813, and 3974–5003 — every `render_config`-driven test, verified against the full call-site list (3447 … 4986).

**Risque.** Only `render_config` needs `pub(super)`; the 22 sections stay private because their tests stay inline in this file (a child `mod tests` sees its parent's private items — this is why the tests should NOT be split out to a sibling `render_tests.rs` in the style of src/config/tests.rs, which would force all 22 sections public). Rustdoc: line 1644 contains ``[`config::view`]`` — it resolves through the file-level `use crate::config;`, so that import MUST be re-declared here or `mise run rustdoc` fails with a broken intra-doc link. The link at 1232 (``[`secrets_section`]`` in `plugins_section`'s doc) has both endpoints in this file and is safe. Needs `use super::format::{provenance_tag, provenance_parts, opt_provenance_tag, bind_mode_tag, write_net_posture_head, service_line, package_line, channel_text};` and `crate::{net_mode_word, short_rev}` (978/1454/1486 and 1418). This file remains the largest piece — see the rationale; the fixture cleanup in other_observations takes it to ~2160. No cycle.

#### `src/cli/config/app_detail.rs` (~695 lignes)

**Rôle.** The `sbx config show --app <name>` document: one app's effective configuration with per-field provenance and the default-posture fold. Also pure.

**Ce qui bouge.** `posture_shown` (1704–1726), `render_app_detail` (1728–2217), `collection_list` (2219–2241), `package_name_tag` (2243–2253). Test `render_app_detail_shows_effective_values_tagged_inherited_or_app_set` (3815–3944), which builds the only `AppDetailView` literal in the file (3819) and is the sole caller of `render_app_detail` in tests (3912, 3937).

**Risque.** Only `render_app_detail` needs `pub(super)` (called at 236). `posture_shown`, `collection_list` and `package_name_tag` stay private — their callers (1846–2028, 2111/2129/2142/2193, 2146) all live in this file. Rustdoc: line 1735 contains ``[`config::view::AppDetailView`]``, so `use crate::config;` must be re-declared here — same failure mode as render.rs. The link at 1734 (``[`posture_shown`]``) is intra-file and safe. Needs `use super::format::{app_provenance_tag, app_provenance_parts, write_net_posture_head, bind_mode_tag, package_line, service_line, channel_text};`. No dependency on render.rs in either direction — verified: nothing in 1704–2253 calls a `*_section` function, and nothing in 424–1702 calls into this set. No cycle.

#### `src/cli/config/edit.rs` (~910 lignes)

**Rôle.** The key-editing verbs and the trust admission behind them: `get`, `set`, `add`, `rm`, `unset`, `path`, `edit`. This is the half that touches the filesystem, the trust store and `$EDITOR`; isolating it puts every write-side security decision (`scope_is_gated`, `admit_config_write`, `report_write_trust`, `is_security_key`) in one 900-line file with no rendering code around it.

**Ce qui bouge.** `app_prefixed_key` (2349–2365) and the whole run 2376–3105: `config_get`, `reject_app`, `scope_is_gated`, `admit_config_write`, `resolve_key_target`, `config_set`, `enum ListEdit`, `config_list_edit`, `config_unset`, `config_path_cmd`, `render_resolution_layers`, `config_edit`, `report_write_trust`, `is_security_key`. (`config_usage`, 2367–2374, goes to mod.rs instead — it is family-wide, not edit-specific.) Tests: `is_security_key_treats_only_the_env_table_as_free` (3112–3125), `resolution_layers_render_marks_presence_and_stays_plain_uncolored` (3127–3162), `resolution_layers_render_handles_a_missing_config_directory` (3164–3178), `resolve_key_target_routes_by_scope_and_app` (3180–3236), `app_prefixed_key_rewrites_a_simple_name_and_rejects_a_dotted_one` (3946–3962). Takes the imports that appear nowhere else: `crate::cli::confirm::{render_config_same_value, render_config_unchanged, render_config_write, render_list_edit, render_list_unchanged, render_trusted_whole_file}` (15–18), `crate::{ScopeArgs, config_cwd, split_scope}` (19), `crate::trust`, `std::path::{Path, PathBuf}`.

**Risque.** `pub(super)` needed on the seven dispatch targets: `config_get`, `config_set`, `config_list_edit`, `config_unset`, `config_path_cmd`, `config_edit`, and `enum ListEdit` (mod.rs constructs `ListEdit::Add`/`ListEdit::Remove` at 34–35, so the enum and its variants must be reachable — `pub(super) enum ListEdit` suffices, variants inherit). Everything else stays private. Rustdoc: line 2457 (``[`report_write_trust`]``) and 2888 (``[`scope_is_gated`]``) have both endpoints in this file; line 2461 (``[`crate::local_save_permitted`]``) is an absolute path and unaffected; line 2937's ``[`admit_config_write`]`` is inside a `//` body comment, not rustdoc. So this module has zero rustdoc breakage. One external prose reference must be updated by hand: `src/main.rs:601` reads "(see `cli::config::admit_config_write`)" — a code span, so rustdoc will not fail, but CLAUDE.md's "carry its doc references with it" rule requires it become `cli::config::edit::admit_config_write`. `crate::testutil::TmpDir` is referenced by full path at 3130, so no import follows it. No cycle: the only outward edge is `super::config_usage`.

### Autres observations

**The net.rs comparison — the most valuable finding.** `src/cli/net.rs` (5168 lines) and `src/cli/config.rs` (5004) are the same size and both flat, so the interesting divergence is internal, and it is in argument parsing.

net.rs has established a pattern for option-heavy verbs: a named args struct plus a pure parser returning `Result<T, String>`, with the handler mapping the error once. Three instances: `struct WatchArgs` (220–224) + `parse_watch_args` (228–250), `struct LiveArgs` (304–312) + `parse_live_args` (313–338), `struct LogView` (1252–1276) + `impl LogView` (1278–1284) + `parse_log_args` (1290–1372). The rationale is stated in the code — `parse_watch_args`'s doc at 225–227 and `parse_log_args`'s at 1286–1289 both read "Pure (no I/O), so every reject path is unit-testable". The payoff is visible in the tests: `parse_watch_args_defaults_and_overrides` / `_rejects_bad_input` (3783–3822), `parse_live_args_*` (3823–3863), `parse_log_args_reads_every_flag_and_rejects_bad_input` (4253–4328). The handler is then trivial — `net_logs` (1450–1457) is six lines of parse-or-exit.

config.rs has adopted none of it. `config_show` (89–204) carries six flags and two conflict rules and parses them inline, returning `ExitCode` from inside the loop, so no reject path is reachable from a unit test. The consequence is measurable: the `--app` + source conflict at 141–158 has **no test anywhere** — `grep -rn "does not combine with" tests/ src/` returns exactly one hit, the source line itself. Only `set_show_source` (62–87) was extracted, and only because someone wanted to test it (3964–3972).

Be honest about the limit of this comparison: net.rs is not uniform either — `net_pending_list` (144–166), `net_stats` (1086–1110) and `net_rules` (2116–2168) still inline-parse exactly like `config_show`. So the finding is not "net is clean, config is dirty"; it is that net has a house pattern for its heaviest verbs and config has never taken it up, despite `config show` being precisely that shape.

Concrete follow-on, independent of the split and cheap: add `struct ShowArgs { json, details, app, source }` + `fn parse_show_args(args: &[OsString]) -> Result<ShowArgs, String>` to the new `src/cli/config/mod.rs`, absorbing `set_show_source` as its private helper. `config_show` collapses to ~25 lines, and the two conflict rules become unit-testable without a cwd.

**A second net.rs parallel, on the write side.** net routes every rule write through `open_rule_write` (src/main.rs:737–798) returning a `RuleWrite` struct (src/main.rs:693–712) — one admission, one place. config repeats the identical six-step preamble in three verbs: `config_set` (2594–2622), `config_list_edit` (2673–2703), `config_unset` (2742–2770), each doing `split_scope` → positional-count check → `config_cwd` → `resolve_key_target` → `trust::default_store_dir` → `admit_config_write`, then repeating the outcome-match/palette/println/`report_write_trust` tail (2626–2646, 2705–2731, 2772–2786). ~25 lines × 3 collapsible into one `open_key_write(verb, args, n_positionals) -> Result<KeyWrite, ExitCode>` in edit.rs, deliberately named to echo `open_rule_write`.

**Do NOT unify the two trust gates.** They look alike and are deliberately different, and both differences are documented. `scope_is_gated` (2440–2450) gates every scope except `Global`, including `Scope::File`; net's `open_rule_write` (src/main.rs:744–750) *refuses* `Scope::File` outright and gates only `Scope::Local`. `admit_config_write` (2452–2533) consults `crate::local_save_permitted` only when `--trust` was passed; `open_rule_write` (src/main.rs:762–772) consults it on every write. The reasons are written out at 2452–2470 and src/main.rs:590–612. A proposal to merge them would dissolve a real seam.

**`config_usage` is bypassed by the verb that most needs it.** `config_usage` (2367–2374) emits `diag::error("sbx: usage: {synopsis}")` then `ExitCode::from(2)`, and is used by all seven other verbs (15 call sites). `config_show`, defined 2200 lines earlier, open-codes the identical two statements four times: 76–80, 105–110, 135–140, 152–157. The text is byte-identical, so replacing each with `return config_usage("show");` (after the existing specific `diag::error`) is behaviour-preserving. This is the one-line justification for hoisting `config_usage` into `mod.rs` rather than leaving it in `edit.rs`.

**~600 lines of duplicated test fixtures.** `sample_config_view()` (3238–3349) is a 110-line `ConfigView` literal, and five tests already use the right pattern — build it, then mutate the two or three fields under test (3354, 3374, 3477, 3496, 3603). Six other tests instead re-inline the entire literal: 4184–~4300, 4327–~4425, 4493–~4560, 4584–~4685, 4719–~4820, 4854–~4970. Diffing 3240–3349 against 4327–4440 shows they differ in about eight fields (`env`, `binds`, `packages`, `mise`, the nixpkgs channel, `network`/`network_origin`, `apps`) and are byte-identical in the other ~60. Rewriting them as `let mut view = sample_config_view(); view.apps = vec![…]; view.network = NetworkView::Shared; view.network_origin = ProvenanceView::Default;` removes roughly 600 lines and takes `render.rs` from ~2760 to ~2160. Note `ConfigView` (src/config/view.rs:44) derives only `Serialize`, so a `..Default::default()` shorthand is not available without changing the view model — the mutate-the-fixture route needs no change to `src/config/view.rs` and is the one to take. This cleanup is worth doing *before* the split, since it shrinks the piece that stays largest.

**Guards that are not at risk.** `src/docs_coverage.rs` reads `docs-site/docs/guide` (its `guide()` helper, lines 24–26) and the help table; it never looks at source module paths, so the split cannot fail it. Completion (`src/cli/completion.rs`, `tests/completion.rs`) derives from `src/help.rs` pages and the CLI surface is unchanged. `tests/config.rs` (5481 lines) drives the binary, not the module, so it pins the behaviour through the whole refactor — take the split in one commit and run that file first.

---

## `binds` — src/sandbox/binds.rs (4612 lines), src/sandbox/fsmask.rs, src/sandbox/fhs.rs

**Verdict : **Découpage utile****

### Cartographie actuelle

`src/sandbox/binds.rs` (4,612 lines) turns already-resolved host paths into a concrete `SandboxSpec` — the ordered mount plan, the cage environment, a synthetic identity, and the host-side runtime tree those mount sources live in. `fsmask.rs` (1,062) realises the `[fs]` deny/readonly table as decoy binds; `fhs.rs` (628) provisions the hermetic userland out of sbx's own store into a `Userland`.

The headline number is misleading. binds.rs is 1,706 lines of production code (of which 820 are comment lines and 86 blank — roughly 800 lines of actual code) followed by 2,906 lines of inline tests: `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` at 1707-3521 (1,815 lines, 57 tests) and `#[cfg(test)] mod smoke` at 3523-4612 (1,090 lines, 6 live-bwrap tests). 63% of the file is test code in two inline modules.

Internal sections of binds.rs, verified:
- 1-17 module header, including the integrity note ("the synthetic /etc must live outside every read-write bind").
- 23-132 in-cage FHS destination constants: `SANDBOX_HOME`(25), `SANDBOX_SHELL`(30), `LOADER_DEST`(36), `SANDBOX_BASH`(47), `SANDBOX_ENV`(55), `XDG_OPEN_INCAGE`(66), `OPEN_ROUTER_DIR`(95), `OPEN_ROUTER_INCAGE`(100), `SBX_INCAGE_DIR`(104), `OPT_DIR`(108), `CAGE_ZONEINFO`(112), `CAGE_LOCALTIME`(118), `DEFAULT_ZONE`(125), `SSH_CONFIG_INCAGE`(132).
- 135-340 the assembler's data types: `Userland`(145), `ExtraBind`(205), `NixMount`(224), `Overlay`(241), `ProjectRuntime`(265), `Identity`(281), `SandboxPaths`(287-340).
- 342-366 `assemble` — the pure keystone: `cage_mounts` + `cage_env` + `SandboxSpec::new`.
- 368-707 `cage_mounts` — the ordered mount plan, 340 lines, one function. This is the "everything the cage sees is declared in one place" property, and the doc at 355-366 states that bwrap argv order is the invariant.
- 709-743 `home_mountpoint_pins`.
- 745-859 `cage_env` — PATH construction and the structural environment.
- 861-901 `STRUCTURAL_DESTS`, kept in lockstep with `cage_mounts` by the test at 1845.
- 903-1000 nesting diagnostics: `Nesting`(904), `structural_nesting_conflict`(920), `structural_nesting_warning`(941).
- 1002-1081 mise/flake in-cage path vocabulary: `MISE_DATA_REL`(1006), `MISE_SHIMS_REL`(1014), `MISE_PROJECT_INCAGE`(1027), `mise_app_global_data_dir`(1036), `FLAKE_ROOTS_REL`(1044), `flake_roots_dir`(1048), `flake_out_link`(1055), `flake_out_link_hash`(1071), `flake_inline_incage`(1079).
- 1083-1105 `SHELL_RC_INCAGE`(1089) and `SHELL_RC_CONTENTS`(1101).
- 1107-1196 `mise_env`.
- 1198-1234 `CAGE_CA_BUNDLE`(1204), `cacert_env`(1211), `upsert_env`(1220), `join_paths`(1229).
- 1236-1306 synthetic file *contents*: `passwd_contents`(1238), `group_contents`(1249), `hosts_contents`(1268), `machine_id_contents`(1294).
- 1308-1413 host-side runtime keying: `Runtime`(1314), `project_runtime`(1328), `home_src`(1358), `project_id`(1369), `project_runtime_id`(1382), `project_identity`(1391), `canonicalize_project`(1404).
- 1415-1484 `current_identity`(1417), `materialize_etc`(1435), `write_atomic`(1476).
- 1486-1706 `build_spec` — the I/O orchestrator that stages every synthetic file and calls `assemble`.

fsmask.rs is 622 production lines forming one linear pipeline (`expand` 154 → `resolve_list` 235 / `admit` 305 → `guard_hard_links` 355 / `guard_git_tracked` 387 → `agent_binds` 503 / `task_mounts` 542), all sharing `Expanded`/`Masked`/`Decoys`. fhs.rs is 400 production lines: a locale sub-cluster (73-179) and one 220-line straight-line `resolve_userland` (181-400).

### Justification

A split is worth doing, but the valuable part is not where the line count suggests, and I want to be explicit about that.

What must NOT move. `cage_mounts` (368-707) is 340 lines of one ordered list where "order is the invariant" (its own doc, 355-366: the `OPT_DIR` tmpfs at 386-389 is emitted before the config binds *precisely* so it shadows nothing, and every structural mount below depends on arriving after them). Splitting that function, or moving the in-cage destination constants (23-132) away from the mount that consumes them, would dissolve exactly the property the scope statement asks to preserve — a reader could no longer see the router's three-link mountpoint chain (`OPT_DIR` → `SBX_INCAGE_DIR` → `OPEN_ROUTER_DIR`) and the constants declaring it on one screen. `assemble`(342) → `cage_mounts` → `cage_env` and the five input types stay together. That is the module's cohesive core and it is genuinely one thing: ~700 lines, over half of it comment.

The real problem is that 2,906 of 4,612 lines are two inline `#[cfg(test)]` modules, and this repo already has the exact idiom for that: `src/sandbox/openpgp/mod.rs:437-438` and `src/sandbox/proxy/mod.rs:2058-2059` both declare `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` against a sibling file (`proxy/tests.rs` is 10,357 lines). Extracting them costs zero visibility changes, zero doc-link rewrites, and zero risk to the security seam, and removes 63% of the file. That alone takes binds.rs from 4,612 to 1,706 and is where nearly all the value is.

Beyond that, three clusters are genuinely separable because they answer a different question than "what does the cage see":
- Host-side runtime keying (1308-1413 + the `ProjectRuntime`/`Identity` types) touches no in-cage constant in code at all — only in prose — yet it is the most externally consumed part of the module: `project_runtime_id` has 10 call sites across flake/prebuilt/nixhub/packages/task/launch, and `project_id`/`project_identity` are re-exported at `src/sandbox/mod.rs:127` and reached from `paths.rs`, `main.rs`, `cli/proc.rs`, `cli/net.rs`, `projects.rs`. It is why `binds` appears as a dependency of modules that have nothing to do with binds.
- The nesting diagnostic (903-1000) generates warning *prose* for config validation (`config/load.rs:183,261`); the launcher never calls it.
- The synthetic-file *content* producers (1091-1105, 1236-1306, 1427-1484) are pure byte generators plus two staging helpers, currently scattered across four disjoint ranges with `mise_env` and `cacert_env` interleaved between them.

Those three total ~370 lines and leave the core at ~1,350. That is a modest but real gain, and — critically — the moves are cheap: I grepped every `super::`/`crate::` path in all five moved ranges and found exactly one (`super::egress::TcpDestination` at line 1268). Everything else is self-contained.

I am recommending "worthwhile" rather than "strongly recommended" because the second step buys ~370 lines for three new files plus ~8 doc-link rewrites and ~12 visibility annotations, under a CLAUDE.md regime where each of those is a build-breaking mistake if botched. Do step 1 unconditionally; step 2 is a positive but smaller return.

fsmask.rs and fhs.rs should be left alone. fsmask is 622 production lines of one pipeline whose stages all share `Expanded`/`Masked`/`Decoys`; there is no seam that does not cut a data structure in half. fhs is 400 lines whose one large function is a straight-line list of `realise(...)` calls where the comments are the content.

### Découpage proposé

#### `src/sandbox/binds/tests.rs` (~1815 lignes)

**Rôle.** The unit-test module, moved out of the file it tests. Same idiom as src/sandbox/openpgp/tests.rs and src/sandbox/proxy/tests.rs. This is the single highest-value, lowest-risk step: it removes 1,815 lines from binds.rs without touching one line of production code.

**Ce qui bouge.** The body of `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` — binds.rs lines 1709-3520 (the `mod tests {` header at 1707-1708 and its closing brace at 3521 are replaced by `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` in binds/mod.rs). File opens with the existing header verbatim: `use super::*; use crate::testutil::TmpDir; use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;` (lines 1709-1711). All 57 tests and the 11 helpers (`userland` 1730, `nix_mount` 1764, `base_paths` 1775, `assembled` 1795, `assembled_from` 1801, `assembled_with_ssh_config` 1823, `assembled_with_every_conditional_mount` 1834, `assemble_with` 2443, `assemble_with_zone` 2453, `argv_strings` 2480, `env_strings` 2490, `ro` 2852, `rw` 2860) move unchanged.

**Risque.** Essentially none. `binds::tests` becomes a child module of `binds` instead of an inline one — identical name resolution, so `use super::*` still reaches every private item (`assemble`, `cage_mounts`, `SHELL_RC_CONTENTS`, `machine_id_contents`, `materialize_etc`, `project_runtime`, `canonicalize_project`). No visibility change, no doc link (the file has none; the one `[`assemble_with`]` reference at 2443 is inside the moved region and stays local). docs_coverage: `module_level_items()` skips files named `tests.rs` whole (src/docs_coverage.rs:996-998), so nothing new enters the guard. Requires converting binds.rs → binds/mod.rs, which forces the docs_coverage.rs:905 edit described under binds/synthetic.rs. Declare it as `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` with NO `///` above the `mod` line — mise.toml:96-98 records that an outer doc comment on a `mod` declaration merges with the module header and shifts link resolution into the declaring module's scope.

#### `src/sandbox/binds/smoke_tests.rs` (~1090 lignes)

**Rôle.** The six live-bwrap end-to-end tests, which are a different kind of test from the rest: they need real bwrap + userns + nix, skip rather than fail without them, and each runs a full launch. Named `smoke_tests.rs` (not `smoke.rs`) both to avoid confusion with the unrelated production module src/sandbox/smoke.rs and to hit the docs-coverage whole-file skip.

**Ce qui bouge.** binds.rs lines 3523-4612: the module doc (3523-3528, rewritten from `///` to `//!` at the top of the new file), the header `use super::*; use crate::testutil::{TmpDir, fingerprint}; use std::process::Command;` (3531-3533), `prerequisites` (3537), and the six tests: `the_generated_argv_launches_a_working_hermetic_shell` (3547), `the_nix_ld_shim_serves_foreign_binaries_and_unskews_cross_channel_tools` (3658), `the_cage_runs_from_a_writable_per_project_store_seeded_with_the_base_closure` (3848), `the_cage_builds_a_fresh_derivation_offline_from_the_seeded_base` (4022), `network_reachable` (4209), `the_cage_self_equips_a_nix_tool_via_mise` (4233), `a_mise_used_tool_is_activated_on_path_in_a_later_launch` (4377), `a_global_app_cage_puts_both_mise_shims_dirs_on_path_and_splits_the_pool` (4522).

**Risque.** Two mechanical edits. (1) 21 path rewrites: the module reaches `super::super::argv` (7×), `super::super::fhs` (7×) and `super::super::projectstore` (4×); from `binds::smoke_tests` these gain one level. Rewrite them as `crate::sandbox::argv` / `crate::sandbox::fhs` / `crate::sandbox::projectstore` rather than adding a third `super`. (2) The module doc at 3525 says "The unit tests above check the argv *structure*" — "above" is false once the two live in sibling files; change to "the unit tests in `tests`". Otherwise no visibility change (it only uses `build_spec`, already `pub(crate)`). `skip_incapable!` still resolves: src/main.rs:13-15 declares `#[macro_use] mod testskip;` before every other module, so textual scope covers any module declared later, including this one. docs_coverage skips `*_tests.rs` whole.

#### `src/sandbox/binds/runtime.rs` (~155 lignes)

**Rôle.** Where a project's host-side runtime state lives, and the identity it is keyed by — a different question from "what does the cage see", and the part of binds that the rest of the crate actually depends on. Zero references to any in-cage constant in code.

**Ce qui bouge.** `ProjectRuntime` (261-275), `Runtime` (1308-1321), `project_runtime` (1323-1352), `home_src` (1354-1361), `project_id` (1363-1374), `project_runtime_id` (1376-1384), `project_identity` (1386-1395), `canonicalize_project` (1397-1413). Needs only `use std::io;` and `use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};` — I grepped 1308-1425 and 261-285 for `super::`/`crate::` and found none. `Identity` (277-285) and `current_identity` (1415-1425) deliberately do NOT come here; they exist only to feed passwd/group and go to synthetic.rs. `STRUCTURAL_DESTS` and every in-cage constant stay in mod.rs.

**Risque.** Visibility: `project_runtime` and `canonicalize_project` are private today and must become `pub(super)` (visible throughout the `binds` subtree, so both binds/mod.rs's `build_spec` and binds/tests.rs reach them). `ProjectRuntime`, `Runtime`, `home_src`, `project_id`, `project_runtime_id`, `project_identity` are already `pub(crate)` and stay so — they must be, because binds/mod.rs re-exports them: `pub(crate) use self::runtime::{ProjectRuntime, Runtime, home_src, project_id, project_identity, project_runtime_id};`. That single line keeps every external path byte-identical: `binds::Runtime` (launch.rs, task.rs), `super::binds::project_runtime_id` (10 sites), and the crate-level re-export at src/sandbox/mod.rs:127 (`pub(crate) use binds::{DEFAULT_ZONE, project_id, project_identity, structural_nesting_warning};`) all keep working, and `super::super::binds::project_runtime_id` in the flake/prebuilt/nixhub tests too.
Rustdoc links that break and their fixes: line 1355 `[`build_spec`]` → `[`super::build_spec`]`; line 271 (`ProjectRuntime::mise_project_src`) `[`MISE_PROJECT_INCAGE`]` → `[`super::MISE_PROJECT_INCAGE`]` (resolves fine even though the const is private — a child module sees its parent's private items). Line 1324 `[`Runtime`]`, 1356 `[`project_runtime`]` and 1380 `[`canonicalize_project`]` become local and are unaffected. In binds/mod.rs the `//!` header at line 15 says "[`project_runtime`] places them in a sibling of the writable home" — that link now resolves only because of a re-export; either add `project_runtime` to a private `use self::runtime::{canonicalize_project, project_runtime};` in mod.rs (a private `use` binding is nameable from the parent module's own docs) or rewrite it as `[`runtime::project_runtime`]`. External doc links are safe: `[`super::binds::project_identity`]` (egress_stats.rs:28), `[`binds::Runtime`]` (launch.rs:1024, 5578) and `[`sandbox::project_id`]`/`[`sandbox::project_identity`]` (paths.rs:321,375; main.rs:529) all resolve through the re-exports.
Tests: `each_runtime_scope_keys_a_distinct_persistent_home` (3026-3060), `project_runtime_keys_the_per_project_mise_pool_per_project_and_app` (3286-3334) and `canonicalize_project_follows_symlinks_and_requires_a_directory` (3160-3178) may move into an inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` here, or stay in binds/tests.rs — `pub(super)` covers both. `synthetic_etc_lives_outside_the_writable_home` (3003-3024) must stay in binds/tests.rs; it drives `build_spec`.
Cycles: none. `runtime.rs` imports nothing from its siblings; the dependency is one-way (mod.rs → runtime).

#### `src/sandbox/binds/synthetic.rs` (~140 lignes)

**Rôle.** The bytes of the synthetic files the cage is handed read-only, and the two helpers that stage them. Today these seven items are scattered across four disjoint ranges (1091-1105, 1236-1306, 1427-1453, 1471-1484) with `mise_env`, `cacert_env`, `join_paths` and the whole runtime cluster interleaved; they belong together, and the module's own integrity note (lines 11-16) is about exactly this group.

**Ce qui bouge.** `SHELL_RC_CONTENTS` (1091-1105), `passwd_contents` (1236-1246), `group_contents` (1248-1255), `hosts_contents` (1257-1282), `machine_id_contents` (1284-1306), `Identity` (277-285), `current_identity` (1415-1425), `materialize_etc` (1427-1453), `write_atomic` (1471-1484). Imports needed: `use std::io;`, `use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};`, `use super::{SANDBOX_HOME, SANDBOX_SHELL};` (for `materialize_etc`; both are private consts of the parent, which a child module may name), and `use crate::sandbox::egress;` for the one cross-module path in the whole moved set — `super::egress::TcpDestination` in the `hosts_contents` signature at line 1268.

**Risque.** This move FORCES a real doc-bug fix, which is a benefit but must be done first. binds.rs lines 1455-1467 are a `///` block written for `build_spec` ("Build a launch-ready [`SandboxSpec`] for `cwd`…"), followed by a `//` block at 1468-1470, then `write_atomic`'s own `///` at 1471-1475 and `fn write_atomic` at 1476. Rust attaches all of it to `write_atomic`, so `write_atomic`'s rustdoc page currently opens with build_spec's prose and `build_spec` (line 1487) is undocumented — which is precisely why `("sandbox/binds.rs", "build_spec")` sits on the grandfather list at src/docs_coverage.rs:905. Fix: move 1455-1470 above the `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` at line 1486, take only 1471-1484 into synthetic.rs, and DELETE the docs_coverage.rs:905 entry. Deleting it is mandatory either way — the `stale` assertion at src/docs_coverage.rs:1082-1090 fails on an entry that no longer applies, and renaming binds.rs → binds/mod.rs alone already invalidates the path key.
Visibility: `hosts_contents` is `pub(super)` today (= visible in `sandbox`, called at task.rs:1057). In a submodule `pub(super)` would mean only `binds`, so it must become `pub(crate)` (or `pub(in crate::sandbox)`) and binds/mod.rs must carry `pub(super) use self::synthetic::hosts_contents;` — the re-export is legal only if the item is at least as visible as the `use`, hence `pub(crate)` rather than `pub(super)` on the item. `SHELL_RC_CONTENTS`, `passwd_contents`, `group_contents`, `machine_id_contents`, `current_identity`, `materialize_etc`, `write_atomic` go private → `pub(super)` (needed by `build_spec` in mod.rs and by binds/tests.rs). `Identity` stays `pub(crate)`; nothing outside `binds` names it, so mod.rs needs only a private `use self::synthetic::Identity;`.
Rustdoc: line 1431 `[`write_atomic`]` stays local. After the fix, build_spec's restored block links `[`assemble`]` and `[`SandboxSpec`]` from mod.rs, where both are in scope. No external doc link names any of these items.
Tests that move or stay: `the_shell_rc_sets_a_cage_naming_prompt_before_sourcing_bashrc` (1714-1728), `a_hosts_line_is_never_written_for_a_name_the_file_already_maps` (1881-1912), `the_synthetic_hosts_maps_localhost_and_the_cage_hostname` (1963-1985), `the_synthetic_machine_id_is_systemd_shaped_deterministic_and_per_home` (1987-2006), `synthetic_passwd_carries_the_identity_and_no_host_account` (3062-3077), `materialize_etc_writes_owner_only_files_with_the_synthetic_content` (3079-3096), `re_materializing_the_identity_installs_a_new_inode_instead_of_rewriting_in_place` (3098-3158) are all content-level and can move here; the `assemble_binds_a_read_only_*` tests must stay in binds/tests.rs since they assert mount output.
Cycles: `synthetic.rs` → `sandbox::egress` and → parent constants; `sandbox::egress` does not reach back into `binds::synthetic` (it uses `binds::project_identity` at egress.rs:769, which will live in runtime.rs). No cycle, and intra-crate module cycles are not a compile error in any case — only a design smell.

#### `src/sandbox/binds/nesting.rs` (~105 lignes)

**Rôle.** The ergonomics tripwire that tells a user their `[[binds]]` entry nests with a structural mount and will not behave as written. A prose generator consumed only by config validation — the launcher never calls it, and it produces no mount.

**Ce qui bouge.** `Nesting` (903-911), `structural_nesting_conflict` (913-933), `structural_nesting_warning` (935-1000). Needs only `use std::path::Path;` plus `use super::STRUCTURAL_DESTS;`. `STRUCTURAL_DESTS` (861-901) deliberately STAYS in binds/mod.rs: it is the declaration of the cage's fixed destinations, held in lockstep with `cage_mounts` by `structural_dests_lists_every_fixed_mount_assemble_emits` (1845-1879), and read by task.rs:3384 as `super::super::binds::STRUCTURAL_DESTS`. Moving it away from the mount list it mirrors is exactly the erosion of the one-place property to avoid.

**Risque.** Visibility: `structural_nesting_warning` stays `pub(crate)` and binds/mod.rs adds `pub(crate) use self::nesting::structural_nesting_warning;` so src/sandbox/mod.rs:127's re-export and both call sites in src/config/load.rs (lines 183 and 261, via `crate::sandbox::structural_nesting_warning`) are untouched. `Nesting` and `structural_nesting_conflict` stay private to the new file. `STRUCTURAL_DESTS` is `pub(super)` today (visible in `sandbox`) and stays exactly as it is in mod.rs — no change, so task.rs:3384 is unaffected; `nesting.rs` reads it as `super::STRUCTURAL_DESTS`, legal for a child module.
Rustdoc: one link breaks — line 916, in `structural_nesting_conflict`'s doc, `[`assemble`]` → `[`super::assemble`]`. The reverse direction is safe: `STRUCTURAL_DESTS`'s doc at line 869 says `[`structural_nesting_warning`]` and stays in mod.rs, where the `pub(crate) use` puts the name back in scope. Line 861 `[`assemble`]` in that same doc is unaffected.
Tests: `structural_nesting_warning_flags_only_a_nesting_overlap` (2032-2078) moves here as an inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`, or stays in binds/tests.rs — either works.
Cycles: none; `nesting.rs` reads one parent constant and nothing else.

### Autres observations

**A live documentation bug, independent of any split.** binds.rs:1455-1467 is a `///` block written for `build_spec` but sitting above `fn write_atomic` (1476), separated only by a `//` block at 1468-1470 which does not break attachment. rustc attaches all of 1455-1475 to `write_atomic`, so `cargo doc` renders build_spec's prose on write_atomic's page and `build_spec` (1487) has no documentation at all. This is the exact failure mode `every_module_level_item_carries_a_doc_comment` was written to catch (src/docs_coverage.rs:1042-1051: "when a refactor moves, renames or deletes an item and leaves its `///` block behind, the block silently becomes the *next* item's documentation"), and it is grandfathered at src/docs_coverage.rs:905. Fix by moving 1455-1470 below `fn write_atomic`'s closing brace at 1484 and above the `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` at 1486, then deleting the grandfather entry. Worth doing on its own even if nothing else here is adopted — the guard's own prose says the list can only shrink.

**A three-way duplication.** `mise_app_global_data_dir()` (binds.rs:1036-1038) is exactly `format!("{SANDBOX_HOME}/{MISE_DATA_REL}")`, and `mise_env` re-inlines the identical expression at line 1165 and its `/installs` sibling at 1175. `mise_env` should call `mise_app_global_data_dir()` at 1165 and `format!("{}/installs", mise_app_global_data_dir())` at 1175, so the app-global pool path has one definition. Four tests (3233, 3248, 3398, 4608) assert the literal, and they keep passing.

**A cluster I considered and recommend against moving.** `MISE_DATA_REL`/`MISE_SHIMS_REL`/`MISE_PROJECT_INCAGE` + `mise_app_global_data_dir` + the four `flake_*` helpers + `mise_env` + `cacert_env` (1002-1234, ~230 lines) look like a natural `binds/toolchain.rs`. Do not do it. `MISE_PROJECT_INCAGE` is a cage destination bound by `cage_mounts` at 616-620 and listed in `STRUCTURAL_DESTS` at 899; `CAGE_CA_BUNDLE` likewise at 559 and 897. Pulling them out separates two structural destinations from the mount plan that declares them, for a smaller gain than the three modules above, and it costs six `[`MISE_PROJECT_INCAGE`]` and three `[`mise_env`]` link rewrites (lines 232, 271, 294-295, 611, 613, 1031, 1113, 1608). The three proposed modules were chosen precisely because none of them contains a cage destination.

**`#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` appears twice** (line 342 on `assemble`, 8 args; line 1486 on `build_spec`, 14 args). The comment at 1468-1470 defends this explicitly as the grouping discipline that keeps the audited core at the argument limit. It is a deliberate seam, not drift — leave it. Note that if `build_spec` ever grows another resolved slice, the right move is a second grouping struct beside `SandboxPaths`, not a split.

**fsmask.rs: leave as is.** 622 production lines forming one pipeline whose every stage shares `Expanded`/`Masked`/`Decoys` (`expand` 154 → `resolve_list` 235 → `match_in_dir` 281 → `admit` 305 → `guard_hard_links` 355 / `guard_git_tracked` 387 → `agent_binds` 503 / `task_mounts` 542 → `lift_paths` 579). The only self-contained sub-cluster is the git-index reader (`git_tracked_paths` 429, `parse_git_index` 445, ~70 lines) — a binary format parser that could live in `fsmask/gitindex.rs`, but at 70 lines that is not worth a file. Its 440-line test module (623-1062) could follow the same `tests.rs` idiom if the file ever grows, but at 1,062 lines it is not yet a problem.

**fhs.rs: leave as is.** 400 production lines. The locale sub-cluster (`normalize_utf8_locale` 73, `host_locales` 101, `locale_set` 113, `provision_locale_archive` 138, `locale_archive_expr` 159) is already isolated behind its own `#[cfg(test)] mod locale_tests` (401-489), and `resolve_userland` (181-400) is a 220-line straight-line sequence of `realise(...)` calls where the prose is most of the volume and the ordering carries meaning (the base is provisioned against `nixpkgs`, mise against `engine_ref`, and `root_channel_source` must come after all of them — comment at 337-340). Splitting it would produce two files that only ever call each other in one direction, once.

**Ordering if all of this is adopted.** (1) Fix the build_spec doc block and drop docs_coverage.rs:905 — a standalone commit that leaves the file layout untouched. (2) binds.rs → binds/mod.rs + tests.rs + smoke_tests.rs, no production edits. (3) The three production submodules, one commit each, each self-checkable by `mise run rustdoc` for the link rewrites named above.

---

## `task` — src/sandbox/task.rs (4436 lines), src/sandbox/task_control.rs (3844 lines), src/sandbox/task_shim.rs, src/sandbox/taskpool.rs

**Verdict : **Découpage utile****

### Cartographie actuelle

Four files, 9,916 lines total (task.rs 4,436; task_control.rs 3,844; taskpool.rs 1,006; task_shim.rs 630). ~4,116 of those lines are inline `#[cfg(test)]` modules.

THE AXIS IS ALREADY RIGHT. The four files split along real trust seams: task.rs = the engine that derives a sibling cage from the agent cage and runs one invocation in it; task_control.rs = the two-socket plane (crossing + host-only) with its wire protocol and in-RAM record; task_shim.rs = the generated in-cage client that can express three verbs and nothing else; taskpool.rs = the host-side mise pool no cage mounts writable. Dependency direction is strictly one-way: task_control.rs:75 imports `super::task::{TaskEngine, TaskOutcome}` and task_control.rs:650 calls `task_shim::write`; task.rs references task_control only from inside its test module (task.rs:3340-3352). The engine deliberately does not draw its own invocation id (task.rs:583-587) so that the plane owns admission — that seam must survive.

INTERNAL MAP OF task.rs (4,436):
- 1-45 module doc ("Why a sibling cage and not the agent's") + imports.
- 47-205 constants: TASK_HOME 48, TASK_OUT_INCAGE/ENV 56-57, TASK_OUT_AGENT/TREE 66-67, TASK_INVOCATION 90 + `next_invocation` 95-97, MAX_DETACHED 106, MAX_LIVE 122, POLL_INTERVAL 127, STOPPED_EXIT 132, STOP_GRACE 140, KEPT_DESTS 155-184, KEPT_ENV 186-205.
- 207-406 types: `TaskEngine` (20 fields) 207-275, `CageForwarder` 280-285, `TaskOutcome` 292-341, `invocation_nonce` 346-355, `TaskError` + Display 360-380, `refusal_block` 396-406.
- 408-1312 impl #1: `from_cage` 412-446, six `with_*` builders 451-513, pool queries `declared_packages`/`ensure_pool`/`upgrade_pool`/`missing_packages` 515-570, `tasks`/`task` 573-587, `run` 589-608, `admit` 610-641, `admit_inner` 642-684, `run_admitted` 685-950 (the 265-line keystone), `substituted_refusals` 951-975, `cage_argv` 976-989, `build_spec` 990-1146, `exec` 1147-1229, registry writers `stop_requested`/`note_pid`/`note_argv` 1230-1265, `describe`/`describe_task` 1266-1311.
- 1313-1780 impl #2: `pool_bins` 1317-1326, `output_root` 1328-1330, `claim_output` 1331-1372, `enter` 1376-1435, `running` 1436-1460, `stop` 1447-1477, `is_running` 1480-1488, then the spawn-policy cluster `host_path` 1490-1536, `resolve_spawn_entry` 1528-1568, `canonical_incage` 1570-1600, `read_incage_link` 1602-1613, `warn_if_multicall` 1615-1660, `entered_as` 1662-1682, `shebang_of` 1684-1703, `spawn_policy` 1705-1779.
- 1782-2290 free items: `lexical_join` 1782-1798, `prepend_path` 1800-1811, `Invocation<'a>` 1813-1826, `output_root_for` 1828-1845, `OutputClaim` + `size` + `Drop` 1847-1883, `Running` 1885-1908, `shell_line` 1910-1922, `declared_fields` 1924-2017, `StopOutcome` 2019-2032, `RunningView` 2030-2049, `Admission` + `hold_registration` 2044-2080, `RunGuard` + `Drop` 2082-2097, `RawOutput` 2099-2107, `read_capped` 2109-2142, `task_mounts` 2144-2174, `mount_dest` 2175-2188, `task_env` 2188-2199, `resolve_params` 2196-2230, `caller_env` 2226-2255, `substitute` 2257-2289.
- 2291-2366 `#[cfg(test)] impl TaskEngine` (inventory_with_launcher, inventory_only, with_launcher, with_tree) — test-only code sitting between production free functions.
- 2368-2412 `credential_needles` 2381-2405 (used by egress.rs:2978/2994/3037), `resolve_secret` 2408-2412.
- 2423-2994 `#[cfg(test)] mod smoke` (572 lines, 5 tests).
- 2996-4436 `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (1,441 lines, 47 tests, flat, with builder helpers interleaved: `task()` 3137, `values()` 3168, `engine_with_pool` 3611, `engine_at` 3647, `engine_with_store` 3665, `engine_with_project` 4032, `tcp_plan` 4152).

INTERNAL MAP OF task_control.rs (3,844):
- 1-64 module doc (the "two sockets, on purpose" argument + the wire protocol), 66-77 imports.
- 79-178 constants: CAGE_TASK_UDS 81, TASK_SOCKET_ENV 85, TASK_SHIM_INCAGE 94, LOG_CAPACITY 97, RESULT_CAPACITY 104, DEFAULT_CALL_QUOTA 109, MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES 113, MAX_CONCURRENT_CONNS 129, CAGE_FIRST_REQUEST 148, MAX_REQUEST_BYTES 162.
- 180-186 `read_request_line`.
- 189-560 THE RECORD: `LogEntry` 192-235 + `to_line` 244-268 + `from_line` 281-323, `epoch_ms` 340-346, `sanitize` 366-368, `head_field` 385-392, `TaskLog` 418-421 + `Inner` 423-431 + `new`/`push`/`since`/`recorded`/`entry` 431-519, `Held` 525, `TaskResults` 535-537 + `store`/`get` 539-559.
- 562-612 `ClientPrograms` 562-570, `TaskPlane` + `Drop` 571-592, INCARNATION 594, `task_dir` 597, `shim_path` 603, `log_socket` 609.
- 617-1338 THE SERVERS: `start` 617-749 (two structurally identical accept loops at 660-690 and 691-725), `serve_cage` 751-841, `Payloads` 842, `read_payloads` 849-907, `admit_quota` 909-935, `finished` 937-956, `serve_run` 957-988, `serve_detach` 998-1063, `serve_result` 1065-1101, `finished_fields` 1103-1148, `refusal` 1150-1170, `write_outcome` 1171-1225, `serve_host` 1227-1338.
- 1339-1512 HOST-SIDE READERS: `ask_host` 1339-1347, `read_log` 1354-1357 (`#[cfg(test)]`), `read_entries` 1367-1388, `StatusRow` 1393-1396, `read_status` 1399-1413, `read_info` 1415-1429, `StopReply` 1431-1437, `stop_invocation` 1439-1471, `session_pids` 1473-1499, `plane_is_live` 1501-1511.
- 1513-1740 `pub(crate) mod client` (TaskRow, list, secrets, run, run_detached, invoke, result, RunResult, parse_run, split_line, exchange).
- 1742-3844 `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (2,103 lines, ~60 tests).

taskpool.rs and task_shim.rs are each one subject with ~580 and ~370 production lines respectively.

### Justification

The four-file axis is correct and should not be touched. What is wrong is depth inside two of the four, and the largest single cause is mechanical rather than architectural: 4,116 of 9,916 lines are inline `#[cfg(test)]` bodies. task.rs is 45% tests, task_control.rs is 55% tests. Moving those out-of-line costs literally nothing semantically — a child module declared `mod tests;` still sees every private item of its parent, the module path stays `sandbox::task::tests` so `cargo test --lib task::tests::…` filters are unchanged, `super::super::task_control::CAGE_TASK_UDS` (task.rs:3340) and `super::super::task_shim::write` (task_control.rs:2194) still resolve, and `src/docs_coverage.rs:997` skips files named `tests.rs`/`*_tests.rs` whole. The precedent is already in the tree: `src/sandbox/proxy/mod.rs:2058-2059` is exactly `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` pointing at a 10,357-line `proxy/tests.rs`. That one move takes task.rs to ~2,420 and task_control.rs to ~1,740 — both then the size of `control/mod.rs` (2,410) and `proxy/mod.rs` (2,059), which nobody is asking to split.

Beyond that, three production clusters are worth extracting because each is a self-contained sub-problem with a single call site into it, not because of line count:

(1) The spawn-policy cluster (task.rs:1490-1779 + `lexical_join` 1782-1798). Eight methods that translate a declared `spawn`/`exec` list into a `ProcPolicy` keyed on absolute in-cage paths, reading the cage's mount list to resolve names, follow symlinks in cage space, and read `#!` lines. It is reached from exactly one place — `run_admitted` at task.rs:769 — and from nine tests. Its doc prose is the densest in the file and is about a subject (what a process *is* when the kernel enters it) that has nothing to do with mounts, credentials or capture.

(2) The cage-skeleton derivation (KEPT_DESTS 155-184, KEPT_ENV 186-205, `task_mounts` 2144-2174, `mount_dest` 2175-2188, `task_env` 2188-2199). This is one security allowlist and the filter that applies it, and the two halves currently sit 1,940 lines apart. The module header (task.rs:12-18) argues about precisely this pair; a reader following that argument has to jump twice.

(3) The in-RAM record in task_control.rs (189-560 + the two `LogEntry` constructors `finished` 936-956 and `refusal` 1149-1170, which sit 200 lines apart with identical "stamped by `TaskLog::push`" comments). It is a data structure with its own documented invariant ("the lock cannot be poisoned, and that is a property to keep", 428-441), its own wire encoding with a round-trip test, and its own sanitisation rules; it calls nothing back into the servers.

Honest counterweights, stated because they are real:
- `run_admitted` (685-950) is genuinely one 265-line sequence — credentials → needles → proc supervisor → proxy → build_spec → exec → redaction — where the ORDER is the security property (the proc shim must wrap innermost, the redaction must happen before anything is returned or logged). Do not split it. `build_spec` (990-1146) is likewise one ordered mount list where the ordering comments are the content. Both stay in mod.rs.
- The two servers `serve_cage` and `serve_host` should NOT be split into two files. The module's entire header is one argument about why these two exist as a pair; separating them separates the two halves of that argument, and they share `read_payloads`, `admit_quota`, `write_outcome`, `finished` and `refusal`.
- Splitting an inherent `impl TaskEngine` across files means "where is `TaskEngine::stop`" becomes a grep. task.rs already pays this (two impl blocks, 408 and 1316) and `proxy` pays it too (`ProxyCtx` has impls at ctx.rs:180 and ctx.rs:671). Mitigate with a two-line "the rest of TaskEngine lives in …" note in the mod.rs header, not by avoiding the split.
- taskpool.rs and task_shim.rs need nothing. Leave both.

### Découpage proposé

#### `src/sandbox/task/tests.rs (task.rs becomes src/sandbox/task/mod.rs)` (~1441 lignes)

**Rôle.** Hold the engine's unit-test module out of line, exactly as src/sandbox/proxy/mod.rs:2058-2059 already does for proxy/tests.rs.

**Ce qui bouge.** task.rs:2996-4436 verbatim (the whole `#[cfg(test)] mod tests { … }` body, 47 tests plus the helpers `task()` 3137, `values()` 3168, `engine_with_pool` 3611, `engine_at` 3647, `engine_with_store` 3665, `engine_with_project` 4032, `tcp_plan` 4152). Replace with `#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests;` at the foot of task/mod.rs. Keep the `use super::*;` at the top of the new file.

**Risque.** Visibility: none. `task::tests` stays a descendant of `task`, so the struct-literal constructions of `TaskEngine` at 3611-3646 and 3647-3664 keep reaching its 20 private fields, and `engine.base_env` / `engine.base_mounts` / `engine.project` field writes keep compiling. Intra-doc links: none broken; the tests carry no `///` on the `mod` line today. Test names: unchanged (`sandbox::task::tests::…`), so no filter breaks. Cross-module paths inside the tests are unchanged — `super::super::task_control::CAGE_TASK_UDS` (3340), `super::super::taskpool::POOL_INCAGE` (4369), `super::super::egress::TcpPlan` (4152) all still resolve because `super::super` is still `sandbox`. docs_coverage: the new file is named `tests.rs`, which `src/docs_coverage.rs:997` skips whole, so the undocumented helpers `task()` (3137) and `values()` (3168) stay exempt. Only cost: the file rename task.rs → task/mod.rs, which changes nothing in `UNDOCUMENTED_MODULE_ITEMS` because no entry keys on `sandbox/task.rs`.

#### `src/sandbox/task_control/tests.rs (task_control.rs becomes src/sandbox/task_control/mod.rs)` (~2103 lignes)

**Rôle.** Same move for the plane's test module, which is 55% of the file.

**Ce qui bouge.** task_control.rs:1742-3844 verbatim (the whole `#[cfg(test)] mod tests { … }`, ~60 tests plus `read_payloads_of` 1759, `probe_plane` 1933, `cage_conn` 2007, `probe_task` 2112, `plane_and_client` 2152, `plane_and_client_with_launcher` 2160, `plane_and_client_inner` 2167, `slow_plane_and_client` 2279, `plane_with_launcher` 2284, `plane_with_output` 2321, `eventually` 2439, `run_client` 2981, `entry` 3401). Replace with `#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests;`.

**Risque.** Visibility: none, for the same descendant rule — the tests keep reaching private `read_payloads`, the `Payloads` type alias (842), `serve_cage`'s `first_request` parameter, `TaskLog::push`/`since`, `TaskResults::store`/`get`, `LogEntry::to_line`, and `TaskPlane`'s private `log_socket` field. Intra-doc links: the two links written inside the test module — `[`plane_and_client`]` (2005) and `[`super::super::task::TaskEngine::inventory_with_launcher`]` (3036) — both still resolve. `super::super::task_shim::write` at 2194/2306/3166/3251/3328 still resolves. docs_coverage: MUST re-key `src/docs_coverage.rs:937` from `("sandbox/task_control.rs", "Inner")` to `("sandbox/task_control/mod.rs", "Inner")` — the list is keyed by file path and the guard "fails just as loudly on an entry that no longer applies". This is the single required edit outside the two files.

#### `src/sandbox/task/smoke_tests.rs` (~572 lignes)

**Rôle.** Hold the end-to-end smoke module (a real task, a real sibling cage, real bwrap) out of line, and stop its name colliding with the unrelated production `sandbox::smoke`.

**Ce qui bouge.** task.rs:2423-2994 (the `#[cfg(test)] mod smoke { … }` body: `engine_with` 2433, `engine_for` 2457, `echo_task` 2528, `params` 2572, and the five smoke tests 2580/2662/2733/2812/2904). Declare as `#[cfg(test)]\nmod smoke_tests;`. The `///` block at task.rs:2423-2424 that currently sits ON the `mod` declaration becomes a `//!` header inside the new file.

**Risque.** Name: the module is renamed `smoke` → `smoke_tests`, which changes test paths from `sandbox::task::smoke::…` to `sandbox::task::smoke_tests::…`. The rename is not cosmetic — it is what makes the filename match `*_tests.rs`, which `src/docs_coverage.rs:997` skips. Without it, `params()` at task.rs:2572 (which carries no `///`) would newly be scanned as a module-level item at column 0 and fail `every_module_level_item_carries_a_doc_comment`. Side benefit: `src/sandbox/mod.rs:178` already has `pub(crate) use smoke::run as smoke;` for a different, production `smoke` module; the rename removes that collision. Doc comment: converting the `///` on the `mod` line to `//!` is required, not optional — mise.toml:96-98 records that a doc comment written on a `mod` declaration merges with the module header and moves the whole block's link resolution into the declaring module's scope. Nothing outside references `task::smoke`.

#### `src/sandbox/task/spawn.rs` (~307 lignes)

**Rôle.** The exec-policy derivation: turn a declared `spawn`/`exec` graph into a ProcPolicy keyed on the absolute in-cage path each program is entered as, resolving names on the cage's PATH, symlinks in cage space, and `#!` interpreters.

**Ce qui bouge.** task.rs:1490-1779 as a `impl TaskEngine` block: `host_path` (1490-1536), `resolve_spawn_entry` (1528-1568), `canonical_incage` (1570-1600), `read_incage_link` (1602-1613), `warn_if_multicall` (1615-1660), `entered_as` (1662-1682), `shebang_of` (1684-1703), `spawn_policy` (1705-1779); plus the free fn `lexical_join` (task.rs:1782-1798), whose only two callers are `canonical_incage` (1596) and `entered_as` (1666).

**Risque.** Visibility: three items must widen from private to `pub(super)` — `spawn_policy` (called from `run_admitted` at task.rs:769 and from five tests at 3738/3799/3844/3933/3978), `resolve_spawn_entry` (four tests at 3699/3705/3712/3719), `host_path` (three tests at 4016/4020/4024). `canonical_incage`, `read_incage_link`, `warn_if_multicall`, `shebang_of` and `lexical_join` stay private to the new module — verified, they have no caller outside 1490-1798. `pub(super)` is narrower than the `pub(crate)` used elsewhere in the file, so no crate surface grows. Intra-doc links: none in this range point outward and none outside points in — the only links in the moved prose are plain code spans. Tests: the nine tests at 3691-4031 stay in task/tests.rs rather than moving, because they depend on `engine_with_store` (3665) and `engine_at` (3647), which the mount/hosts/pool tests also use; this follows the proxy precedent of one tests.rs per module tree. Cyclic risk: none — a child module reading `super::TaskEngine` cannot cycle, and `spawn.rs` needs only `crate::proc_policy::{CallerGraph, ProcRule}`, `super::proc_enforce::SHIM_CAGE_PATH` and `super::spec::Mount`.

#### `src/sandbox/task/registry.rs` (~297 lignes)

**Rôle.** The live-invocation registry: the single `running: Arc<Mutex<BTreeMap<u64, Running>>>` field, the two concurrency caps it enforces, the guards that keep it from stranding entries, and the admission handoff that makes attached and detached one path.

**Ce qui bouge.** Types: `Running` (task.rs:1885-1908), `StopOutcome` (2019-2032), `RunningView` (2030-2049), `Admission` + `hold_registration` (2044-2080), `RunGuard` + `Drop` (2082-2097). Methods, as an `impl TaskEngine` block: `stop_requested` (1230-1239), `note_pid` (1241-1248), `note_argv` (1250-1265), `enter` (1376-1435), `running` (1436-1460), `stop` (1447-1477), `is_running` (1480-1488). Constants whose prose is about this registry: `MAX_DETACHED` (98-106), `MAX_LIVE` (108-122), `STOP_GRACE` (134-140).

**Risque.** Visibility: `enter`, `stop_requested`, `note_pid`, `note_argv` become `pub(super)` (called from `admit_inner` 656, `exec` 1160/1179, `run_admitted` 703 in mod.rs); `running`/`stop` are already `pub(crate)` (task_control.rs:1284, 1329); `is_running` stays private. `Running` and `Admission`'s private fields stay private to `registry` — but `run_admitted` at task.rs:690-696 destructures `Admission { id, live, values, caller_env, output }`, so those five fields must become `pub(super)` OR `Admission` grows a `pub(super) fn into_parts()`. Prefer the latter: it keeps the guard discipline (`live` is an `Option` only so `hold_registration` can take it) expressed in one place. Re-exports needed in task/mod.rs so external paths survive: `pub(crate) use registry::{Admission, RunGuard, RunningView, StopOutcome};` (task_control.rs:1327-1333 matches on `super::task::StopOutcome::…`; launch/plane code holds `Admission` and `RunGuard`), plus `pub(super) use registry::{MAX_DETACHED, MAX_LIVE};` because task_control.rs:103 has the intra-doc link `[`super::task::MAX_DETACHED`]`. Precedent for both: `src/sandbox/control/mod.rs:39-41` re-exports with `pub(crate) use capture::*;` for exactly this reason. Intra-doc links: moving MAX_DETACHED/MAX_LIVE/STOP_GRACE INTO registry.rs is what keeps `[`MAX_DETACHED`]` (117), `[`MAX_LIVE`]` (1385) and `[`TaskEngine::stop`]` (134) intra-file; leaving them behind would require rewriting each to `[`super::MAX_LIVE`]`. `[`OutputClaim`]` (2083, in RunGuard's doc) and `[`TaskEngine::run_admitted`]` (2048, in Admission's doc) become cross-module and are the two links to re-verify with `mise run rustdoc` — `OutputClaim` is private to mod.rs, so that one needs `[`super::OutputClaim`]`. `POLL_INTERVAL` stays in mod.rs (used by both `exec` 1208 and `stop` 1476); registry.rs adds `use super::POLL_INTERVAL;`. Tests: the four tests under the `// --- how many run at once ---` banner (task.rs:3001-3136) stay in task/tests.rs and reach `enter`/`running`/`hold_registration` through the `pub(super)` widening. Cyclic risk: none.

#### `src/sandbox/task/skeleton.rs` (~115 lignes)

**Rôle.** What a task cage inherits from the agent cage: the two allowlists and the two pure filters that apply them, in one place instead of 1,940 lines apart.

**Ce qui bouge.** `KEPT_DESTS` (task.rs:147-184, including the doc block that argues why it is an allowlist), `KEPT_ENV` (186-205), `task_mounts` (2144-2174), `mount_dest` (2175-2188), `task_env` (2188-2199).

**Risque.** Visibility: all five become `pub(super)`. `task_mounts` and `task_env` are called by `from_cage` (task.rs:427-428) and by tests (3347/3409/3452/3488 and 3422/3465/3511); `mount_dest` is called by `task_mounts` and by tests at 3350/3411/3454/4188/4237/4369; `KEPT_DESTS` by `every_kept_destination_is_one_the_cage_emits` (3382). Intra-doc links: exactly two break and must be requalified — `TaskEngine`'s field docs at task.rs:211 (`[`KEPT_DESTS`]`) and 213 (`[`KEPT_ENV`]`) stay in mod.rs while their targets move, so they become `[`skeleton::KEPT_DESTS`]` / `[`skeleton::KEPT_ENV`]`. The link at 184 (inside KEPT_ENV's own doc) and 2145 (inside `task_mounts`'s doc) move with their target and are unaffected. Note task.rs:487 writes `` `KEPT_DESTS` `` as a plain code span, not a link, so it costs nothing. Name collision: none — `taskpool::task_env` (taskpool.rs:466) is a different module. Cyclic risk: none; skeleton.rs needs only `super::spec::Mount` and `super::binds`'s constants.

#### `src/sandbox/task_control/record.rs` (~426 lignes)

**Rôle.** The session's in-RAM record: the bounded invocation log, the detached-result ring, the entry type with its wire encoding, and the two sanitisers that keep a cage-chosen task name inside its own field.

**Ce qui bouge.** `LOG_CAPACITY` + `RESULT_CAPACITY` (task_control.rs:96-104), `LogEntry` + `to_line` + `from_line` (189-338), `epoch_ms` (326-346), `sanitize` (348-368), `head_field` (370-392), `TaskLog` + `Inner` + `new`/`push`/`since`/`recorded`/`entry` (394-519), `Held` (521-525), `TaskResults` + `store`/`get` (527-559), and the two entry constructors that currently sit 200 lines apart in the server section: `finished` (936-956) and `refusal` (1149-1170).

**Risque.** Visibility: `TaskLog`, `TaskResults`, `LogEntry` and `epoch_ms` are already `pub(crate)` (cli/task.rs and cli/logs.rs consume `LogEntry::from_line` and `epoch_ms`). Widening to `pub(super)` needed for: `TaskLog::push`/`since`/`recorded`/`entry`, `TaskResults::store`/`get`, `LogEntry::to_line`, `sanitize`, `head_field`, `Held`, `LOG_CAPACITY`, `RESULT_CAPACITY` (the last is interpolated into a message in `serve_result` at 1082), `finished`, `refusal` — twelve items, all currently private, all called from the servers in mod.rs and from tests. `Inner` stays private to record.rs. Re-export in task_control/mod.rs: `pub(crate) use record::{LogEntry, StatusRow-free items…}` — concretely `pub(crate) use record::{LogEntry, TaskLog, TaskResults, epoch_ms};` so `crate::sandbox::task_control::LogEntry::from_line` (linked from cli/task.rs:1442) and `task_control::epoch_ms` (cli/task.rs) keep resolving. Precedent that a link through a `use` re-export resolves: proxy/mod.rs:300 links `[`ProxyCtx::max_conns`]` to a type defined in proxy/ctx.rs and re-exported at proxy/mod.rs:202. Intra-doc links: `[`TaskLog`]`, `[`TaskLog::push`]`, `[`TaskLog::since`]`, `[`head_field`]`, `[`sanitize`]`, `[`to_line`]`, `[`LogEntry::from_line`]`, `[`cursor`]`, `[`seq`]`, `[`at_epoch_ms`]`, `[`TaskResults`]`, `[`LOG_CAPACITY`]` all move together and stay intra-file. Three cross the boundary and need requalifying: `[`MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES`]` (356, in `sanitize`'s doc → `[`super::MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES`]`), `[`super::proc_control`]` (382, already absolute-ish but `super` now means `task_control`, so it becomes `super::super::proc_control`), and `[`super::sanitize`]` (350, the sandbox-level one — likewise `super::super::sanitize`). `[`TaskLog::since`]` from read_entries' doc (1361) and `[`super::task::MAX_DETACHED`]` (103) resolve through the re-export/unchanged path. docs_coverage: `Inner` moves, so line 937's entry becomes `("sandbox/task_control/record.rs", "Inner")`. Cyclic risk: none — record.rs imports `super::super::task::TaskOutcome` for `finished`, and task.rs does not import task_control outside its tests.

#### `src/sandbox/task_control/client.rs` (~229 lignes)

**Rôle.** The wire client both callers use: the in-cage generated shim's host-side twin and the CLI's invoker. One connection, one command.

**Ce qui bouge.** task_control.rs:1512-1740, the entire `pub(crate) mod client { … }` body: `TaskRow`, `list`, `secrets`, `run`, `run_detached`, `invoke`, `result`, `RunResult`, `parse_run`, `split_line`, `exchange`. Declared in mod.rs as `pub(crate) mod client;`.

**Risque.** Visibility: zero change. The module path stays `sandbox::task_control::client`, so every external reference (`client::run`, `client::parse_run`, `client::RunResult`) is untouched. Its `use super::*;` still picks up the parent's imports. Intra-doc links: `[`run`]` (1560) is intra-module and unaffected. Tests: the client tests (task_control.rs:2997-3400 region, `the_client_lists_what_the_plane_serves`, `the_client_parses_what_write_outcome_produces`, `the_run_parser_takes_streams_by_length_not_by_keyword`, etc.) stay in task_control/tests.rs and reach `client::…` by the same path they use today. Cyclic risk: none. This is the cheapest of the production moves and mirrors `src/sandbox/control/client.rs` exactly.

#### `src/sandbox/task_control/host.rs` (~133 lignes)

**Rôle.** The host-only socket's reader side — the API `sbx task status|show|stop|logs` and `sbx logs --feed task` call. Separated from the servers because it is the other end of the same wire, and because it is the module's only consumer-facing surface.

**Ce qui bouge.** task_control.rs:1339-1471: `ask_host` (1339-1347), `read_log` (1349-1357, `#[cfg(test)]`), `read_entries` (1359-1388), `StatusRow` (1390-1396), `read_status` (1398-1413), `read_info` (1415-1429), `StopReply` (1431-1437), `stop_invocation` (1439-1471). Re-export from mod.rs with `pub(crate) use host::*;`, the pattern `src/sandbox/control/mod.rs:39-41` already uses ("re-exported so callers keep reaching them as `control::…`").

**Risque.** Visibility: everything here is already `pub(crate)` except `ask_host`, which is private and called only by the seven functions moving with it — it stays private. External paths `task_control::read_entries` (cli/logs.rs, cli/task.rs), `read_status`, `read_info`, `stop_invocation`, `StatusRow`, `StopReply` all survive via the glob re-export. Intra-doc links: `[`TaskLog::since`]` (1361) and `[`LogEntry::to_line`]`/`[`LogEntry::from_line`]` (1351) become cross-module; they resolve through the record re-export in mod.rs, but this is the pair to confirm with `mise run rustdoc`. `[`read_entries`]` (1350, inside `read_log`'s doc) moves with its target. Note `session_pids` (1473-1499) and `plane_is_live` (1501-1511) deliberately stay in mod.rs — they are the plane's directory lifecycle, called by `start` (620), not a wire reader. Cyclic risk: none.

### Autres observations

A SEVERED DOC BLOCK, ALREADY PRESENT. task.rs:1313-1315 is a three-line `///` block ("The in-cage `bin` directories for `task`'s declared mise tools, or `None` when it declares none…") attached to `impl TaskEngine {` at 1316, not to `pool_bins` at 1317, which is what it actually describes. This is precisely the failure mode `every_module_level_item_carries_a_doc_comment` was written for (src/docs_coverage.rs:1039-1050: "a refactor moves, renames or deletes an item and leaves its `///` block behind"), and it slips the guard because `impl` is not in the item regex at src/docs_coverage.rs:961-963 and `pool_bins` is indented so it is never scanned. Fix while splitting: move the block down onto `fn pool_bins`. Note the second `impl TaskEngine` block (1316) exists only because of this stray doc; once spawn.rs and registry.rs take 1376-1779 out of it, what remains (`pool_bins`, `output_root`, `claim_output`) can fold back into the first impl block.

TEST-ONLY CODE INSIDE THE PRODUCTION BODY. task.rs:2291-2366 is a `#[cfg(test)] impl TaskEngine` (`inventory_with_launcher`, `inventory_only`, `with_launcher`, `with_tree`) sitting between `substitute` (2257-2289) and `credential_needles` (2381), so the production run of the file is interrupted twice. It cannot move into `task/tests.rs` unchanged, because `task_control`'s tests call `TaskEngine::inventory_only` (task_control.rs:2167 region) and link to it at task_control.rs:3036 — but it can move to the end of task/mod.rs, immediately before the two `mod` declarations, which makes production contiguous at no cost. Similarly `read_log` (task_control.rs:1349-1357) is `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate)` production-file code whose only caller is the test module; it belongs in task_control/tests.rs.

NEAR-IDENTICAL BLOCKS, WORTH NAMING BUT NOT ALL WORTH COLLAPSING.
- task_control.rs:660-690 and 691-725: the two accept loops in `start` are structurally identical (clone the Arcs, `ConnCap::new(MAX_CONCURRENT_CONNS)`, spawn, `for stream in …incoming()`, `accept_backoff` on error, `cap.take()` or drop, spawn per connection). A `fn accept_loop(listener, cap, handler)` taking a closure would collapse ~50 lines. But the two comment blocks are deliberately different and one of them says so in the code ("The same as the crossing socket above, and worth saying twice"), and the second loop clones one extra Arc (`results`). Judgement: leave it. The duplication is 30 lines of boilerplate carrying 20 lines of distinct argument.
- task.rs:524-563: `ensure_pool` and `upgrade_pool` are the same 18-line shape differing only in which `taskpool::` function is called and the return type (`PoolOutcome` vs `Option<InstallRun>`). A shared private `fn pool_call<T>(&self, f: impl Fn(...) -> io::Result<T>, empty: T)` would collapse ~15 lines; the two `format!("{}-task-pool", self.slug)` and the six-argument prefix are the real duplication. Worth doing, low value.
