- task_control.rs:964-971 and 1003-1010: `serve_run` and `serve_detach` open with an identical `read_payloads` + `admit_quota` preamble (8 lines). Collapsing it would need a helper returning `Option<(Payloads, u64)>`; marginal.
- task_control.rs:936-956 (`finished`) and 1149-1170 (`refusal`) are two `LogEntry` literals with the same four zeroed stamp fields and the same comment ("Both stamped by `TaskLog::push`, which is their authority; zero until it runs"). They belong beside each other in record.rs, which the proposal does. A `LogEntry::unstamped(seq, task)` constructor would remove the repeated comment entirely.

NOTHING BELONGS IN A DIFFERENT EXISTING MODULE. In particular, do not move `spawn_policy` (task.rs:1705-1779) into `proc_policy` or `proc_enforce`: `crate::proc_policy` is the pure policy type and `proc_enforce` is the supervisor, while `spawn_policy` is the *task engine's* translation of a declaration into in-cage paths using the engine's own mount list, `PATH` and project root — it cannot exist without `TaskEngine`'s fields. Likewise `credential_needles` (task.rs:2381-2405) is called from `egress.rs` tests (2978/2994/3037) but stays in the task module: it applies the task-side spelling rule and the `[redact] min_len` floor per encoding variant, which the wire-injection path deliberately does differently.

TASKPOOL.RS AND TASK_SHIM.RS: LEAVE BOTH. taskpool.rs (1,006; ~580 production) is one subject with one entry point pair (`ensure` 250, `upgrade` 337) and a small pure core (`split_version` 86, `version_dir` 105, `recorded_specs` 151, `wanted_spec` 180, `bins_for` 198). task_shim.rs (630; ~370 production) is one `format!` template (`render` 46-381) plus a nine-line `write` (387-397); splitting a generated script's text from the function that writes it would be pure loss. Neither is near a size problem.

ORDER OF WORK, IF ONLY PART IS DONE: the two `mod tests;` moves and `smoke_tests.rs` first — they are ~4,100 lines for one required edit (docs_coverage.rs:937) and zero visibility change. Then spawn.rs and record.rs, which are the two clusters a reader most often needs whole. registry.rs, skeleton.rs, client.rs and host.rs are real but smaller wins; skip them before skipping the first four.

---

## `allowlist` — src/allowlist/mod.rs (4089 lines), src/allowlist/grammar.rs

**Verdict : **Découpage utile****

### Cartographie actuelle

`src/allowlist/` is the authorization surface for network egress: it turns declared config entries into typed match rules, and answers "is this request permitted?" on three planes (inspected-over-TLS, inspected-cleartext, raw L4 splice). It is consumed by the config layer (classify + display + warnings), the filtering proxy (live verdicts), and `sbx test net`.

`src/allowlist/grammar.rs` (574 lines) is ALREADY the parse seam and needs no change. Its own header says so: "Turns text into the matcher's data model; the matching itself lives in [`super`]." It holds `Slot` (12-33), `classify` (41), `classify_in` (53), `split_scheme` (111), `split_method_prefix` (137), `parse_methods` (165), `parse_default_methods` (192), `classify_kind` (217), `reject_catch_all` (259), `scheme_of` (295), `has_explicit_port` (310), `split_host_ports` (328), `parse_ports` (362), `parse_port` (395), `parse_url_target` (414), `parse_tcp_target` (471), `parse_path_rule` (530), `is_valid_hostname` (561). It carries no `#[cfg(test)]` block — all of its tests live in mod.rs.

`src/allowlist/mod.rs` (4089 lines) is 57% tests. Its internal sections, verified by line:
- 1-80: module header doc (the rule-syntax reference; the only intra-doc links are `[`regex`]` at 66 and `[`Request::canonical_url`]` at 72).
- 82-88: `use std::fmt` / `use std::net::IpAddr` / `use regex::Regex`, `mod grammar;`, `pub(crate) use grammar::*;`.
- 91-369 THE RULE DATA MODEL: `Rule` + hand-written `PartialEq`/`Eq` (91-120), `Layer` + `default_port`/`inspected` (122-174), `RuleKind` enum (176-205), `Methods` + `admits`/`admits_deny`/`prefix` (206-287), `Ports` + `Default`/`single`/`admits`/`intersects`/`render_suffix` (289-369).
- 371-439 THE REQUEST: `Request` (371-413) and `Request::new` (415-439) — the canonicalize-once anti-evasion keystone.
- 441-560 THE MATCH PREDICATES: `impl Rule { concrete_host 447, matches 459, matches_deny 465, matches_mute 475, opens_every_host 493 }` (441-505); `impl RuleKind { matches 513, matches_any_port 542 }` (507-560).
- 562-680 CANONICALIZATION PRIMITIVES: `path_matches` (568), `canonical_segments` (587), `percent_decode` (606), `hex_val` (626), `apex_or_subdomain` (644), `canonical_host` (660), `display_host` (675).
- 682-711: `PartialEq`/`Eq for RuleKind`.
- 713-773 RENDER: `Display for Rule` (721-741), `impl RuleKind { render }` (743-767), `Display for RuleKind` (769-773).
- 775-952 POLICY VALUE TYPES: `DefaultAction` (775-800), `WebsocketSecret` + `parse` (802-827), `Http2Host` + `parse`/`display`/`matches` (829-913), the four `DEFAULT_*` consts (915-952).
- 954-1656 THE POLICY: `EgressPolicy` struct (954-1013), `Default` (1015-1026), and one 629-line `impl` (1028-1656) that splits cleanly in two at line 1370/1371: a SETTINGS half (1029-1370 — `new`, `with_rules`, `settings_dropped_from`, and 30 more builders/accessors, every one of whose doc says some form of "never a verdict") and a VERDICT half (1372-1655 — `muted` 1384, `permits` 1407, `explain` 1435, `method_denied` 1480, `explain_clear` 1506, `method_denied_clear` 1535, `l4_decision` 1572, `splices_any` 1601, `apply_default_methods` 1617, `l4_l7_conflicts` 1637).
- 1658-1740: `exact_host_ports` (1661), `L4Decision` (1672), `Decision` (1692), `rule_matches` (1717), `host_port_rule` (1727).
- 1742-4089: one `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` block, 2348 lines, ~95 tests plus four fixtures (`rule` 1746, `every_setting` 1865, `allow` 2018, `deny_with_allow_all` 3074).

Call graph: `grammar` → (`Rule`/`RuleKind`/`Ports::single`/`Layer::default_port`/`canonical_host`/`is_valid_hostname`); `Rule`/`RuleKind` match predicates → (`Request`, `path_matches`, `apex_or_subdomain`); `EgressPolicy` verdicts → (`Request::new`, `Rule::matches{,_deny,_mute}`, `RuleKind::matches`, `Methods::admits`, `Layer::inspected`, `Ports::intersects`, `exact_host_ports`); `Http2Host::matches` → `apex_or_subdomain`; render → (`Methods::prefix`, `Ports::render_suffix`, `Layer::default_port`, `display_host`). The layering is already acyclic: canon ← rule ← matching ← policy, with grammar hanging off rule+canon.

### Justification

Worthwhile, but the reason is not the 4089 lines — it is that 2348 of them are tests and the parse seam the task asks about is already cut. Extracting `mod tests` alone takes mod.rs to ~1745 lines with literally zero visibility, doc-link or guard changes, following a convention the repo already uses twice (`src/config/mod.rs:5841`, `src/sandbox/proxy/mod.rs:2059` both declare `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` against an external file). That is the whole first stage and it is nearly free.

The remaining ~1740 lines then split along three seams the code itself already draws:

1. `canon.rs` (562-680) is a true leaf: seven pure string/segment functions with no dependency on `Rule`, `EgressPolicy`, or anything else in the module. `canonical_host`'s own doc names the property — "Applied on both sides — the rule (`parse_url_target`) and the request (`Request::new`)" — and `apex_or_subdomain`'s says "One definition for the three places that ask it". These are shared by parse, match, render and the http2 host list. Putting them in one leaf file makes the anti-evasion normal form auditable in isolation.

2. `matching.rs` (371-439, 453-560, 1709-1719) is the security keystone the module header spends 40 lines on: `Request` canonicalized once, then the rule-level predicates. ~200 lines that answer exactly one question. Separating it from the data model means `impl Rule` lives in two files and `impl RuleKind` in two — a real, acknowledged cost — but `Rule`'s shape is 30 lines and its behaviour is the part anyone audits.

3. `policy.rs` (775-1707) is the largest new file at ~930 lines and is genuinely one thing: a policy value plus the three-plane verdict. `explain`, `explain_clear` and `l4_decision` are deliberately co-designed (each doc cross-references the other two on deny-wins and opt-in asymmetry) and must NOT be separated from each other; this proposal keeps them together.

What I am NOT proposing, and why: (a) render/serialize IS separable — `Display for Rule`, `RuleKind::render`, `Display for RuleKind`, `Methods::prefix`, `Ports::render_suffix`, `display_host` form a closed cluster and are the exact inverse of `classify` — but it totals ~100 lines and extracting it fragments `impl Ports` and `impl Methods` across two files each, for a type with three methods. Keep it as a contiguous, labelled section at the bottom of `rule.rs` instead. (b) Splitting the `EgressPolicy` inherent impl at 1370/1371 is tempting and defensible but is a second-order move; see other_observations. (c) `grammar.rs` should be left exactly as it is.

### Découpage proposé

#### `src/allowlist/tests.rs` (~2348 lignes)

**Rôle.** The module's entire test suite, moved out of mod.rs verbatim. Do this first and independently of everything else — it is the only stage with essentially no cost.

**Ce qui bouge.** The whole body of `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` at src/allowlist/mod.rs:1742-4089 (the inner lines 1744-4088, keeping the leading `use super::*;`). mod.rs keeps a two-line `#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests;` declaration in its place. Includes the four fixtures `rule` (1746), `every_setting` (1865), `allow` (2018), `deny_with_allow_all` (3074) and ~95 `#[test]` fns.

**Risque.** Lowest of any move here. VISIBILITY: none. `allowlist::tests` remains a direct child of `allowlist`, so it keeps its access to every private item in mod.rs exactly as today — including the three private things the tests actually touch: `apex_or_subdomain` (used at 1987), `percent_decode` (2998-3000), and `Http2Host::matches` (4055-4074). DOC LINKS: none break; test docs resolve through `use super::*` as now, and rustdoc does not build `cfg(test)` modules anyway. DOCS-COVERAGE GUARD: safe, and verified — `src/docs_coverage.rs:995-997` skips any file named `tests.rs` or `*_tests.rs`, so the four non-`#[test]` fixture fns do not suddenly become module-level items needing doc comments (they would, in any other filename). CYCLES: none. CAVEAT: if this move is done AFTER the other four, the fixtures' access to now-private-to-a-sibling items needs the `pub(super)` markings listed under rule.rs/canon.rs — so do this stage FIRST, in its own commit, and the later stages' `pub(super)` list already accounts for it.

#### `src/allowlist/canon.rs` (~130 lignes)

**Rôle.** The canonicalization primitives — the single normal form that the parser, the matcher, the renderer and the http2 host list all reduce to. A leaf module: no `Rule`, no `EgressPolicy`, no policy types, nothing but strings and segments.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/allowlist/mod.rs:562-680 verbatim: `path_matches` (doc 562-567, fn 568-585), `canonical_segments` (587-604), `percent_decode` (606-624), `hex_val` (626-642), `apex_or_subdomain` (644-658), `canonical_host` (660-674), `display_host` (675-680). Nothing stays behind.

**Risque.** VISIBILITY: four items need `pub(super)` (which grants exactly today's reach — visible in `allowlist` and its descendants — so nothing widens): `path_matches` (called by `RuleKind::matches`/`matches_any_port` in matching.rs), `percent_decode` (called by `Request::new` and by tests.rs), `apex_or_subdomain` (called by `RuleKind::matches`/`matches_any_port`, by `Http2Host::matches`, and by tests.rs at 1987), `display_host` (called by `Request::new` and by `RuleKind::render`). `hex_val` stays private. `canonical_segments` and `canonical_host` are already `pub(crate)` and must be re-exported from mod.rs (`crate::allowlist::canonical_segments` is imported by src/sandbox/netlearn.rs:45; `crate::allowlist::canonical_host` by grammar.rs, and linked from src/sandbox/proxy/inject.rs:329). NOTE: a `pub(super)` item CANNOT be `pub(crate) use`-re-exported (E0365), so consumers must write an explicit `use super::canon::{path_matches, display_host, percent_decode, apex_or_subdomain};` rather than relying on a glob — which is also what keeps the intra-doc link `[`path_matches`]` at mod.rs:509 resolving. DOC LINKS: none inside this block point outward. DOCS-COVERAGE GUARD: all seven already carry doc comments, so no new `UNDOCUMENTED_MODULE_ITEMS` entry is needed. CYCLES: impossible — this module imports nothing from its siblings.

#### `src/allowlist/rule.rs` (~425 lignes)

**Rôle.** The rule data model and everything a rule can say about its own shape: the five kinds, the port sets, the method sets, the layer, structural equality, and rendering back to the text `classify` accepts. No `Request`, no verdict.

**Ce qui bouge.** From src/allowlist/mod.rs: `Rule` + `PartialEq`/`Eq for Rule` (91-120); `Layer` + `impl Layer { default_port 161, inspected 171 }` (122-174); the `RuleKind` enum declaration (176-205); `Methods` + `impl Methods { admits 250, admits_deny 271, prefix 280 }` (206-287); `Ports` + `Default for Ports` + `impl Ports { single 319, admits 324, intersects 334, render_suffix 348 }` (289-369); `Rule::concrete_host` (441-452, as its own `impl Rule` block); `PartialEq`/`Eq for RuleKind` (682-711); the render cluster `Display for Rule` / `impl RuleKind { render }` / `Display for RuleKind` (713-773), kept contiguous at the bottom of the file under a section comment rather than given a file of its own; `exact_host_ports` (1658-1669); `host_port_rule` (1721-1740).

**Risque.** VISIBILITY: six `pub(super)` markings, each preserving today's exact reach — `Layer::default_port` (161, called by grammar.rs:69 and by `Display for Rule` at 736), `Layer::inspected` (171, called by `apply_default_methods` 1642 and `l4_l7_conflicts` 1646 in policy.rs), `Methods::admits` (250, called by `method_denied` 1487 and `method_denied_clear` 1557 in policy.rs, and by `Rule::matches`/`matches_mute` in matching.rs), `Ports::single` (319, called by grammar.rs:341/348/357), `Ports::intersects` (334, called by `l4_l7_conflicts` 1653), `Ports::admits` (324, called by `RuleKind::matches` in matching.rs). `Methods::prefix`, `Ports::render_suffix` and `RuleKind::render` are called only from the render cluster in this same file and stay private. `Rule`'s fields are already `pub(crate)` (grammar.rs builds `Rule` literals). DOC LINKS: `[`EgressPolicy::apply_default_methods`]` at 259 and `[`classify`]` at 719 now point out of the file — both resolve if this file opens with `use super::*;` (a glob, which never triggers `unused_imports`), exactly as grammar.rs already does. `[`Ports::single`]` (308), `[`Self::admits_deny`]` (245ish), `[`Rule::group`]`, `[`Methods::Any/Only/Unspecified`]`, `[`Layer::L7/L7Clear/L4`]` all stay in-file. DOCS-COVERAGE GUARD: every moved module-level item is documented; no new grandfather entry. CYCLES: depends on canon (`display_host`) and, for doc links only, on policy — no runtime cycle. `impl Rule` and `impl RuleKind` will each exist in two files (here and matching.rs); that is legal and `[`Self::…`]` links resolve across inherent impl blocks, but it is the real legibility cost of this proposal and should be stated in each file's header.

#### `src/allowlist/matching.rs` (~200 lignes)

**Rôle.** The rule-level match: build the one canonicalized view of a request, then answer whether a given rule matches it. The evasion-proof keystone, isolated so it can be audited on its own.

**Ce qui bouge.** From src/allowlist/mod.rs: `Request` struct + doc (371-413) and `impl Request { new }` (415-439); an `impl Rule` block carrying `matches` (453-461), `matches_deny` (463-467), `matches_mute` (469-481) and `opens_every_host` (483-505); `impl RuleKind { matches 508-540, matches_any_port 536-560 }` (507-560); `rule_matches` (1709-1719).

**Risque.** VISIBILITY: three `pub(super)` markings, all preserving today's reach — `Rule::matches` (459, called by `explain` 1449 and `explain_clear` 1518 in policy.rs), `Rule::matches_deny` (465, called by `explain` 1441), `Rule::matches_mute` (475, called by `muted` 1404), `RuleKind::matches` (513, called by `method_denied` 1487, `method_denied_clear` 1557, `l4_decision` 1578/1583, and by `rule_matches` in this file). `RuleKind::matches_any_port` (542) is called only from `Rule::matches_mute` in this same file and stays private. `Request` is `pub(crate)` today; grep confirms nothing outside `allowlist` names it, so it can be narrowed to `pub(super)` — BUT see the doc-link note. `Request`'s fields (`host`, `port`, `segs`, `url`, `canonical_url`) are read by `RuleKind::matches` in this same file, so they stay private with no change. `Rule::opens_every_host` and `rule_matches` stay `pub(crate)` (used by src/config/view.rs:530/558/590 and by the proxy). DOC LINKS: THE ONE REAL BREAK IS mod.rs:72 — the module header says `([`Request::canonical_url`] — …)`, and once `Request` leaves mod.rs that link resolves to nothing, which `mise run rustdoc` denies. Fix by adding `pub(crate) use matching::Request;` to mod.rs (which also settles the visibility question) or by rewriting it as `[`Request::canonical_url`](matching::Request::canonical_url)`. Everything else resolves through `use super::*;`: `[`Slot`]` at 480 (re-exported from grammar), `[`path_matches`]` at 509 (explicit `use super::canon::path_matches;`, which this file needs for the call anyway), `[`Rule::matches_mute`]` at 538, `[`RuleKind::matches`]` at 458, `[`RuleKind::matches_any_port`]` at 470, `[`Self::segs`]`/`[`Self::url`]` in the `Request` doc. DOCS-COVERAGE GUARD: `Request` and `rule_matches` are documented; the `impl` blocks are skipped by the guard. CYCLES: depends on rule + canon only.

#### `src/allowlist/policy.rs` (~930 lignes)

**Rôle.** The policy: the classified allow/deny/mute lists plus every `[network]` setting, and the three-plane verdict (inspected-over-TLS, inspected-cleartext, raw L4 splice) plus the derived reports the config layer surfaces.

**Ce qui bouge.** From src/allowlist/mod.rs, in one contiguous run: `DefaultAction` (775-800); `WebsocketSecret` + `parse` (802-827); `Http2Host` + `impl { parse 866, display 890, matches 905 }` (829-913); the four consts `DEFAULT_DNS_CACHE_TTL`/`DEFAULT_IDLE_TIMEOUT`/`DEFAULT_MAX_CONNECTIONS`/`DEFAULT_BODY_MAX` (915-952); the `EgressPolicy` struct (954-1013) and `Default for EgressPolicy` (1015-1026); the whole `impl EgressPolicy` (1028-1656); `L4Decision` (1670-1688) and `Decision` (1689-1707). `exact_host_ports` is its only outbound helper and goes to rule.rs (it is a pure `RuleKind` projection).

**Risque.** VISIBILITY: one `pub(super)` — `Http2Host::matches` (905), because tests.rs exercises it directly at 4055-4074 and is now a sibling rather than a child. Everything else this file calls is covered by the `pub(super)` markings listed under rule.rs/matching.rs/canon.rs. `EgressPolicy`'s own fields stay private (only this file reads them, including the exhaustive destructure in `settings_dropped_from` at 1084-1105, which is a deliberate compile-time tripwire and must not be weakened). DOC LINKS: all resolve through `use super::*;` — `[`Rule::matches_mute`]` (1376), `[`RuleKind::matches`]` (1561), `[`Rule`]`/`[`RuleKind`]`/`[`Methods::…`]`/`[`Layer::…`]`, the ten `[`Self::explain`]` and other `Self::` links (which resolve on the type, not the impl block), and `[`CaptureLevel::Off`](crate::sandbox::control::CaptureLevel::Off)` which is already fully qualified. Every external link INTO this module — src/sandbox/proxy/splice.rs:43, forward.rs:20, mod.rs:29/61, ctx.rs:159/164/168, src/cli/test.rs:415 — is written as `crate::allowlist::…` and keeps resolving through mod.rs's `pub(crate) use`. DOCS-COVERAGE GUARD: THIS ONE FAILS UNLESS TOUCHED. `src/docs_coverage.rs:857` grandfathers `("allowlist/mod.rs", "EgressPolicy")`, and the guard's stale-check (docs_coverage.rs:1082-1092) fails when an entry no longer applies. Two options: re-key it to `("allowlist/policy.rs", "EgressPolicy")`, or — better, and what the guard is asking for — fix the underlying defect described in other_observations and delete the entry. CYCLES: depends on rule + matching + canon; nothing depends on it, so the layering stays acyclic.

### Autres observations

1. A REAL DEFECT THE SPLIT WOULD SURFACE AND FIX. `EgressPolicy`'s type doc has been severed from its type. mod.rs:829-836 ("A classified egress policy: an allow list, a deny list, and a default action…") is the first paragraph of the doc block that now sits on `Http2Host` (struct at line 850) — `Http2Host`'s own prose starts mid-block at line 837 ("A `[network] http2` entry: …"). `EgressPolicy` itself, at line 955, has no doc comment at all: line 954 is a bare `#[derive]` preceded by the end of `DEFAULT_BODY_MAX` at 952. This is exactly the failure mode `every_module_level_item_carries_a_doc_comment` was written for (`src/docs_coverage.rs:1042-1055` describes it verbatim), and it is currently papered over by the grandfather entry at docs_coverage.rs:857. The fix is a clean cut at 836/837: move 829-836 down to sit above line 954, and delete the entry from `UNDOCUMENTED_MODULE_ITEMS`. Worth doing whether or not the split happens.

2. mod.rs's `use` lines are load-bearing for grammar.rs and will break silently. `grammar.rs` has exactly one import — `use super::*;` at line 6 — yet it uses `IpAddr` (grammar.rs:231, 235, 342, 458, 486, 546) and `Regex::new` (grammar.rs:57). Those names reach it through mod.rs:83 (`use std::net::IpAddr;`) and mod.rs:85 (`use regex::Regex;`), which are private `use` bindings glob-imported by the child. Once mod.rs's code moves out, mod.rs itself no longer references `fmt`, `IpAddr` or `Regex`, and `cargo clippy -- -D warnings` will reject them as unused. Add `use std::net::IpAddr;` and `use regex::Regex;` to grammar.rs (and to rule.rs/matching.rs/policy.rs as each needs them, plus `use std::fmt;` in rule.rs for the `Display` impls), and delete mod.rs:82-85.

3. Use explicit re-export lists, not globs, for the new modules. mod.rs should end up as: the 1-80 header, then `mod canon; mod grammar; mod matching; mod policy; mod rule; #[cfg(test)] mod tests;`, then `pub(crate) use grammar::*;` (unchanged — it is the existing contract, and 51 call sites reach `allowlist::classify` through it) plus explicit lists for the rest: `pub(crate) use canon::{canonical_host, canonical_segments};`, `pub(crate) use matching::{Request, rule_matches};`, `pub(crate) use policy::{DEFAULT_BODY_MAX, DEFAULT_DNS_CACHE_TTL, DEFAULT_IDLE_TIMEOUT, DEFAULT_MAX_CONNECTIONS, Decision, DefaultAction, EgressPolicy, Http2Host, L4Decision, WebsocketSecret};`, `pub(crate) use rule::{Layer, Methods, Ports, Rule, RuleKind, host_port_rule};`. Five globs feeding five `use super::*;` children is the one way this split can bite: two modules exporting the same identifier becomes an E0659 ambiguity at the use site. Explicit lists also make the module's crate-facing surface readable in one screen. All 25 distinct `allowlist::X` paths used elsewhere in the crate are covered by that list — I enumerated them.

4. There is no cyclic-dependency risk at the language level (Rust permits mutual references between sibling modules in a crate), and the intended layering is acyclic anyway: canon ← {rule, grammar} ← matching ← policy. Worth writing into each new file's header so it does not decay.

5. A SECOND-ORDER SPLIT I AM NOT RECOMMENDING YET, but which is real: `impl EgressPolicy` (1028-1656) cleanly divides at line 1370/1371. The settings half (1029-1370, ~340 lines) is `new`, `with_rules`, `settings_dropped_from`, and 30 builder/accessor pairs whose docs say over and over "never a verdict" — `pool` (1249), `ca_roots` (1300), `capture_level` (1318), `http2_hosts` (1348), `speaks_http2` (1356). The verdict half (1372-1655, ~285 lines) is the security-relevant code. If the dilution ever becomes a problem, the mechanical move is `src/allowlist/policy/verdict.rs` as a CHILD of `policy` (not a sibling), which needs zero visibility changes at all: a descendant module sees its ancestor's private fields, so `muted`/`explain`/`explain_clear`/`l4_decision`/`method_denied`/`method_denied_clear`/`splices_any`/`apply_default_methods`/`l4_l7_conflicts` can read `self.allow`/`self.deny`/`self.mute`/`self.default_action` unchanged. I would not do it in the same pass as the primary split.

6. `permits` (mod.rs:1407, with `#[allow(dead_code)]` at 1406) is genuinely test-only, but across three test modules — 87 call sites in allowlist's own tests, plus src/sandbox/proxy/tests.rs:6752-6826 and src/config/tests.rs:6938-6945 — so it must stay `pub(crate)`. `#[cfg(test)]` would be more honest than `#[allow(dead_code)]` and would let the allowance go; it compiles for all three consumers. Minor, orthogonal to the split.

7. RENDER, THE AXIS THE TASK ASKS ABOUT, IN FULL. The cluster is `Display for Rule` (721-741), `RuleKind::render` (743-767), `Display for RuleKind` (769-773), `Methods::prefix` (280-286), `Ports::render_suffix` (348-368), `display_host` (675-680), `Http2Host::display` (890-902) — about 100 lines, closed (nothing else calls `prefix`, `render`, or `render_suffix`), and the exact inverse of `classify`, a contract two tests pin (`display_round_trips_each_kind` at 3423, `a_method_prefix_round_trips_through_display` at 3523). It is separable. I am still not proposing a file for it: extracting it fragments `impl Ports` (3 remaining methods) and `impl Methods` (2 remaining) across two files each, and pulls `Http2Host::display` away from `Http2Host::parse`, for a file smaller than the module header. Keep it as the final, section-commented block of rule.rs, with the header naming grammar.rs as its inverse. If a later change makes rendering non-trivial — a machine-readable serialization beside the human one, say — revisit it then.

---

## `help-completion` — src/help.rs (3850 lines), src/cli/completion.rs (1851 lines)

**Verdict : **Découpage fortement recommandé****

### Cartographie actuelle

Two modules, both of which are "one table, read many ways" — but they are not the same shape internally.

**src/help.rs (3,850 lines)** is the CLI's single source of truth for usage text. It is ~79% flat data.
- 1–23: module doc (the WHY: one table, aliases resolved-but-never-offered, the deliberate seam that option prose duplicates each handler's parser).
- 25–29: `use` (std + `crate::style::Palette`).
- 31–44: the two types — `type Opt` (31–32), `struct Page` (34–44), both private, 5 private fields.
- 46–3089: `const PAGES: &[Page]` — 104 page literals, 3,035 lines. Verified group sizes: net 18 pages/653 lines, config 9/330, task 9/281, app 9/273, proc 10/268, plugins (incl. `plugins store`) 17/257, run 1/142, session 5/106, storage 1/98, projects 2/75, bundle 3/68, upgrade 1/65, fs 2/57, logs 1/51, gc 1/50, ssh-agent 2/45, plus 12 smaller namespaces. Five `// ---- … ----` banner comments (48, 1912, 2108, 2134, 2840, 2951) try to group it and have already drifted (`bundle` at 2173 sits under "net subcommands"; `projects show` at 2049 under "app subcommands"; most subcommand pages sit under "top-level commands").
- 3091–3125: `const ALIASES` — 20 alternate spellings.
- 3127–3197: the query layer — `canonical` (3127), `find` (3135), `children` (3141), `synopsis_of` (3153), `synopsis` (3158), `is_command` (3165), `is_command_path` (3171), `subcommands_of` (3178), `all_paths` (3188, `#[cfg(test)]`), `options_of` (3195).
- 3199–3337: the renderer, which touches no path logic at all — `item` (3200), `paint_synopsis` (3213), `paint_inline_code` (3241), `top_level` (3248), `render` (3278).
- 3339–3433: the entry points — `show` (3341), `resolve_path` (3364), `maybe_help` (3390), `dispatch` (3402), `top_level_usage` (3423), `page_usage` (3431).
- 3435–3850: `mod tests` — 12 tests plus 5 helpers, including `dispatched_alias_pairs` (3727–3792) and `DISPATCHERS` (3679–3691), a source-text scanner that reads `src/cli/*.rs` to prove every dispatched alias is declared in `ALIASES`.

Call graph: `find`/`children` read `PAGES`; everything public reads `find`/`children`; `render`/`top_level` read `Page` fields + `children` + `PAGES` and nothing else. The renderer is a leaf; the table is a leaf; nothing in the table knows the renderer exists.

**src/cli/completion.rs (1,851 lines)** is the completion oracle — ~40% tests, ~60% a genuinely coupled pipeline (words → command path → operand slot → `ValueKind` → candidates).
- 1–33: module doc + `use` (`crate::{config, diag, help, plugins, session}`, `plugins::stores`, `sandbox::proc_control`).
- 35–46: `SHELLS` (35–36), `DESC_WIDTH` (38–41), `FILES` (43–46).
- 48–75 / 77–108: `completion_cmd`, `complete_cmd` (the `__complete` protocol).
- 110–145: `insertable` — the shell-injection gate every candidate crosses, with a 20-line rationale.
- 139–234: `candidates` — the dispatcher of the whole file.
- 236–252: `flag_menu`.
- 254–540 ("Values." banner at 254): `ValueKind` (258–290), `RuleList` (292–297), `removal_list` (299–314), `rule_values` (316–330), `rule_files` (332–346), `value_candidates` (348–365), `registry_values` (367–498), `config_tokens` (500–519), `config_files` (521–527), `project_config_file` (529–534), `global_config_file` (536–540). This is the only I/O in the file, and it runs on a keystroke.
- 542–719 ("operand grammar" banner): `Operand` (546–557), `all_literal_words` (559–575), `operand_slots` (577–618), `metavar_of` (620–639), `optional_of` (641–655), `is_literal` (657–670), `kind_of_metavar` (672–712), `is_pending_page` (714–719).
- 721–825 ("cursor" banner): `PendingWord` (725–734), `cursor_value_kind` (736–825).
- 827–971 ("value of a flag" banner): `flag_value_kind` (831–867), `alternation_cells` (869–898), `flag_takes_value` (895–905), `flag_metavar_kind` (906–925), `flag_literals` (927–942), `flag_tail` (944–971).
- 973–1017 ("Flags." banner): `flag_names` (977–995), `describe` (997–1017).
- 1019–1097: the `BASH` and `ZSH` script constants.
- 1099–1851: `mod tests` — 4 word-list helpers plus 24 tests, several of which sweep `help::all_paths()` over the whole table.

Dependency direction is already strictly layered and acyclic: grammar (`operand_slots`, `metavar_of`, `flag_*`) → value types (`ValueKind`, `removal_list`); the registry readers (`registry_values`, `rule_values`, `config_tokens`) call nothing in the grammar half.

### Justification

The verdict is strong for **help.rs** and merely worthwhile for **completion.rs**; they are different cases.

help.rs is not one cohesive anything — it is a 3,035-line catalogue, a 139-line renderer that never touches path logic, a 71-line query layer, and a 416-line guard suite that mostly reads *other files' source text*. The engine a reader needs in order to understand how help works starts at line 3127, i.e. after 81% of the file. Nothing about the split touches the property the module doc actually guarantees: "one table is the single source of truth" is about there being exactly one `PAGES` slice with exactly one aggregation point, not about which file the literal sits in. Moving the const verbatim into `src/help/pages.rs` preserves that literally — one const, one definition site.

The cost is unusually low, and this is the load-bearing reason for the strong verdict: in Rust a child module can see its parent's *private* items **and their private fields**, so `src/help/pages.rs` can construct all 104 `Page { … }` literals with `Page`, `Opt` and every field left exactly as private as they are today. The only visibility change the whole help split needs is `pub(super)` on `PAGES` plus four items in the new `render.rs`. Exactly one intra-doc link in the crate is affected (`src/help.rs:3092`). The four links in the module header (`[\`Page\`]`, `[\`synopsis\`]`, `[\`maybe_help\`]`, `[\`ALIASES\`]` at lines 3, 6, 9, 15) and the two external ones (`src/cli/session.rs:16`, `src/docs_coverage.rs:638`) all name items that stay in `mod.rs` and are untouched. And the const sits at module level in both the old and new file, so rustfmt reproduces it byte-for-byte.

completion.rs is the weaker case and I want to be honest about it: 1,851 lines minus 753 lines of tests is a ~1,100-line body that really is one pipeline, and I am explicitly **not** proposing to fragment that pipeline. `candidates` → `cursor_value_kind` → `operand_slots`/`flag_value_kind` is a chain that reads best in one place, and `insertable` must stay welded to `complete_cmd` because its whole argument (lines 110–129) is that it is the *single* point every candidate crosses — moving it next to a producer would dissolve that seam. What I do propose is three cuts that follow seams the file already draws with banner comments and that the module doc already argues for: the 753-line test module out (matching `src/config/tests.rs`), the ~283-line registry-reading half out (the only I/O, the only part that touches the store layout, the session registry, the plugin registry and the proc control plane on a keystroke — worth being a file you can point at), and the two 40-line shell-script string constants out. The remaining `grammar.rs` cut is the one genuine cohesion win: it collects every function whose job is "parse a human-written option row from the help table", which is the code most likely to be edited when a page's grammar changes, and putting `kind_of_metavar` and `flag_metavar_kind` in one file exposes a near-duplicate table that is currently 240 lines apart.

What I am not recommending: sharding `PAGES` further by namespace. It is a real but modest win and it introduces the one new failure mode this design does not have today (a page group nobody aggregates). Design and guard are in other_observations if it is wanted later.

### Découpage proposé

#### `src/help/pages.rs` (~3040 lignes)

**Rôle.** Hold the page catalogue and nothing else, so that src/help/mod.rs is the ~250-line help engine rather than a 3,850-line file whose logic starts at line 3127.

**Ce qui bouge.** `const PAGES: &[Page]` verbatim — its doc comment at src/help.rs:46 through the closing `];` at src/help.rs:3089 (104 `Page { … }` literals, 3,035 lines), including the five `// ---- … ----` banner comments at 48, 1912, 2108, 2134, 2840, 2951. New file header: `use super::{Opt, Page};` and the declaration becomes `pub(super) const PAGES: &[Page]`. STAYS in src/help/mod.rs: `type Opt` (31–32) and `struct Page` (34–44) with all five fields left private, `const ALIASES` (3091–3125), and the whole query layer `canonical`/`find`/`children`/`synopsis_of`/`synopsis`/`is_command`/`is_command_path`/`subcommands_of`/`all_paths`/`options_of` (3127–3197).

**Risque.** Visibility: exactly one change — `PAGES` gains `pub(super)`. `Page` and `Opt` and every one of `Page`'s five fields stay private: a child module sees its parent's private items and private fields, so all 104 struct literals compile unchanged. Intra-doc: one link breaks — src/help.rs:3092, the `ALIASES` doc, says "Aliases stay out of [`PAGES`]"; mod.rs will carry `use pages::PAGES;` (needed by `find` at 3137 and `children` at 3143 anyway), which makes the bare link resolve again, but if that import is elided the link must be written `[`pages::PAGES`]` or `mise run rustdoc` fails. Nothing else: the module-header links at src/help.rs:3, 6, 9, 15 name `Page`/`synopsis`/`maybe_help`/`ALIASES`, all of which stay in mod.rs; src/cli/session.rs:16 and src/docs_coverage.rs:638 name `help::maybe_help` / `crate::help::subcommands_of`, both unmoved. Tests: `every_page_renders_balanced_in_color` (3496) and `top_level_commands_are_listed_alphabetically` (3826) iterate `PAGES` and go to src/help/tests.rs with an explicit `use super::pages::PAGES;`. Cycles: none — pages.rs imports only `super::{Opt, Page}` and is a pure leaf. docs-coverage: `module_level_items` (src/docs_coverage.rs:966) matches only column-0 `fn|struct|enum|trait|union`, so a bare `const` triggers no new doc requirement; document it anyway (it already has a doc comment to carry across). rustfmt: the const is at module level in both files, so the move is byte-identical and `cargo fmt --check` stays green. Real hazard: a botched copy silently drops a page — but `every_cli_verb_has_a_reference_page` (src/docs_coverage.rs:79), the alias tests, and tests/help.rs (which resolves every accepted path to a page) all fail loudly if one goes missing.

#### `src/help/render.rs` (~150 lignes)

**Rôle.** Isolate the presentation layer — ANSI painting and page layout — which reads Page fields and `children` and knows nothing about aliases, path resolution or dispatch.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/help.rs:3199–3337 verbatim: `item` (3200), `paint_synopsis` (3213), `paint_inline_code` (3241), `top_level` (3248), `render` (3278). New file header: `use super::pages::PAGES;`, `use super::{Page, children};`, `use crate::style::Palette;`. STAYS in mod.rs: `show` (3341), `resolve_path` (3364), `maybe_help` (3390), `dispatch` (3402), `top_level_usage` (3423), `page_usage` (3431) — the entry points that decide *which* page and *which* stream's palette, calling `render::render` / `render::top_level`.

**Risque.** Visibility: four items gain `pub(super)` — `render` and `top_level` (called from `show`, `page_usage`, `dispatch`, `top_level_usage` in mod.rs), plus `paint_synopsis` and `paint_inline_code` (called directly by the tests at src/help.rs:3504 and 3526). `item` stays private to render.rs. `children` (3141) and `Page` stay private in mod.rs and remain visible to `help::render` as a descendant module — no change there. Intra-doc: none. Verified by grep that the only `[`…`]` links in src/help.rs are at lines 3, 6, 9, 15, 3092, 3361, 3428, and none of them sits in or points into 3199–3337. Cycles: mod.rs → render.rs → mod.rs (for `Page`, `children`) is a parent/child pair, which Rust permits and which creates no crate-level cycle; render.rs → pages.rs is one-way. docs-coverage: all five fns already carry `///`, and src/help.rs has no entry in `UNDOCUMENTED_MODULE_ITEMS` (src/docs_coverage.rs:856), so `every_module_level_item_carries_a_doc_comment` stays green as long as the doc blocks travel with the fns — which is precisely the failure that test was written to catch, so move each `///` with its item, not separately.

#### `src/help/tests.rs` (~420 lignes)

**Rôle.** Move the 416-line guard suite out of the source file, matching the pattern already established by src/config/tests.rs and src/config/catalogue_tests.rs.

**Ce qui bouge.** The body of `mod tests` at src/help.rs:3435–3850, dedented by four spaces: helpers `assert_balanced` (3443), `names_command` (3555), `arm_names` (3659), `const DISPATCHERS` (3679), `body_lines` (3699), `dispatched_alias_pairs` (3727); tests `colored_render_balances_every_span`, `plain_render_emits_no_escapes`, `maybe_help_stops_at_a_double_dash`, `every_page_renders_balanced_in_color`, `paint_synopsis_wraps_only_metavariables`, `paint_inline_code_strips_backticks_and_wraps_the_content`, `the_alias_table_is_well_formed`, `every_alias_is_advertised_on_the_page_that_owns_it`, `an_alias_resolves_to_the_page_of_the_name_it_stands_for`, `every_dispatched_alias_is_declared_in_the_table`, `top_level_commands_are_listed_alphabetically`. src/help/mod.rs keeps only `#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests;`. File header: `use super::*;` plus `use super::pages::PAGES;` and `use super::render::{paint_inline_code, paint_synopsis, render, top_level};`.

**Risque.** The module path is unchanged (`crate::help::tests` either way), so no `super::`-depth arithmetic anywhere. `use super::*;` reaches mod.rs's private items (`find`, `children`, `ALIASES`, `Page`, `canonical`, `resolve_path`, `maybe_help`, `is_command`, `is_command_path`) exactly as the inline module does today; it does NOT reach items that now live in sibling child modules, hence the two explicit imports — omit them and you get `cannot find value PAGES` / `cannot find function render`, a compile error, not a silent pass. `dispatched_alias_pairs` reads `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")/src/cli` and `body_lines` scans for `fn <name>(` at column 0 in those files; nothing under src/help/ is in its path, so the `pairs.len() >= 19` floor at src/help.rs:3802 is untouched. docs-coverage: `module_level_items` skips files named exactly `tests.rs` (src/docs_coverage.rs:997), so `DISPATCHERS`, `body_lines` etc. incur no new doc requirement. Intra-doc: `mod tests` is `#[cfg(test)]`, which `cargo doc` does not enable, so its doc comments are never link-checked — but clippy `--all-targets` does compile it, so name resolution must still hold. Honest cost: 416 lines are re-indented, so `git blame` on the guard suite points at the move commit; src/config/tests.rs (11,317 lines) shows the project has already paid this price once.

#### `src/cli/completion/grammar.rs` (~345 lignes)

**Rôle.** Collect every function whose job is to read a human-written option row out of the help table — the operand grammar and the flag grammar, which today sit in two banner-separated blocks 100 lines apart despite being the same parsing problem, and which are what you edit when a page's synopsis changes.

**Ce qui bouge.** From src/cli/completion.rs, verbatim: the operand block 546–719 — `enum Operand` (546–557), `all_literal_words` (559–575), `operand_slots` (577–618), `metavar_of` (620–639), `optional_of` (641–655), `is_literal` (657–670), `kind_of_metavar` (672–712), `is_pending_page` (714–719); the flag-value block 831–971 — `flag_value_kind` (831–867), `alternation_cells` (869–893), `flag_takes_value` (895–905), `flag_metavar_kind` (906–925), `flag_literals` (927–942), `flag_tail` (944–971); and `flag_names` (977–995) from the "Flags." block. Keep the two banner comments as section headers inside the new file. File header: `use super::values::{RuleList, ValueKind, removal_list};` and `use crate::help;`. STAYS in mod.rs: `describe` (997–1017) with its `DESC_WIDTH` (38–41) — it formats a candidate line, it does not parse a row.

**Risque.** Visibility: ten items gain `pub(super)` — `Operand` (variants inherit), `all_literal_words`, `operand_slots`, `kind_of_metavar`, `flag_value_kind`, `flag_takes_value`, `flag_names`, `metavar_of`, `flag_metavar_kind`, `flag_tail` (the last four only because the tests at lines 1739, 1754, 1730/1570 and 1731 call them directly). `optional_of`, `is_literal`, `alternation_cells`, `flag_literals`, `is_pending_page` stay private to grammar.rs. Path depth: exactly one line changes — `flag_literals` at line 935 reads `super::logs::FEED_NAMES`, which from `cli::completion::grammar` must become `crate::cli::logs::FEED_NAMES` (or `super::super::logs::FEED_NAMES`). Intra-doc: `flag_takes_value`'s doc at line 897 links `[`flag_value_kind`]`; both move together into the same file, so it still resolves. Cycles: grammar → values only (`ValueKind`, `RuleList`, `removal_list`); values calls nothing in grammar — verified, `registry_values`/`rule_values`/`config_tokens` reference no grammar symbol. mod.rs → grammar is the third edge; no cycle. docs-coverage: every moved fn already carries a `///`; carry each block with its item or `every_module_level_item_carries_a_doc_comment` fires.

#### `src/cli/completion/values.rs` (~295 lignes)

**Rôle.** Name the file that does I/O on a keystroke. Everything here reads the machine — the store layout, the session registry, the plugin registry, the signed catalogues, the config files, and each live session's proc control plane — under the constraints the module doc spells out (resolve the layout without mounting; a slow session drops out rather than stalling the prompt; never write to stderr). It is worth being able to point at that surface as one file.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/cli/completion.rs:254–540 verbatim, banner included: `enum ValueKind` (258–290), `enum RuleList` (292–297), `removal_list` (299–314), `rule_values` (316–330), `rule_files` (332–346), `value_candidates` (348–365), `registry_values` (367–498), `config_tokens` (500–519), `config_files` (521–527), `project_config_file` (529–534), `global_config_file` (536–540). File header: `use super::FILES;`, `use crate::plugins::stores;`, `use crate::sandbox::proc_control;`, `use crate::{config, plugins, session};`, `use std::path::PathBuf;`. STAYS in mod.rs: `FILES` (43–46) and `insertable` (110–145) — `FILES` is the protocol token both the oracle and the emitted scripts agree on, and `insertable` is deliberately the single gate every candidate crosses (its own doc, 110–129, argues exactly that it must not be pushed down into the producers).

**Risque.** Visibility: six items gain `pub(super)` — `ValueKind` (variants inherit; called from mod.rs's `candidates`/`cursor_value_kind` and from grammar.rs), `RuleList` (must, or `ValueKind::Rules { which: RuleList }` trips rustc's `private_interfaces` lint, which `-D warnings` turns into a build failure), `removal_list` (called by `kind_of_metavar` in grammar.rs), `value_candidates` (called at mod.rs lines 194 and 204), `registry_values` and `config_tokens` (called by the tests at 1478/1485/1512 and 1507). `rule_values`, `rule_files`, `config_files`, `project_config_file`, `global_config_file` stay private. Intra-doc: `ValueKind::Files`' doc at line 287 says `(see [`FILES`])`; `use super::FILES;` is required by `value_candidates` at line 356 anyway, and that import is what keeps the bare link resolving — drop the import and `mise run rustdoc` fails. `registry_values`' link to `[`crate::store::Layout::from_env_without_mounting`]` (line 372) is an absolute path and is unaffected. Cycles: values.rs imports one name from its parent (`FILES`) and nothing from grammar.rs; grammar.rs and mod.rs import from values.rs. Parent/child edges only, no cycle. docs-coverage: all eleven items already carry `///`.

#### `src/cli/completion/scripts.rs` (~90 lignes)

**Rôle.** Park the two shell scripts, which are 80 lines of embedded bash and zsh with their own commentary and are edited on a completely different cadence from the Rust around them.

**Ce qui bouge.** `const SHELLS` (src/cli/completion.rs:35–36), `const BASH` (1013–1045, doc comment included) and `const ZSH` (1047–1097). File header: none needed beyond the doc comments already on each. STAYS in mod.rs: `completion_cmd` (48–75), which now reads `scripts::SHELLS` — the shell-name lookup, the unsupported-shell diagnosis, and the `supported shells:` hint are argument handling, not script text.

**Risque.** Visibility: `SHELLS` and `ZSH` gain `pub(super)` (`SHELLS` for `completion_cmd` and the test at 1838; `ZSH` for `the_zsh_script_escapes_a_colon_inside_a_candidate`, lines 1811–1832). `BASH` is referenced only by `SHELLS` inside the same file and stays private. Intra-doc: one link must be rewritten — `BASH`'s doc at line 1019 says "Files appear only where the [`FILES`] marker asks for them", and scripts.rs deliberately does NOT import `FILES` (the scripts embed the literal string `__sbx_files__`), so it must become `[`super::FILES`]`. `ZSH`'s doc mentions `the_zsh_script_escapes_a_colon_inside_a_candidate` in plain backticks, not as an intra-doc link, so it is unaffected. Cycles: none — scripts.rs imports nothing. docs-coverage: `module_level_items` matches only `fn|struct|enum|trait|union` at column 0, so a file of three consts adds no doc obligations. Note the invariant the split must preserve: the scripts and `FILES` (43–46) are two halves of one protocol; the test `every_emitted_script_names_the_completion_entry_point` (1834) and the integration drives in tests/completion.rs are what hold them together, and both keep working across the file boundary.

#### `src/cli/completion/tests.rs` (~750 lignes)

**Rôle.** Move the 753-line test module — 41% of the file — out of the way, matching src/config/tests.rs.

**Ce qui bouge.** The body of `mod tests` at src/cli/completion.rs:1099–1851, dedented by four spaces: helpers `words` (1105), `names` (1109), `at` (1115), `names_at` (1122), `names_at_with` (1130); the 24 tests from `flag_names_reads_every_row_shape_the_table_uses` (1141) through `every_emitted_script_names_the_completion_entry_point` (1834), including the whole-table sweeps `every_command_path_in_the_table_completes` (1525), `every_documented_flag_completes_on_the_page_that_documents_it` (1565), `a_candidate_the_shell_would_expand_is_never_offered` (1598), `no_page_offers_a_malformed_candidate` (1639), `every_value_position_is_completed_or_declared_unenumerable` (1714) and `every_candidate_renders_as_one_clean_line` (1761). src/cli/completion/mod.rs keeps only `#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests;`. Header: the existing `use super::*;` and `use crate::testutil::{EnvVar, TmpDir, env_lock};` plus `use super::grammar::{Operand, flag_metavar_kind, flag_names, flag_tail, flag_value_kind, kind_of_metavar, metavar_of, operand_slots};`, `use super::scripts::{SHELLS, ZSH};`, `use super::values::{ValueKind, config_tokens, registry_values};`.

**Risque.** The module path is unchanged (`crate::cli::completion::tests` either way), so `super::super::upgrade::TARGETS` at line 1311 still resolves and needs no edit — the same is true of every `super::` in the test bodies. `use super::*;` still reaches mod.rs's private `candidates`, `describe`, `insertable`, `cursor_value_kind`, `flag_menu`, `FILES`, `DESC_WIDTH`; it does not reach the sibling child modules, hence the three explicit imports above — omitting one is a compile error, not a silent skip. Intra-doc links inside the tests (`[`names_at`]` at 1129, `[`every_value_position_is_completed_or_declared_unenumerable`]` at 1686, `[`insertable`]` at 1800) all resolve through `use super::*` and the new imports exactly as they do today, and in any case `cargo doc` never enables `cfg(test)`. docs-coverage: `module_level_items` skips `tests.rs` whole (src/docs_coverage.rs:997). Honest cost: 753 re-indented lines move `git blame` for the whole suite onto one commit.

### Autres observations

**A hard prerequisite for the completion split, easy to miss.** src/docs_coverage.rs:861 contains the literal `("cli/completion.rs", "completion_cmd")` inside `UNDOCUMENTED_MODULE_ITEMS` (declared at :856). The moment `src/cli/completion.rs` becomes `src/cli/completion/mod.rs`, that key stops matching, and `every_module_level_item_carries_a_doc_comment` (:1058) fails on its *stale* assertion at :1082–1092 ("names items that are now documented or gone"). Do not retarget the key — write the missing `///` on `completion_cmd` (src/cli/completion.rs:48, currently the only undocumented item in either file) and delete the entry. The list's own doc at :830–834 says it can only shrink. Nothing equivalent exists for help.rs: it has no entry in that table, so the help split has no docs-coverage prerequisite at all.

**A coverage hole the completion split opens, worth writing down.** `dispatched_alias_pairs` (src/help.rs:3727) does a non-recursive `read_dir` of `src/cli` filtered to `extension == "rs"`. After the move, `src/cli/completion/*.rs` is outside its scan. That is harmless today — I verified by re-implementing the scan that every `Some(...) =>` arm carrying two or more subcommand spellings lives in app.rs:35, fs.rs:18, net.rs:52, plugins.rs:26 and :761, proc.rs:28 and :30, projects.rs:20 and :22, secret.rs:26, session.rs:24 and :29, sshagent.rs:17, task.rs:40 and :47 (fifteen pairs; completion.rs contributes none), so the `pairs.len() >= 19` floor at src/help.rs:3802 is unaffected. But the scan is now structurally blind to any future dispatcher placed in a `src/cli/<name>/` directory, and `DISPATCHERS` (src/help.rs:3679) keys on bare file names like `"mod.rs"`, which would collide across directories if the walk were ever made recursive. Either make `walk` recursive and key `DISPATCHERS` on the path relative to `src/cli`, or leave a comment at :3727 saying the scan is top-level-only by design.

**A genuine near-duplicate that the grammar.rs cut would put side by side.** `kind_of_metavar` (src/cli/completion.rs:694–706) and `flag_metavar_kind` (:911–923) are two name→`ValueKind` tables 200 lines apart. They agree exactly on `"id"|"pid"|"session" => Sessions`, `"name"|"app"|"profile"|"sketch" => Apps`, `"store" => Stores`, `"key" => ConfigKeys`, and differ only at the edges: the operand table adds `"scheme"` to Plugins, `"invocation"` to Tasks and `"out"` to Files; the flag table adds `"locator"`, `"location"`, `"key-file"` and `"dir:"` to Files. A metavariable name added to one and not the other silently stops completing on the other side, and neither `every_value_position_is_completed_or_declared_unenumerable` (:1714) nor `every_documented_flag_completes_on_the_page_that_documents_it` (:1565) would catch it, because each sweeps only its own half. The mechanical fix once both live in grammar.rs: a `fn shared_metavar_kind(name: &str) -> Option<ValueKind>` holding the four agreed rows plus the common Files set, with each caller keeping only its own extra arms and its own guards (`flag_metavar_kind`'s uppercase/`=` rejection, `kind_of_metavar`'s `plugins`/`projects`/`rule`/pending page overrides). That is ~20 lines collapsed and one drift class removed. I would do this *after* the move, not during it, so the move stays a pure relocation.

**The PAGES banner comments have already drifted, which is the argument for the optional second step.** src/help.rs:48 says "top-level commands" but the block runs to 1911 and contains most of the `proc`, `fs`, `secret`, `task`, `session` and `config` subcommand pages; src/help.rs:2134 says "net subcommands" but `bundle` (2173), `bundle export` (2197) and `bundle import` (2212) are top-level commands sitting inside it; src/help.rs:1912 says "app subcommands" but `projects show` (2049) is in it. If a namespace shard is ever wanted, the concrete design is: `src/help/pages/` holding `net.rs` (18 pages / 653 lines), `config.rs` (9/330), `task.rs` (9/281), `app.rs` (9/273), `proc.rs` (10/268), `plugins.rs` (17/257, `plugins store` included), `session.rs` (5/106), and `misc.rs` for the remaining ~28 pages, each exposing `pub(super) const PAGES: &[Page]`, with `src/help/pages/mod.rs` holding `const GROUPS: &[&[Page]]` and `pub(super) fn pages() -> impl Iterator<Item = &'static Page>`. The five call sites that read `PAGES` today (src/help.rs:3137 `find`, :3143 `children`, :3189 `all_paths`, :3258 `top_level`, and the tests at :3499 and :3848) become `pages()`. The cost is the one new failure mode the flat version does not have — a group file nobody lists in `GROUPS` — so it must ship with a guard in the house style of `module_level_items`: `read_dir("src/help/pages")` and assert the count of `.rs` files (minus `mod.rs`) equals `GROUPS.len()`. My recommendation is to skip this: `pages.rs` is a catalogue you navigate by grepping `path: &["net", "logs"]`, the localized-diff benefit is modest, and the aggregation seam is the only part of this whole proposal that can lose a page silently.

**Two smaller notes.** `paint_inline_code` (src/help.rs:3241–3246) is a one-line forward to `crate::style::paint_spans`; it earns its place only because its doc comment records the non-terminal-is-plain invariant, so keep it (and its doc) rather than inlining the call at its four use sites. And `all_paths` (src/help.rs:3188) is a `#[cfg(test)] pub fn` consumed by *another module's* tests (src/cli/completion.rs:1527, 1567, 1640, 1720, 1764) — an unusual but deliberate cross-module test API; it stays in `src/help/mod.rs` under the proposal, so those five call sites need no change.

---

## `proxy-shape` — src/sandbox/proxy/ — all files, focusing on mod.rs, wire.rs, ctx.rs, tunnel.rs, h2mitm.rs, websocket.rs

**Verdict : **Découpage utile****

### Cartographie actuelle

`src/sandbox/proxy/` (17 files, 27,649 lines; 10,357 of them in the single flat `tests.rs`, 1,363 more in `bench.rs`) is the host-side TLS-terminating filtering proxy. `mod.rs` states in its own doc (mod.rs:16-25) that it is "the shared vocabulary" and that each way a request can arrive gets a module of its own which contributes only "one plane's *sequencing* of decisions that all live here" — that seam is real and my proposal works inside it, not across it.

Files and their internal sections (verified by grep -n on declaration lines):

**mod.rs (2,059)** — module doc 1-165 (includes the refusal-reason table 89-111 and the credential-injection contract 126-165); imports + `mod`/`use` 166-217; `serve` accept loop 223-292; connection consts `CONNECTION_CAP`:301, `CAP_REFUSAL_WRITE_TIMEOUT`:306, `HEAD_MAX`:309, `head_deadline`:317, `UNREADABLE_HEAD`:324, `CONTROL_BYTE_DETAIL`:329, `ASK_PENDING_CAP`:337; `handle_client` dispatch 346-518; `parse_status_code`:520; **verdict cluster** `PolicyRefusal`:536 / `AskPosture`:561 / `impl PolicyRefusal`:568 / `inspected_rule_destination`:637 / `decide_https`:664-741; **upstream cluster** `UpstreamError`:743 / `connect_upstream`:755 / `acquire_upstream`:790 / `refuse_upstream`:809 / `idempotent_method`:854 / `upstream_spoke`:869; **head cluster** `head_terminated`:886 / `read_head_raw`:898 / `read_head_buffered`:925 / `Head`:950 / `impl Head`:955-997; **body-budget cluster** `CONCURRENT_CHUNKED_UPLOADS`:1016 / `BODY_BUDGET_RAM_SHARE`:1038 / `host_ram`:1041 / `BodyLimits`:1058 + impl 1068 / `POOL_HOLD_MAX`:1106 / `BODY_BUFFER_CAP`:1112 / `BodyBudget`:1119 + Drop 1124 / `reserve_body_buffer`:1135 / `body_budget_message`:1164 / `body_exceeds_hold`:1175 / `hold_request_body`:1189; **credential glue** `matching_injection_ids`:1215 / `injected_names`:1241 / `any_refreshable`:1247 / `SIGNER_REFUSED`:1254 / `SIGNER_BODY_TOO_LARGE`:1259 / `body_too_large_message`:1263 / `signer_refusal_message`:1284 / `carries_secret`:1305 / `reserialize_request`:1330; **relay cluster** `begin_response_stream`:1405 / `relay_response_head`:1446 / `RelayedHead`:1511 / `ClientLeg`:1531 / `finish_tls`:1550 / `pump_to_eof`:1562 / `pump_redacting`:1603 / `needle_prefix_suffix`:1648 / `redact_in_place`:1659; **refusal writers** `refusal_body`:1673 / `egress_notice_line`:1681 / `print_egress_notice`:1701 / `write_refusal`:1726 / `refuse_unreadable_head`:1757 / `unreadable_head_detail`:1788 / `write_all_str`:1798; **absolute-form admission** `RawRequest`:1807 / `Plane`:1817 / `AbsoluteForm`:1849 / `admit_absolute_form`:1869-1983; `invalid`:1985; `mod suggestion_tests` 1991-2056; `mod tests` 2058.

**wire.rs (1,667; code 1-872, tests 873-1667)** — `head_expects_continue`:11, `header_name_eq`:20, `parse_head`:48, `head_carries_control_byte`:99, `Framing`:106/`FramingRefusal`:118/`inspect_framing`:135, `content_length`:207, `request_line_parts`:237, `split_authority`:250, `strip_port`:261, `CountingReader`:281/`CountingWriter`:302, `RELAY_CHUNK`:339, `copy_exact`:342, chunked I/O 362-451, `read_response_head`:454, `BodyFraming`:477/`response_framing`:503, `response_keeps_alive`:558, `force_close_in_head`:607/`offer_reuse_in_head`:619/`rewrite_client_connection`:629, `BodyState`:671/`FramedBody`:703-849, `strip_eol`:862. Cohesive and correctly named.

**ctx.rs (998)** — `builtin_allow_hosts`:39/`builtin_allow_rules`:60; `ProxyCtx` fields 70-179; `impl ProxyCtx` 180-669 (constructor 185, ~20 `with_*` builders, then the observation chokepoints `begin_capture`:340, `websocket_secret_seen`:360, `register_flow`:372, `outcome`:393, `outcome_l7`:424, `announce_refusal`:471, `allow_suggestion`:494, `push_log`:507, `set_status`:569, `redact_query`:584, `credential_refused`:635); test-only impl 670-714; `refusal_block`:715/`refusal_detail`:751; `union_with_builtin`:778; `effective_policy`:792; three test mods 812-998.

**tunnel.rs (968)** — `Turn`:22, `ClientTls`:40, then ONE function `serve_tunneled_request` 53-927 with the plane's numbered steps as comments (4 at :78, 4c at :207, 5 at :231, 5b at :253, 6 at :277, 7 at :301, 7' at :308, 7a at :476, 7b at :465/:493, 8a at :544, 8b at :554, 9 at :595, 9b at :777, 9c at :795, 10 at :863, 11 at :882), plus `refuse_unreadable_inner_head`:928 and `respond_refusal_tls`:960.

**h2mitm.rs (2,956; code 1-1181, tests 1182-2956)** — doc+imports 1-53; `MAX_STREAMS`:42/`MAX_HEADER_LIST`:51; `handle`:55; `serve` (TLS accept, ALPN, SNI, h2 handshake, accept/drain loop) 71-186; `stream` (per-stream gates) 188-327; `relay` (credentials → upstream → allow → capture → rebuild → body) 328-627; capture rendering `capture_request_head`:628/`capture_response_head`:649/`append_headers`:657; body pumps `send_granted`:682/`relay_body`:716/`strip_request_trailers`:773/`relay_body_redacting`:809/`send_masked`:847/`head_carries_secret`:859/`redact_header_map`:881; h2 pool `MAX_POOLED`:896/`UpstreamPool`:909/`ready_upstream`:924/`open_upstream`:968/`binds_identity_to_the_connection`:1019; refusal framing `refuse_upstream`:1044/`handshake_reason`:1072/`refuse`:1084/`refuse_with_detail`:1111; `H2Headers`:1134; `forbidden_request_header`:1161/`forbidden_response_header`:1175.

**websocket.rs (2,083; code 1-1243, tests 1244-2083)** — two unrelated halves. **(a) frame decoding**, lines 15-761: `FrameTee`:31 + `impl FrameTee` 453-707, `SCAN_MESSAGE_CAP`:82, `CONTROL_MAX`:90, `LeakScan`:107 + impl 120-189, `Inflater`:191 + impl 203-357, `Inflated`:333, `RESYNC_PLAINTEXT_CAP`:359, `Deflate`:364, `negotiated_deflate`:385, `HeaderScan`:429, `scan_frame_header`:709. **(b) upgrade + byte relay**, 763-1243: `is_websocket_upgrade`:763, `reserialize_upgrade`:777, `relay_upgrade`:823, `flush_tls`:920, `read_plaintext`:939, `TunnelObservers`:984, `follow`:1006, `tunnel_needles`:1033, `relay_websocket`:1041.

**Supporting files, all already right-sized and cohesive:** forward.rs 714, cleartext.rs 296, capture.rs 960, inject.rs 1,896, ssrf.rs 403, ca.rs 303, dns.rs 213, pool.rs 205, splice.rs 208.

### Justification

**Direct answer to the shape question: the authorization *verdict* is stated once; the machinery on either side of it is stated two to four times, and the codebase's own comments record three bugs that came from exactly that.**

Stated once, and correctly so: the policy verdict `decide_https` (mod.rs:664, called from tunnel.rs:231, forward.rs:92, h2mitm.rs:271); DNS + SSRF `resolve_checked`; framing `inspect_framing` (wire.rs:135); needle matching `carries_secret` (mod.rs:1305); injection matching `matching_injection_ids` (mod.rs:1215); signer refusal prose `signer_refusal_message` (mod.rs:1284); refusal body `refusal_body` (mod.rs:1673); header-strip rule `header_name_eq` (wire.rs:20); masking bytes `redact_in_place` (mod.rs:1659).

Restated, with line cites:
1. **WS-into-injected-host refusal** — tunnel.rs:253-276 ≡ forward.rs:108-130. Verbatim but for the writer.
2. **Injection + signer + body-hold + budget block** — tunnel.rs:307-474 ≈ forward.rs:158-307. I diffed these: ~165 lines each, differing only in identifier names (`connect_host/imethod/itarget` vs `host/method/path`), the refusal writer (`respond_refusal_tls` vs `write_refusal`), the reader (`&mut br` vs a scoped `BufReader::new(&client)`), and one extra `&& !ws_upgrade` (tunnel.rs:355). h2mitm.rs:356-417 is a genuinely different third statement (no held body on h2).
3. **Pool eligibility** — tunnel.rs:481-491 ≡ forward.rs:312-315.
4. **Upstream take + one-shot replay loop** — tunnel.rs:496-540 + 668-733 ≈ forward.rs:320-330 + 432-478.
5. **`masks_reflection`** — the identical three-line predicate at tunnel.rs:784-788, forward.rs:520-524, h2mitm.rs:580-584.
6. **401 → `credential_refused`** — tunnel.rs:858-859 ≡ forward.rs:576-577 ≡ h2mitm.rs:557-558.
7. **`observe_head` placement** — tunnel.rs:472-474, forward.rs:305-307, cleartext.rs:159, h2mitm.rs:415-416.
8. **Response relay tail** (`relay_response_head` → empty-head `upstream-closed` → `set_status` → framed pump → park) — tunnel.rs:795-899 ≈ forward.rs:515-607 ≈ cleartext.rs:267-295.
9. **Allow record + flow + capture open** — tunnel.rs:520-552, forward.rs:332-352, h2mitm.rs:432-465.

The comments themselves name three defects this produced: forward.rs:83-89 (the absolute form judged a WS upgrade as its literal `GET`, which a `CONNECT` to the same host refused); h2mitm.rs:552-556 (only the h2 plane failed to refresh on a 401, so a stale token stayed stale on that plane alone); websocket.rs:786-789 (`proxy-authorization` forwarded to the origin on the upgrade and h2 rebuilds only). Each is a decision that was written out N times and drifted at one of them — which is precisely the failure mode mod.rs:22-25 says the design exists to prevent. The design is right; the two HTTP/1.1 planes just never finished obeying it.

**Why "worthwhile" and not "strongly recommended".** The two biggest files each have a real defence. `mod.rs` at 2,059 lines is a *deliberately* central vocabulary, and scattering it across six files makes the vocabulary harder to read whole — but 8 disjoint clusters with clean edges is more than one file should carry, and 165 of its lines (`Head`, the head readers, `parse_status_code`, `reserialize_request`) are plain HTTP/1.1 wire concerns that `wire.rs` currently reaches back *up* for through `use super::*`. `tunnel.rs` is one 875-line function, and I am **not** proposing to carve it up: it is a linear sequence whose whole value is that a reader can follow one request from head to close, and chopping it into `step_5`/`step_7` helpers would destroy that. What I propose is narrower — lift out only the ~200 lines it shares verbatim with `forward.rs`, so the sequence stays visible and the shared decisions stop being copies.

**The cost is unusually low here**, and I verified the mechanism rather than assuming it: `tests.rs`, `tunnel.rs`, `forward.rs`, `cleartext.rs`, `websocket.rs` and `wire.rs` all begin with `use super::*;`, and mod.rs already re-globs its children (`use wire::*;` at mod.rs:214, `use websocket::*;` at :213). So a new child module plus one `use newmod::*;` line in mod.rs leaves every sibling and all 10,357 lines of `tests.rs` compiling with **zero edits**. Intra-doc links survive the same chain, and I can prove it from this tree rather than from memory: mod.rs:104 links ``[`inspect_framing`]`` and ``[`head_carries_control_byte`]``, both `pub(super) fn` defined in wire.rs and reachable only through that glob, under a build that denies rustdoc warnings. `docs_coverage.rs` is unaffected — it checks CLI verbs, config fields and profiles, plus a (file, symbol) allowlist at docs_coverage.rs:856-943 whose only entry in this directory is `("sandbox/proxy/websocket.rs", "relay_websocket")` (docs_coverage.rs:935), and `relay_websocket` does not move under this proposal.

Do modules 1-3 (the deduplication and the two obvious mis-filings). Modules 4-7 are honest tidying of mod.rs with modest payoff — worth doing in the same pass, not worth a pass of their own. Module 8 is optional.

### Découpage proposé

#### `src/sandbox/proxy/exchange.rs` (~420 lignes)

**Rôle.** The headline. What the two inspected HTTP/1.1 planes do BETWEEN the verdict and the wire, stated once instead of twice — closing the ~200 lines that tunnel.rs and forward.rs currently keep as near-verbatim copies of each other. This adds to mod.rs's stated "shared vocabulary" rather than merging the planes: each plane keeps its own sequencing and its own refusal writer, and only the decisions become shared. New module doc explains that this is the tunnel/forward common denominator and that h2 states its own (no held body, no CONNECT).

**Ce qui bouge.** MOVED from mod.rs (the credential glue that already serves all planes): `matching_injection_ids`:1215-1240, `injected_names`:1241-1246, `any_refreshable`:1247-1253, `SIGNER_REFUSED`:1254, `SIGNER_BODY_TOO_LARGE`:1259, `signer_refusal_message`:1284-1303, `carries_secret`:1305-1328.

NEW, built by lifting the duplicated blocks:
- `trait ClientChannel: BufRead { fn refuse_on(&mut self) -> &mut dyn Write; }` — the one thing that differs between the planes, named. `impl` for `BufReader<StreamOwned<ServerConnection, UnixStream>>` (returns `self.get_mut()`) and for `BufReader<&UnixStream>` (returns `self.get_mut()`, relying on std's `impl Write for &UnixStream`). The forward plane then reads and refuses through ONE borrow, which is what forces its present scoped-reader dance at forward.rs:236-240 and 397-401.
- `struct Refused;` — a zero-sized token meaning "the refusal is already written and already counted". tunnel maps it to `Ok(Turn::Close)`, forward to `Ok(())`. Preserves the property respond_refusal_tls's doc (tunnel.rs:951-958) exists to hold, and now holds it for both planes.
- `fn refuse_ws_into_injected_host(...) -> Result<(), Refused>` — from tunnel.rs:253-276 ≡ forward.rs:108-130.
- `fn prepare_credentials(ch: &mut impl ClientChannel, ctx, creds, host, port, target, method, framing, upgrade: bool) -> Result<Prepared<'_>, Refused>` — from tunnel.rs:307-474 and forward.rs:158-307. Returns `Prepared { injected_ids, injected, held, budget, pool_key, replayable, keep_alive }`. Absorbs `injected_ids`, `digest_wanted`, the SIGNER_BODY_TOO_LARGE refusal, the budget reservation, `hold_for_reuse`, the 100-continue answer, `hold_request_body` and its chunked refusal, `body_facts`, `injection_values` and its SIGNER_REFUSED refusal, `observe_head`, and the pool-eligibility triple (tunnel.rs:481-491 ≡ forward.rs:312-315). The `upgrade: bool` parameter is where tunnel.rs:347-353's `!ws_upgrade` fix — a real bug, documented in place — gets stated once for both planes instead of once for one.
- `fn masks_reflection(creds: &CredentialSet, host: &str) -> bool` — from tunnel.rs:784-788 ≡ forward.rs:520-524 ≡ h2mitm.rs:580-584. All three call sites become one line; h2mitm.rs:580-584 calls it too.
- `fn note_final_status(ctx, seq, creds, ids, code)` — the `set_status` + `code == 401 && any_refreshable(..) => credential_refused()` pair from tunnel.rs:848-861 ≡ forward.rs:566-579 ≡ h2mitm.rs:546-559.

STAYS: `serve_tunneled_request` remains one readable sequence in tunnel.rs; `handle_https_forward` remains one in forward.rs. Both shrink by ~200 lines and read as: admit → decide → prepare → forward → relay.

**Risque.** **The highest-risk item in this proposal, and the only one that changes control flow on the security path.** Every refusal it absorbs is a fail-closed exit whose `ctx.outcome(...)`/`ctx.push_log(...)` call must fire exactly once — mod.rs:1968-1983 records that the push_log/outcome split is "the part most likely to drift under copying", and this moves ~10 such sites. Do it with the existing tests green at every step and lift one block at a time (`refuse_ws_into_injected_host` first — it is 23 lines and self-contained — then `masks_reflection` and `note_final_status`, then `prepare_credentials` last).

Visibility: all seven moved mod.rs items are private `fn`/`const` and become `pub(super)`. `carries_secret` and `matching_injection_ids` and `injected_names` and `any_refreshable` and `SIGNER_REFUSED` and `signer_refusal_message` are named in h2mitm.rs's explicit `use super::{...}` at h2mitm.rs:24-28 (plus `super::injected_names` at h2mitm.rs:414 and `super::any_refreshable` at h2mitm.rs:557) — mod.rs must carry `use exchange::*;` so those `super::` paths keep resolving, or update h2mitm's import list to `use super::exchange::{...}` (cleaner; 6 names).

Rustdoc: two links break unless mod.rs re-globs — h2mitm.rs:17 ``[`carries_secret`]`` and h2mitm.rs:857 ``[`carries_secret`]``. Both resolve today via h2mitm's explicit import, so updating that import fixes both. mod.rs:1235 mentions `observe_head` in prose (not a link). No `[`matching_injection_ids`]`, `[`injected_names`]`, `[`any_refreshable`]` or `[`signer_refusal_message`]` links exist anywhere.

Tests: no `#[cfg(test)]` block moves — `tests.rs` reaches these through `use super::*` and only touches `reserialize_request` (1 site) among them. The plane-level tests that cover these paths live in `tests.rs` and `forward.rs`'s `mod tests` (forward.rs:614-714, which asserts the `ws-injection-refused` transcript at forward.rs:706) and are unaffected.

Cycles: none — `exchange` is a leaf that tunnel/forward/h2mitm call. It must not call back into them.

The one thing to watch: `hold_request_body` on the tunneled plane reads from the persistent `BufReader` that carries pipelined requests; forward.rs:237-238 deliberately scopes its reader because over-reading is harmless there. Routing both through one `ClientChannel` gives forward the tunnel's (stricter) behaviour, which is a silent improvement, not a regression — but it should be called out in the new module doc rather than left to be rediscovered.

#### `src/sandbox/proxy/wsframe.rs` (~1340 lignes)

**Rôle.** websocket.rs is two unrelated modules in one file: a WebSocket frame decoder (with DEFLATE reassembly and a leak tripwire) and a byte-relay plane. They share only `SecretNeedle` and `CapBuf`. The decoder half is pure, self-contained, and has no reference anywhere outside websocket.rs — the cleanest split in the directory.

**Ce qui bouge.** MOVED from websocket.rs: `FrameTee` struct :31-80 together with `impl FrameTee` :453-707 (`new`, `consume`, `sightings`, `spent`, `push`, `plaintext_cap`, `compressed_budget`, `end_of_frame`); `SCAN_MESSAGE_CAP`:82; `CONTROL_MAX`:90; `LeakScan`:107-118 + `impl LeakScan` :120-189 (`new`, `start_message`, `take_standalone`, `take`, `drain`); `Inflater`:191-201 + `impl Inflater` :203-331 (`new`, `message`, `drain`); `Inflated`:333-357; `RESYNC_PLAINTEXT_CAP`:359; `Deflate`:363-383; `negotiated_deflate`:385-427; `HeaderScan`:429-451; `scan_frame_header`:709-761. Plus the frame-decoder half of `mod tests`: the helpers `frame`:1252, `frame_with_fin`:1258, `tee`:1283, `deflating_tee`:1292, `scanning_tee`:1304, `deflated_message`:1311, `raw_deflate_flags`:1461, `deflated_frame`:1467, `captured`:1485, `needle`:1916, and the ~30 tests from :1354 through :2081 except the two named below.

STAYS in websocket.rs: `is_websocket_upgrade`:763, `reserialize_upgrade`:777, `relay_upgrade`:823, `flush_tls`:920, `read_plaintext`:939, `TunnelObservers`:984, `follow`:1006, `tunnel_needles`:1033, `relay_websocket`:1041 — and the two tests that belong to them, `a_tunnel_does_not_scan_for_a_credential_learned_on_its_own_host`:1831 (tests `tunnel_needles`) and `a_websocket_upgrade_does_not_hand_the_proxy_credential_to_the_origin`:1886 (tests `reserialize_upgrade`). websocket.rs drops from 2,083 to ~740.

**Risque.** Lowest risk in the proposal. I verified with grep that `FrameTee`, `Deflate`, `negotiated_deflate`, `LeakScan`, `Inflater`, `HeaderScan` and `scan_frame_header` have **zero** references outside websocket.rs — so nothing but websocket.rs has to see them.

Visibility: unchanged. `FrameTee`, `Deflate`, `negotiated_deflate` are already `pub(super)` (visible in `proxy`, hence in the sibling `websocket`); `LeakScan`, `Inflater`, `Inflated`, `HeaderScan`, `scan_frame_header`, `SCAN_MESSAGE_CAP`, `CONTROL_MAX`, `RESYNC_PLAINTEXT_CAP` are private and must become `pub(super)` — except `LeakScan`/`Inflater`/`Inflated`/`HeaderScan`/`scan_frame_header`, which are used only *within* the moved code and can stay private in the new file. Only `FrameTee`, `Deflate`, `negotiated_deflate`, and `FrameTee::{new, push, sightings}` need to be reachable from websocket.rs.

Imports the new file needs: `use super::capture::CapBuf;`, `use super::inject::SecretNeedle;`, `std::sync::Arc`, and the three `miniz_oxide` items currently at websocket.rs:11-12.

Rustdoc: no external link breaks — no `[`FrameTee`]`/`[`Deflate`]`/`[`LeakScan`]`/`[`Inflater`]` link exists outside websocket.rs. The internal ones (e.g. `FrameTee::control_payload`'s doc referring to `[`CONTROL_MAX`]`, `Inflater`'s to `[`RESYNC_PLAINTEXT_CAP`]`) all move together and keep resolving. websocket.rs's remaining doc references to `[`FrameTee`]` and `[`Inflater`]` (websocket.rs:24 in the module doc, which itself should be split between the two files) resolve through mod.rs's `use wsframe::*;` by the same chain that already makes mod.rs:104's ``[`inspect_framing`]`` resolve.

Tests: the `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` at websocket.rs:1244 splits. Both halves are self-contained — the frame helpers are used only by frame tests, `needle()`:1916 by the scan tests (which move). No shared harness spans the boundary.

Cycles: none. `wsframe` depends on `capture` and `inject` only; `websocket` depends on `wsframe`.

#### `src/sandbox/proxy/wire.rs` (~150 lignes)

**Rôle.** Not a new file — an ABSORB. wire.rs's own doc (wire.rs:1-5) claims "request-head parsing" and "byte plumbing", yet `Head`, the two head readers and `parse_status_code` live in mod.rs and wire.rs reaches back *up* for them through `use super::*` (wire.rs:504 calls `parse_status_code`; wire.rs:11, :99, :136 all take `&Head`; wire.rs:77 constructs one). Moving them down closes the up-reference and makes wire.rs the one place the HTTP/1.1 head is read, parsed, checked, rewritten and serialized.

**Ce qui bouge.** MOVED from mod.rs into wire.rs: `parse_status_code`:520-528; `head_terminated`:886-897; `read_head_raw`:898-924; `read_head_buffered`:925-949; `Head`:950-954 and `impl Head` :955-997 (`header`, `count`, `keeps_alive`); `reserialize_request`:1330-1403; `CONTROL_BYTE_DETAIL`:329-335 (used at exactly one site, wire.rs:142, as `super::CONTROL_BYTE_DETAIL` — the `super::` prefix goes away).

STAYS in mod.rs: `HEAD_MAX`:309 and `head_deadline`:317 — those are launch policy (`head_deadline` takes `&ProxyCtx`), and the readers already take `max` and `deadline` as parameters, so they move without dragging either.

Placement inside wire.rs: `Head` + `impl Head` next to `parse_head`:48 (which constructs it) and `head_expects_continue`:11; the readers next to `read_response_head`:454, whose doc already says "like [`read_head_buffered`]" (wire.rs:448); `parse_status_code` next to `response_framing`:503, its only present caller in the file; `reserialize_request` next to `force_close_in_head`:607/`offer_reuse_in_head`:619/`rewrite_client_connection`:629, the other head rewriters, and next to `header_name_eq`:20 which it calls.

**Risque.** Very low, and it removes a dependency edge rather than adding one.

Visibility: all seven items are private in mod.rs and become `pub(super)` in wire.rs — the visibility every neighbour in that file already has. `Head`'s two fields and its three methods must be `pub(super)` too, since tunnel.rs:135, forward.rs:365 and cleartext.rs:232 construct `Head { request_line, headers }` literally and call `.header(..)`/`.keeps_alive()`.

Rustdoc: five links point at moved symbols, and all five keep resolving through mod.rs's existing `use wire::*;` (mod.rs:214) — mod.rs:104 (``[`HEAD_MAX`]``, which does not move), mod.rs:878 (``[`read_head_buffered`]``), wire.rs:448 (``[`read_head_buffered`]``, now same-module), mod.rs:1427 and mod.rs:1749 (``[`HEAD_MAX`]``, does not move). `Head`'s own doc at mod.rs:963-965 cites `wire::the_framing_lookups_fold_case_only_while_the_injection_strip_also_folds_underscores` — a plain code span, not a link, but once `Head` lives in wire.rs the citation should lose its `wire::` prefix. `Head::keeps_alive`'s doc links ``[`response_keeps_alive`]`` (wire.rs:558), which becomes a same-module link.

Tests: `tests.rs` calls `parse_status_code` (12 sites), `read_head_buffered` (3), `read_head_raw` (2), `parse_head` (3), `reserialize_request` (1) — all through `use super::*`, all unaffected. wire.rs's own `mod tests` at :873 already builds `Head` literals at :1349, :1383, :1431 and currently reaches them via `use super::*`; after the move they are same-module.

Cycles: none — this REMOVES one. Today mod.rs→wire (`use wire::*`) and wire→mod (`use super::*` for `Head`, `parse_status_code`, `CONTROL_BYTE_DETAIL`) are mutual; afterwards wire.rs's `use super::*` at wire.rs:7 keeps only the std/`Duration`/`Instant` re-exports and could largely be replaced by explicit imports.

#### `src/sandbox/proxy/verdict.rs` (~320 lignes)
