**Rôle.** The policy answer as a value: what a refusal is, what it is called on the wire, what sentence it prints, whether the transport can park an `ask`, and the one function that reaches the decision. This is the security keystone of the directory and deserves to be a file whose name says so — a reader auditing "how is a request authorized" currently has to find lines 530-741 inside a 2,059-line module.

**Ce qui bouge.** MOVED from mod.rs: `PolicyRefusal`:536-559 and `impl PolicyRefusal`:568-635 (`status_line`, `status`, `tag`, `message`); `AskPosture`:561-566; `inspected_rule_destination`:637-662; `decide_https`:664-741; `ASK_PENDING_CAP`:337-341 (referenced only at mod.rs:718, inside `decide_https`); `egress_notice_line`:1681-1699 and `print_egress_notice`:1701-1724 (called only from `decide_https`'s ask branch at mod.rs:709); `#[cfg(test)] mod suggestion_tests` :1991-2056, which tests `PolicyRefusal::DeniedDefault.message` and moves with it verbatim.

STAYS: `refusal_body`:1673 and `write_refusal`:1726 stay in mod.rs — they are written by every plane for non-policy refusals too (bad-request, upstream, cap), so they are not the verdict's.

**Risque.** Low. `decide_https` is pure decision plus recording — it takes `&ProxyCtx` and returns `Result<Option<Rule>, PolicyRefusal>`; it writes nothing.

Visibility: all become `pub(super)`. `egress_notice_line` and `print_egress_notice` can stay private to the new file — grep shows `print_egress_notice`'s only caller is inside `decide_https` (mod.rs:709), and `egress_notice_line`'s only other caller is `tests.rs` (2 sites), which reaches it via `use super::*` and so needs `pub(super)` plus mod.rs's glob.

Rustdoc: seven links, all surviving through `use verdict::*;` in mod.rs — tunnel.rs:231 and forward.rs:92 (bare ``[`decide_https`]``, via their `use super::*`); h2mitm.rs:10 and :262 (bare ``[`decide_https`]``, via h2mitm's explicit `use super::{... decide_https ...}` at h2mitm.rs:24-28, which must be updated or left to resolve through the re-glob); h2mitm.rs:12 ``[`AskPosture::RefuseUnsupported`](super::AskPosture)`` — an explicit `super::` path that still resolves through the glob but reads better as `super::verdict::AskPosture`; mod.rs:564 ``[`PolicyRefusal::Http2AskUnsupported`]`` and mod.rs:657 ``[`AskPosture`]`` (same-module after the move); mod.rs:2000 ``[`PolicyRefusal::message`]`` (moves with suggestion_tests).

One link points OUT and must not be disturbed: `PolicyRefusal`'s doc at mod.rs:538 links ``[`ssrf::ConnectRefusal`]``. From `proxy::verdict` that path resolves as `super::ssrf::ConnectRefusal`, so it needs rewriting to `super::ssrf::ConnectRefusal` or the file needs `use super::ssrf;`. **This is the one link in the whole proposal that genuinely breaks.**

Tests: `suggestion_tests` moves whole; it needs `use crate::allowlist::{self, Decision}` and `super::ProxyCtx`/`super::ca::Ca`, all already imported there. `tests.rs` never names `decide_https`, `PolicyRefusal` or `AskPosture` (verified by grep: 0 occurrences each) — it drives them through whole connections.

Cycles: none; `verdict` depends on `ctx`, `allowlist` and `control`.

#### `src/sandbox/proxy/budget.rs` (~245 lignes)

**Rôle.** The host-side request-body memory ceiling: one body's cap, the sum across every connection, the reservation that admits one, the refusal that turns one away, and the sentence that explains it. It is a single self-contained subsystem with ~200 lines of prose reasoning attached (mod.rs:998-1052 alone is a sustained argument about RAM shares) that currently sits in the middle of the vocabulary file, between the head parser and the credential glue.

**Ce qui bouge.** MOVED from mod.rs: `CONCURRENT_CHUNKED_UPLOADS`:1016 (with its doc :998-1015); `BODY_BUDGET_RAM_SHARE`:1038 (doc :1018-1037); `host_ram`:1041-1052; `BodyLimits`:1058-1066 and `impl BodyLimits` :1068-1103 (`new`, `sized`); `POOL_HOLD_MAX`:1106; `BODY_BUFFER_CAP`:1112; `BodyBudget`:1119-1123 and its `Drop` :1124-1133; `reserve_body_buffer`:1135-1162; `body_budget_message`:1164-1173; `body_exceeds_hold`:1175-1187; `hold_request_body`:1189-1213; `body_too_large_message`:1263-1281.

STAYS: `ProxyCtx::held_bodies` (the atomic itself) stays a field on `ProxyCtx` in ctx.rs:139-146, and `ctx.body: BodyLimits` stays a field (ctx.rs:171-176, built at ctx.rs:221) — only the type moves.

**Risque.** Low; it is a leaf that nothing outside `proxy` touches.

Visibility: `BodyLimits` is already `pub(super)` with `pub(super) fn new`; its `per_request`/`total` fields are private-to-mod.rs and are read at tunnel.rs:337 (`ctx.body.per_request`), forward.rs:178, cleartext.rs, mod.rs:1272 and `tests.rs` (17 sites) — so both fields must become `pub(super)`. `CONCURRENT_CHUNKED_UPLOADS` is declared `pub(crate)` at mod.rs:1016 but I verified it is used only inside `proxy` (mod.rs:1085 and tests.rs:5181/5183); it can safely stay `pub(crate)` (re-exported through mod.rs's glob) or be narrowed to `pub(super)`. Everything else becomes `pub(super)`.

Rustdoc: five links, all internal to the moved block and all surviving — mod.rs:1003 ``[`BodyLimits::per_request`]``, mod.rs:1028 ``[`CONCURRENT_CHUNKED_UPLOADS`]``, mod.rs:1035 ``[`BodyLimits::sized`]``, mod.rs:1062 ``[`CONCURRENT_CHUNKED_UPLOADS`]``, mod.rs:1114 ``[`BodyLimits::total`]``, mod.rs:1188 ``[`body_exceeds_hold`]``. Two point OUT and need checking from the new path: mod.rs:1005 ``[`crate::sandbox::cgroup::wrap`]`` and mod.rs:1031 ``[`crate::sandbox::cgroup`]`` are absolute and unaffected; mod.rs:1010 and mod.rs:1041-vicinity ``[`ProxyCtx::max_conns`]`` resolves as `super::ProxyCtx` from the new module (ctx.rs re-exported through mod.rs) — safe, but worth confirming. tunnel.rs:345 and forward.rs:191 both say "See [`POOL_HOLD_MAX`]" and resolve through their `use super::*`.

Tests: no `#[cfg(test)]` block moves. `tests.rs` hits `BodyLimits` 17 times, `reserve_body_buffer` 6, `hold_request_body` 3, `body_exceeds_hold` 3 — all via `use super::*`, all unaffected by `use budget::*;` in mod.rs.

Cycles: none — `budget` calls only `wire::read_chunked_body` (from `hold_request_body`) and std.

#### `src/sandbox/proxy/relay.rs` (~235 lignes)

**Rôle.** Moving a permitted response back to the cage: read the upstream's head, state sbx's own answer about the client's leg, stream the framed body, and mask a reflected credential out of both. This is the second half of every inspected plane and, unlike the verdict, it is genuinely shared byte-plumbing with no policy in it — the natural companion file to wire.rs.

**Ce qui bouge.** MOVED from mod.rs: `relay_response_head`:1446-1508; `RelayedHead`:1511-1529; `ClientLeg`:1531-1548; `finish_tls`:1550-1560; `pump_to_eof`:1562-1601; `pump_redacting`:1603-1646; `needle_prefix_suffix`:1648-1657; `redact_in_place`:1659-1679.

STAYS in mod.rs: `begin_response_stream`:1405-1443 goes to `upstream.rs` instead — it is a socket-option call on the upstream `TcpStream`, not a relay step, and it is what the pool's `park` puts back (mod.rs:1403-1404).

**Risque.** Low mechanically, with **one export that must not be dropped**.

`redact_in_place` is `pub(crate)` and is imported from OUTSIDE the proxy: `src/sandbox/control/capture.rs:38` does `use crate::sandbox::proxy::redact_in_place;`. So mod.rs must carry an explicit `pub(crate) use relay::redact_in_place;` — a bare `use relay::*;` would make the name private to `proxy` and break that import. It is not in `src/sandbox/mod.rs`'s `pub(crate) use proxy::{...}` list (sandbox/mod.rs:173-176), so that list needs no change.

Visibility: everything else becomes `pub(super)`. `RelayedHead`'s three fields (`head`, `framing`, `persistent`) are destructured at tunnel.rs:797-802, forward.rs:531-535 and cleartext.rs:268-272, so they must be `pub(super)` too. `ClientLeg::MayReuse { idle }` is constructed at tunnel.rs:836 and `ClientLeg::Close` at forward.rs:545 and cleartext.rs:281.

Rustdoc: five links, all surviving through `use relay::*;` — forward.rs:40 ``[`relay_response_head`]``, mod.rs:1510 ``[`relay_response_head`]``, mod.rs:1576 ``[`pump_to_eof`]``, mod.rs:1581 ``[`relay_response_head`]``, mod.rs:1585 ``[`needle_prefix_suffix`]``, mod.rs:155 ``[`pump_redacting`]`` (in the module doc) and tunnel.rs:573 ``[`pump_redacting`]``. Three links point OUT of the moved block and resolve as `super::X` from the new module: mod.rs:1427 ``[`HEAD_MAX`]``, mod.rs:1400-vicinity ``[`force_close_in_head`]`` (a wire.rs symbol, reachable via `super::` glob), and `pump_redacting`'s doc reference to `relay_body_redacting in h2mitm.rs` (prose, not a link). `begin_response_stream`'s doc links ``[`pool::UpstreamPool::park`]`` (mod.rs:1404) which must become `super::pool::UpstreamPool::park` once it moves to upstream.rs.

Tests: no `#[cfg(test)]` block moves. `tests.rs` uses `relay_response_head` (2), `ClientLeg` (2), `pump_redacting` (4), `redact_in_place` (4), `needle_prefix_suffix` (2) — all via `use super::*`.

Cycles: none. `relay` depends on `wire` (`read_response_head`, `response_framing`, `response_keeps_alive`, `force_close_in_head`, `offer_reuse_in_head`), `capture` and `inject::SecretNeedle`.

#### `src/sandbox/proxy/upstream.rs` (~250 lignes)

**Rôle.** Opening, reusing, refusing and replaying the validated HTTP/1.1 upstream leg — the whole of what happens between "the address is guarded" and "the bytes are on the wire", including the one-shot replay loop that tunnel.rs and forward.rs currently keep as two copies. Names the seam that mod.rs's own doc calls out (mod.rs:791-798): reuse sits AFTER the verdict and never instead of it.

**Ce qui bouge.** MOVED from mod.rs: `UpstreamError`:743-753; `connect_upstream`:755-788; `acquire_upstream`:790-807; `refuse_upstream`:809-852; `idempotent_method`:854-867; `upstream_spoke`:869-884; `begin_response_stream`:1405-1443.

NEW, lifting the fourth restated decision: `fn send_with_retry<W: Write>(w: &mut W, upstream: &mut UpstreamTls, from_pool: &mut bool, req: &[u8], ctx, ip, port, host, method, path, flow) -> Result<(), Refused>` — built from tunnel.rs:668-733 ≡ forward.rs:432-478. Both copies implement the same three rules (a fresh connection that refuses the write is a real error; a parked one that refuses is a far side that is gone; a non-idempotent method gets the `502 upstream-closed` rather than a replay) and both re-call `acquire_upstream(ctx, None, ...)` for the one retry. The `upstream-closed` refusal it writes goes through the `Refused` token from exchange.rs, so tunnel maps it to `Turn::Close` and forward to `()`.

**Risque.** Low-to-moderate — the moves are trivial, the `send_with_retry` extraction is not.

Visibility: all seven moved items are private and become `pub(super)`. `UpstreamError`'s two variants are matched in `refuse_upstream` only (same file after the move) but constructed at tunnel.rs and forward.rs call sites indirectly through `acquire_upstream`'s `Result`, so the enum itself must be `pub(super)`.

Rustdoc: two links break by name and must be repointed — h2mitm.rs:15 writes ``[`connect_upstream`](super::connect_upstream)`` and h2mitm.rs:1034 writes ``[`refuse_upstream`](super::refuse_upstream)``. Both are EXPLICIT `super::` paths. They still resolve through mod.rs's `use upstream::*;` (a glob import creates a binding in `proxy`, visible to the descendant `h2mitm`), but they should be rewritten to `super::upstream::connect_upstream` / `super::upstream::refuse_upstream` so the prose matches the tree. Note h2mitm.rs has its own private `fn refuse_upstream` at h2mitm.rs:1044, so the two names coexist in different modules today and will continue to — do NOT glob-re-export in a way that collides.

One more link to watch: `begin_response_stream`'s doc at mod.rs:1403-1404 links ``[`pool::UpstreamPool::park`]``, which from `proxy::upstream` must become ``[`super::pool::UpstreamPool::park`]``.

Tests: no `#[cfg(test)]` block moves. `tests.rs` names none of these directly (verified: `connect_upstream` 0, `acquire_upstream` 0, `idempotent_method` 0, `upstream_spoke` 0) — it exercises them through whole connections against real listeners.

Cycles: `upstream` depends on `ctx`, `ca::upstream_server_name`, `pool::{PoolKey, UpstreamTls}` and — for `send_with_retry`'s refusal — `exchange::Refused`. `exchange` must not depend on `upstream`; keep `send_with_retry` here and `prepare_credentials` there.

#### `src/sandbox/proxy/h2mitm/` (~355 lignes)

**Rôle.** OPTIONAL, lowest priority. h2mitm.rs is 2,956 lines but only 1,181 of them are code, in five clean clusters; the other 1,775 are one `mod tests` with a heavy shared harness. Turn the file into a directory (mirroring the existing `src/sandbox/control/`) so the h2 upstream pool and the flow-controlled body pumps stop sitting between the plane's sequence and its refusal writers.

**Ce qui bouge.** `h2mitm/mod.rs` keeps the module doc :1-21, imports :22-40, `MAX_STREAMS`:42, `MAX_HEADER_LIST`:51, `handle`:55, `serve`:71-186, `stream`:188-327, `relay`:328-627, the refusal writers `refuse_upstream`:1044 / `handshake_reason`:1072 / `refuse`:1084 / `refuse_with_detail`:1111, `H2Headers`:1134 + its `HeaderLookup` impl :1136, and `forbidden_request_header`:1161 / `forbidden_response_header`:1175. ≈ 700 lines.

`h2mitm/pool.rs` takes `MAX_POOLED`:896, `UpstreamPool`:909, `ready_upstream`:924, `open_upstream`:968, `binds_identity_to_the_connection`:1019. ≈ 140 lines.

`h2mitm/body.rs` takes `send_granted`:682, `relay_body`:716, `strip_request_trailers`:773, `relay_body_redacting`:809, `send_masked`:847, `redact_header_map`:881, plus the capture renderers `capture_request_head`:628, `capture_response_head`:649, `append_headers`:657, and `head_carries_secret`:859. ≈ 300 lines.

**Risque.** Moderate, and the payoff is the smallest of the eight — do it only if you are already editing this file.

**The test harness is the cost.** `mod tests` at h2mitm.rs:1182-2956 is one block with a large shared harness — `trusting_h2`:1836, `relaying_ctx`:1853, `grpc_request`:1883, `send_under_flow_control`:1900, `upstream_stream`:1916, `upstream_conn`:1980, `spawn_h2_upstream`:2024, `through_h2_proxy`:2074, `through_h2_proxy_streams`:2096, `h2_verdict`:1409, `one_denial`:1499 — that nearly every test uses. The pool tests (`streams_share_an_upstream_only_with_the_credential_set_they_share`:2234, `a_connection_the_upstream_binds_an_identity_to_stops_being_shared`:2368) and the body tests (`a_body_larger_than_the_peers_window_is_relayed_in_pieces_rather_than_deadlocking`:1223, `a_requests_trailers_are_stripped_like_its_head`:1593, `redact_header_map_masks_a_reflected_secret_in_a_header_value`:1628) all lean on it. Either keep the whole `mod tests` in `h2mitm/mod.rs` (simplest — the tests reach `pool`/`body` items via `use super::*` plus a glob re-import), or promote the harness into a `#[cfg(test)] mod harness` the three files share. The first is the honest choice.

**Name collision.** `h2mitm::pool::UpstreamPool` and `proxy::pool::UpstreamPool` (pool.rs:85) are different types with the same name; `h2mitm::refuse_upstream` (h2mitm.rs:1044) and `proxy::upstream::refuse_upstream` likewise. Neither must ever be glob-re-exported into `proxy` — keep the h2 items reachable only as `self::pool::…` / `self::body::…` inside `h2mitm`.

Visibility: everything moved is private and becomes `pub(super)` within the `h2mitm` sub-tree (i.e. visible in `h2mitm`, not in `proxy`).

Rustdoc: h2mitm.rs:1034's ``[`refuse_upstream`](super::refuse_upstream)`` currently means `proxy::refuse_upstream`; once the file becomes `h2mitm/mod.rs`, `super::` from a doc IN `h2mitm/mod.rs` still means `proxy`, so that one is unaffected by THIS split (only by the `upstream.rs` split above). But `body.rs`'s and `pool.rs`'s docs contain `super::`-relative prose that must be re-checked: `open_upstream`'s doc mentions `connect_upstream` and `handshake_reason` (h2mitm.rs:988, :991), and `binds_identity_to_the_connection`'s doc is referenced from `relay` at h2mitm.rs:562 as ``[`binds_identity_to_the_connection`]`` — that one becomes a cross-module link needing `self::pool::` or a `use`.

Cycles: none; `pool` and `body` are leaves under `h2mitm/mod.rs`.

### Autres observations

**1. A real documentation defect, verified, worth fixing whether or not you split anything.** `src/sandbox/proxy/websocket.rs:965-978` is `relay_websocket`'s doc comment ("Relay an established bidirectional connection (a WebSocket) between the cage `client` and the `upstream`…"). It is followed at websocket.rs:979 by `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` and then at websocket.rs:980-983 by a *second* doc comment belonging to `TunnelObservers`, which is declared at websocket.rs:984. So the doc and the `#[allow]` both attach to `TunnelObservers` — a struct, for which `too_many_arguments` is meaningless — while the function they were written for sits undocumented 57 lines further down at websocket.rs:1041. That is exactly why `("sandbox/proxy/websocket.rs", "relay_websocket")` appears in the `UNDOCUMENTED_MODULE_ITEMS` allowlist at `src/docs_coverage.rs:935`. This happened when `TunnelObservers` was inserted between a function's doc and the function. The fix is three lines of movement — put websocket.rs:965-978 and the `#[allow]` back immediately above websocket.rs:1041 — after which docs_coverage.rs:935 must be deleted, or the staleness assertion at src/docs_coverage.rs:1082-1089 fires ("names items that are now documented or gone"). `relay_websocket` takes 7 parameters, and clippy's default `too_many_arguments` threshold warns at 8, so the `#[allow]` is not needed at all and can simply be dropped.

**2. Two modules I considered extracting from mod.rs and decided against.** `admit_absolute_form` and its three types (`RawRequest`:1807, `Plane`:1817, `AbsoluteForm`:1849, `admit_absolute_form`:1869-1983) is ~185 lines that reads as a candidate file. Leave it in mod.rs: its own doc (mod.rs:1874-1883) is the clearest statement in the directory of *why* the vocabulary exists — "the per-plane copy is precisely the mistake `wire::inspect_framing` exists to have fixed… steps 1, 3 and 4 were still copied, and now are not" — and putting it in a side file weakens the module doc's argument at exactly the point it is being made. Same call on the refusal writers (`refusal_body`:1673, `write_refusal`:1726, `refuse_unreadable_head`:1757, `unreadable_head_detail`:1788, `write_all_str`:1798, `invalid`:1985, `CONNECTION_CAP`:301, `UNREADABLE_HEAD`:324): ~130 lines, referenced by every plane, and mod.rs's reason table (mod.rs:89-111) is the documentation for them. Both stay.

**3. The one abstraction that would collapse several near-identical blocks and that I did NOT propose.** The relay tail — `relay_response_head` → empty-head `upstream-closed` → `set_status`/401 → `FramedBody` + `CountingReader` + `CaptureReader` + `pump_redacting`-or-`pump_to_eof` → the reuse triple — appears three times: tunnel.rs:795-899, forward.rs:515-607, cleartext.rs:267-295. A shared `relay_and_finish(...)` would fold all three. I left it out of the proposal because the three genuinely diverge in what they may announce (tunnel is the only one that offers `ClientLeg::MayReuse`, cleartext never masks and never parks) and folding them means a function with three behaviour flags — which is how a shared decision turns back into three decisions wearing one name. The two pieces of it that are *identical* in all three (`masks_reflection`, and the `set_status` + 401 pair) are in the `exchange.rs` proposal; the rest should stay written out.

**4. `tests.rs` (10,357 lines) and `bench.rs` (1,363, gated `#[cfg(test)] mod bench` at mod.rs:181-182) are out of scope but constrain every proposal here.** `tests.rs` is a flat `mod tests` whose only imports are `use super::ca::CertResolver;`, `use super::splice::{SpliceGuard, splice_copy};` and `use super::*;` (tests.rs:1-3). That single glob is why none of the eight splits above requires touching it — and it is also why every new child module must be glob-re-exported from mod.rs rather than merely declared. If someone later replaces the glob with explicit imports, the cost of all eight proposals rises sharply.

**5. Two `UpstreamPool` types.** `src/sandbox/proxy/pool.rs:85` (HTTP/1.1, take-and-return, `Mutex`, keyed by host+port+credential-ids) and `src/sandbox/proxy/h2mitm.rs:909` (HTTP/2, shared-not-returned, `RefCell`, keyed by credential-ids alone). Both are correct and the h2 one's doc (h2mitm.rs:897-907) explains the difference well. This is not a defect, but it is a hazard for any glob re-export, and it is worth knowing before proposal 8 is attempted.

**6. `ctx.rs` needs nothing.** At 998 lines (811 code) it is a struct with ~20 `with_*` builders and a set of observation chokepoints (`outcome`:393, `outcome_l7`:424, `push_log`:507, `set_status`:569, `register_flow`:372, `begin_capture`:340) that are deliberately the single funnel every plane records through — ctx.rs:145-152 and ctx.rs:161-166 both say so explicitly. Splitting the builders away from the chokepoints would put the fields and the one function that must see all of them in different files. Leave it.

**7. `wire.rs` needs nothing beyond the absorb.** 873 code lines, one concern, correctly named, tests already local (wire.rs:873-1667). After proposal 3 it is ~1,020 code lines and self-contained, and its `use super::*;` at wire.rs:7 could then be narrowed to explicit imports — a small follow-up that would make the directory's dependency shape readable from the import lines alone.

---

## `store-storage` — src/store.rs (3832), src/storage.rs (2612), src/sandbox/projectstore.rs, src/config/view.rs (2600), src/config/manage.rs (3011)

**Verdict : **Découpage utile****

### Cartographie actuelle

FIVE MODULES, AND WHAT EACH ACTUALLY HOLDS.

Headline line counts are misleading: roughly half of each file is an inline `#[cfg(test)]` block. Real code sizes are store.rs 2,090 (tests 2091-3832), storage.rs 1,554 (tests 1555-2612), projectstore.rs 670 (tests 671-1458), view.rs 1,909 (tests 1910-2600), manage.rs 1,597 (tests 1598-3011).

=== src/store.rs — claims "the user-owned, daemonless nix store"; holds four unrelated subjects ===
Internal sections (verified; 425, 940, 981, 1564, 1602 are blank separator lines):
 * 19-41 — channel constants: DEFAULT_SOURCE, NIXPKGS_FLAKE_PREFIX, NIXPKGS_LOCK, MISE_ENGINE_LOCK.
 * 43-424 — DATA-DIRECTORY RESOLUTION: `Layout` (46) + impl (50-237: from_env, from_env_without_mounting, default_data_dir, under, store_dir, data_dir, engine_dir, plugins_dir, stores_dir, apt_keys_dir, store_path, icon_path), follow_volume (247), mounted_volume (267), refusal_unspoken (294), data_dir_overridden (303), SUN_PATH_MAX/LONGEST_SOCKET_SUFFIX/DATA_DIR_MAX (311-326), check_data_dir_override (337), check_resolved_data_dir (367), data_dir_from (390), ensure (413).
 * 426-939 — HOST-BINARY RESOLUTION AND TRUST: ENGINE_OVERRIDE_ENV, resolve_nix (449), resolve_nix_store (458), `mod bundled` (468), `mod proc_shim_blob` (475), PROC_SHIM_NAME, embedded_proc_shim (485), ensure_proc_shim (500), absolute_override, resolve_engine_bin, ensure_owned_engine (576), EngineProbe (617), host_exec_verdict (641), engine_probe, pick_engine_bin (700), engine_sibling, BWRAP_OVERRIDE_ENV, APPARMOR_USERNS_RESTRICT, `mod bundled_bwrap` (767), BwrapSource, BwrapChoice, resolve_bwrap (819), apparmor_userns_restricted, ensure_owned_bwrap (864), pick_bwrap (898), resolve_git (937).
 * 941-980 — nix_command (946) + btrfs_nix_config (974).
 * 982-1565 — CHANNEL/LOCK STATE MACHINE: Origin (985), LockTarget (1016) + impl (1033-1149), global_source, global_lock_path, engine_lock_path, app_lock_path, project_lock_path, read_global_lock, live_base_revisions (1212), live_mise_revisions (1233), resolve_engine_ref, is_pinned_revision, Upgrade (1281), revision_of, resolve_ref (1311), refresh_ref, resolve_source_rev, read_lock, write_lock, resolve_channel_rev, revision_from_metadata, valid_revision (1452); plus the GitHub reachability WITNESS at 1459-1565 (NIXPKGS_WITNESS_BRANCH, Reachability, reachability_url, ahead_by, reachability, verdict, witness_revision).
 * 1565-1601 — physical_path (1578): pure logical→physical derivation, clamped to the store root.
 * 1603-2090 — PROVISIONING: provision (1617), provision_unfree (1656), provision_command (1682), provision_licensed (1724), CHANNEL_SOURCE_ROOT, root_channel_source (1786), channel_source_path, source_path_from_metadata, provision_flake (1876), provision_expr (1947), expr_stamp_path, expr_digest, reuse_built_expr, write_expr_stamp, select_marked_output (2068).
The layering inside it is already one-directional and acyclic: layout ← {engine, channel, provisioning}, engine ← provisioning. `channel` never calls `engine` — every entry point takes `nix: &Path` as a parameter (LockTarget::resolve 1129, resolve_ref 1311).

=== src/storage.rs — claims "a self-managed, compressed volume for sbx's data directory" ===
 * 40-95 — DEFAULT_LABEL, DEFAULT_IMAGE_NAME, DEFAULT_SIZE_BYTES, MIN_SIZE_BYTES, State (62), default_image (89).
 * 96-251 — POINTER PROTOCOL: POINTER, read_pointer (103), pointer_can_name (148), pointer_tmp_name, write_pointer (196), clear_pointer, OFFERED_MARKER, has_been_offered, mark_offered.
 * 253-546 — MOUNT PLUMBING: ensure_mounted (253), is_valid_label, parse_size, parse_loop_setup, parse_mount, mount_of (326), mount_is_noexec, refuse_noexec, unescape_mountinfo, loop_for (429), same_file, state (465), set_compression, compression.
 * 547-758 — BTRFS SPACE ACCOUNTING: Space (548), ENTRY, device_factor, tally, reclaimable_bytes (628), reported_count, space (677), discard_queue, discard_queue_under, free_bytes.
 * 759-926 — GENERIC TREE COPY: Census (767), census (782), copy_tree (827), set_mtime (894).
 * 925-950 — ImageLock (927), lock_image (929).
 * 943-1118 — MKFS: tool (951), Mkfs (962), resolve_mkfs (989), mkfs_command (1024), run (1100).
 * 1117-1288 — LIFECYCLE: init (1119), up (1189), down (1246), image_bytes, image_capacity.
 * 1287-1421 — HOST FILESYSTEM CLASSIFICATION: FsKind (1289) + impl, fs_kind (1350), fs_kind_of_nearest, nearest_existing, on_btrfs (1396), kernel_supports_btrfs.
 * 1422-1554 — Preflight (1432), is_remote_session, impl Preflight (1465), volume_adds_anything.

=== src/sandbox/projectstore.rs — claims "a per-project, writable nix store seeded from the shared store" ===
670 lines of code, one subject: DIR_MODE/SEQ (72-77), ProjectStore + path derivation (81-123), the shared-store GC lock (128-183), write_marker (186), prepare (225) with closure_of/seed_path/copy_into_place/place_atomically/copy_recursive/place_file/reflink (287-433), the reflink capability probe (434-457), unique/discard, load_db + retry (485-605), gcroots (606-670). Cohesive; nothing here is a candidate for extraction.

=== src/config/view.rs — claims "the presentation-agnostic view model for `sbx config`" ===
 * 27-705 — BASELINE MODEL AND PROJECTORS, types interleaved with their projector by design: PluginView (29), ConfigView (44, 126 lines of fields), EnvVar, BindView, open_views/OpenView, service_views/ServiceView/ServiceReadyView, ProvenanceView + From, app_bind_view, PackageView, MiseView, ToolsView, NixToolView, NonNixToolView, ChannelView, NetDefaultView, NetworkView (401-474), the NET-RULES sub-cluster (475-597: NetRuleKind, RuleSourceView, NetRuleView, project_config_rules, net_rules_view), GuiView, ProcView/proc_view, NotifyView/notify_view, LimitsView, LimitView, BrokerView, TaskView, SecretView.
 * 707-965 — APP MODEL: AppProvisionView (712), AppEnvVar (725), AppNetworkView (737), AppLimitsView (756), AppView (767-860), AppDetailView (861-965).
 * 967-1209 — build (970), apply_ambient_override (991), build_scoped (1017).
 * 1210-1456 — shared projectors: device_paths, task_views, package_view, flake_pinned_rev, tools_view, nixpkgs_channel, engine_channel, network_view, ask_notice_view, ask_timeout_view, fmt_secs, limits_view.
 * 1457-1909 — APP PROJECTORS: app_view (1458), app_limits_view (1559), build_app_detail (1572), app_detail_view (1600-1895), origin_or_inherited (1896).

=== src/config/manage.rs — claims "the presentation-agnostic management engine for the on-disk config files" ===
 * 19-30 Scope; 31-105 ManageError (24 variants); 106-168 EgressList/ProcList/AddOutcome/Written; 170-277 impl Display for ManageError.
 * 278-332 — scope_path, scope_app_path, Layer, resolution_layers.
 * 333-506 — GENERIC DOTTED-KEY EDIT: get (333), SetOutcome, set (390), commit (421), add (446), remove (484).
 * 508-684 — list_at (508), rule_list_verb (551), render_value, space_entries, parsed_array, put_value (603), scalar_value (655).
 * 685-755 validate_layer; 756-789 unset.
 * 780-1256 — RULE VERBS (`[network]`/`[proc]` posture matrix): add_egress_rule (791), add_proc_rule (899), guard_proc_mode (985), new_proc_table, RemoveOutcome (1016), remove_egress_rule (1025), remove_proc_rule (1041), remove_rule_from (1057), egress_rules_in (1129), proc_rules_in (1135), rules_in (1142), remove_from_array (1174), refuse_non_filtering (1191), layer_parent (1203), implicit_table (1232), new_network_table (1239), push_outcome (1249).
 * 1257-1339 — split_key (1258), read_or_empty (1300), write_doc (1324).
 * 1340-1425 — ATOMIC RESTRICTED WRITE: write_text (1341), write_restricted (1378), create_restricted (1410).
 * 1426-1597 — PORTABLE FRAGMENTS: ImportOutcome, export_bundles (1441), import_bundles (1459), export_net_groups (1509), import_net_groups (1535).

OVERLAP: DO THEY?

(1) store.rs ↔ storage.rs is a genuine two-way dependency, and the split between them is by IMPLEMENTATION TECHNOLOGY (nix vs btrfs), not by responsibility. "Where does sbx's data live" is one decision implemented in two mutually-recursive modules: storage.rs owns the pointer that redirects it (POINTER 96, read_pointer 103, write_pointer 196, clear_pointer 222, default_image 89, has_been_offered/mark_offered 236-251); store.rs owns the resolution that follows it (Layout::from_env 63, follow_volume 247, mounted_volume 267, data_dir_from 390). store.rs:252-253 and 268-271 call storage::read_pointer/ensure_mounted/state; store.rs:950 calls storage::on_btrfs. In the other direction storage.rs:989-1021 (`resolve_mkfs`) reaches back into store::Layout::from_env, store::resolve_nix, store::resolve_bwrap, store::LockTarget::global, store::revision_of and store::provision — i.e. into all four of store.rs's internal clusters. A third edge closes a triangle: storage.rs:1483 calls `crate::sandbox::reflink_verdict`, re-exported at src/sandbox/mod.rs:172 from projectstore.rs:443.

(2) store.rs vs projectstore.rs: NO overlap. store.rs owns the shared store and the host's own directories; projectstore.rs owns a per-project copy of it. Their only shared surface is the `Layout` type, and the seam (shared store read-only, project store written) is argued at length in the module doc and must be preserved.

(3) view.rs vs manage.rs: NO overlap. view.rs reads the RESOLVED configuration; manage.rs edits the RAW layer files. The one function that looks like a read in manage.rs — rules_in (1142) with its egress_rules_in/proc_rules_in wrappers — is deliberately paired with remove_rule_from, and its own doc says why ("navigating exactly as [`remove_rule_from`] does so the two cannot disagree about where a rule lives"). Moving it to view.rs would dissolve a stated seam.

NAME vs BOUNDARY, one line each:
 * store.rs — name claims "the nix store"; boundary held is "everything anchored on sbx's data directory, plus every host binary sbx execs, plus the nixpkgs channel pin, plus a GitHub reachability oracle". host_exec_verdict (641) is consumed by sandbox/resolver.rs:683 for plugin-declared programs and has nothing to do with a store.
 * storage.rs — name claims "storage"; boundary held is "a loop-mounted btrfs volume" PLUS two things unrelated to volumes: a general hardlink-preserving tree copier (759-926) and host-filesystem classification (1287-1421, whose `on_btrfs` is consumed by store.rs:950 and sandbox/launch.rs to pick a nix setting).
 * projectstore.rs — name and boundary agree exactly. The one misfit is the reflink capability probe (434-457), a filesystem question re-exported out of the module to serve storage.rs.
 * view.rs — name and boundary agree; the module has simply grown a second, self-contained consumer (the per-app roster and detail views) beside the baseline one.
 * manage.rs — name claims "management engine"; boundary held is four subjects at once: generic dotted-key editing, the `[network]`/`[proc]` posture matrix, atomic restricted file writing, and portable TOML fragment export/import.

### Justification

store.rs is the one I would do first, and on its own it would be strongly recommended: 2,090 lines of code holding four subjects whose internal dependency graph is already one-directional and acyclic (layout ← engine, channel, provisioning; engine ← provisioning), and — verified item by item — the split requires ZERO visibility widenings. Every symbol crossing a proposed boundary (ensure, physical_path, resolve_nix_store, Layout) is already `pub(crate)`. Only two intra-doc links inside the file cross a new boundary (store.rs:1841 and store.rs:2024). All 217 external `store::Layout` references and all ~15 `crate::store::X` intra-doc links from other modules survive unchanged behind `pub(crate) use` re-exports in `store/mod.rs`, because rustdoc resolves re-exported paths. That is an unusually cheap split for an unusually clear payoff. The `channel` cluster in particular is a self-contained state machine (source → lock → pinned revision, with a seeded-not-baked rule spelled out over 60 lines of prose) that a reader currently has to find between the bwrap AppArmor logic and the nix-build argv assembly.

storage.rs, view.rs and manage.rs are worthwhile but not urgent — each is under the project's own 4,000-line pain threshold and each has a coherent module essay at the top. For them I propose only the extractions where the cluster is a DIFFERENT SUBJECT with its own consumers, not merely a large one: the tree copier and the filesystem classifier out of storage.rs, the per-app projection out of view.rs, the rule-verb posture matrix out of manage.rs. I deliberately do NOT propose splitting view.rs by "types here, projectors there": the file interleaves each view struct with the function that builds it (OpenView beside open_views, ServiceView beside service_views), which is a real property worth keeping, and separating them would make every change touch two files.

projectstore.rs is left alone. 670 lines of code, one subject, and its module doc carries the crate's most load-bearing argument (why the shared store stays byte-identical, why atomic rename per store path is sufficient, why no lock of sbx's own is needed). Splitting it would scatter that argument across files where no one reads it.

The one thing I would NOT do is merge store.rs and storage.rs to break their cycle. The cycle is real but its two directions are asymmetric: store→storage is a genuine layering (the data directory may live in a volume), while storage→store exists solely so `resolve_mkfs` can provision btrfs-progs. Isolating that one function into `storage/mkfs.rs` turns a diffuse cycle into a single named file, which is worth more than a merge that would produce a 3,600-line module.

### Découpage proposé

#### `src/store/layout.rs` (~420 lignes)

**Rôle.** Where sbx's data directory is, and how a logical store path maps onto it. Pure path derivation plus the guards that refuse a directory sbx cannot bind sockets under.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/store.rs:43-424 verbatim — `Layout` (46) and its whole impl (50-237: from_env, from_env_without_mounting, default_data_dir, `#[cfg(test)] under`, store_dir, data_dir, engine_dir, plugins_dir, stores_dir, apt_keys_dir, store_path, icon_path), follow_volume (247), mounted_volume (267), refusal_unspoken (294), data_dir_overridden (303), SUN_PATH_MAX/LONGEST_SOCKET_SUFFIX/DATA_DIR_MAX (311-326), check_data_dir_override (337), check_resolved_data_dir (367), data_dir_from (390), ensure (413). PLUS src/store.rs:1565-1601 (`physical_path`), pure derivation over `layout.store_dir()`, matching the struct's own doc ("Pure path derivation — holds no I/O"). Tests that move: store.rs:2104, 2245, 2252, 2262, 2284, 2318, 2345, 2368, 2389, 2578, 2596.

**Risque.** `ensure` (413) reads the PRIVATE field `layout.data_dir` at store.rs:416, and tests at 2247/2373 do the same — which is exactly why `ensure` must stay in this file rather than follow its callers into provisioning.rs. No visibility change needed: Layout, ensure, physical_path, data_dir_overridden are all already `pub(crate)`. No intra-doc link breaks out: [`Layout::from_env`], [`check_data_dir_override`] (336, 359) and [`follow_volume`] (261) all stay in-file. One link breaks INTO this file: store.rs:2024 [`physical_path`] (in reuse_built_expr, which lands in provisioning.rs) must become [`crate::store::physical_path`]. `store/mod.rs` must `pub(crate) use` Layout, physical_path, ensure, data_dir_overridden. No cycle — this is the leaf everything else in store/ depends on.

#### `src/store/engine.rs` (~515 lignes)

**Rôle.** Which host binaries sbx execs (nix, nix-store, bwrap, git, the exec shim), where each may come from, and whether one is trustworthy to run. Nothing about the store's content.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/store.rs:426-939 verbatim — ENGINE_OVERRIDE_ENV (437), resolve_nix (449), resolve_nix_store (458), `mod bundled` (468), `mod proc_shim_blob` (475), PROC_SHIM_NAME (480), embedded_proc_shim (485), ensure_proc_shim (500), absolute_override (525), resolve_engine_bin (542), ensure_owned_engine (576), EngineProbe (617), host_exec_verdict (641), engine_probe (668), pick_engine_bin (700), engine_sibling (737), BWRAP_OVERRIDE_ENV (753), APPARMOR_USERNS_RESTRICT (760), `mod bundled_bwrap` (767), BwrapSource (773) + impl (782), BwrapChoice (798), resolve_bwrap (819), apparmor_userns_restricted (844), ensure_owned_bwrap (864), pick_bwrap (898), resolve_git (937). Tests that move: store.rs:3188, 3208, 3234, 3350, 3377, 3412, 3442, 3474, 3608.

**Risque.** Zero visibility changes — resolve_nix, resolve_nix_store, resolve_bwrap, resolve_git, host_exec_verdict, ensure_proc_shim, embedded_proc_shim, BwrapSource, BwrapChoice are already `pub(crate)`. Every intra-doc link in the range ([`ENGINE_OVERRIDE_ENV`] 441/748, [`resolve_nix`] 456, [`ensure_owned_engine`] 466, [`pick_engine_bin`] 541, [`resolve_bwrap`] 752/759, [`ensure_owned_bwrap`] 765, [`BWRAP_OVERRIDE_ENV`] 774/806) stays in-file. External links survive via re-export: sandbox/proc_enforce.rs:21 and :364 name [`crate::store::ensure_proc_shim`]; sandbox/resolver.rs:683 names [`crate::store::host_exec_verdict`]. THE ONE REAL RISK: `mod bundled` and `mod bundled_bwrap` sit behind `#[cfg(feature = "bundled-nix")]`/`bundled-bwrap`, which default CI never compiles (only `mise run lint-bundled`, mise.toml:241-249, and it needs host nix). Verify the move with `cargo clippy --bins --features bundled-nix,bundled-bwrap -- -D warnings` or a broken `include!(concat!(env!("OUT_DIR"), ...))` path lands green. No cycle: depends only on layout.rs and crate::pathfind.

#### `src/store/channel.rs` (~610 lignes)

**Rôle.** The nixpkgs channel pin: which source a launch resolves against, which lock file pins it, and whether a pinned revision is one nixpkgs history actually contains. Never resolves a binary — every entry point takes `nix: &Path`.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/store.rs:19-41 (DEFAULT_SOURCE, NIXPKGS_FLAKE_PREFIX, NIXPKGS_LOCK, MISE_ENGINE_LOCK — grep-verified used only inside this cluster: 1153, 1159, 1165, 1181, 1221, 1322-1387) PLUS src/store.rs:982-1565 — Origin (985) + impl (994), LockTarget (1016) + impl (1033: global, engine, project, app, source, origin, locked_revision, previously_locked, resolve, refresh), global_source (1150), global_lock_path (1158), engine_lock_path (1164), app_lock_path (1174), project_lock_path (1185), read_global_lock (1196), live_base_revisions (1212), live_mise_revisions (1233), resolve_engine_ref (1263), is_pinned_revision (1273), Upgrade (1281), revision_of (1295), resolve_ref (1311), refresh_ref (1341), resolve_source_rev (1359), read_lock (1380), write_lock (1399), resolve_channel_rev (1418), revision_from_metadata (1442), valid_revision (1452), and the witness block 1459-1565. Tests that move: store.rs:2156, 2167, 2184, 2199, and the whole lock/LockTarget group 2716-3179.

**Risque.** Zero visibility changes — Origin, LockTarget, Upgrade, read_global_lock, live_base_revisions, live_mise_revisions, resolve_engine_ref, is_pinned_revision, revision_of, witness_revision are all `pub(crate)`. All in-range links ([`resolve_ref`] 21, [`LockTarget::project`] 1012, [`LockTarget::seed_from`] 1249/1307, [`Self::engine`] 1129) stay in-file. External links survive by re-export: sandbox/mise.rs:70 [`crate::store::LockTarget::engine`], sandbox/launch.rs:3228 [`crate::store::LockTarget::app`], sandbox/gc.rs:169/170/1156. ONE LINK BREAKS OUT: store.rs:1841, in `source_path_from_metadata` (which lands in provisioning.rs), says "mirroring [`revision_from_metadata`]" — that item is private, so the link becomes [`super::channel::revision_from_metadata`] and `channel` must be declared `pub(crate) mod` for the path to resolve under `--document-private-items`. Outgoing deps unchanged: crate::config::is_valid_app_name (1175), crate::sandbox::nixhub::fetch_url_json (1508), crate::diag. No new cycle. Optional further cut: witness 1459-1565 into its own file; I would NOT — it shares `valid_revision` with read_lock and is called from resolve_source_rev (1368).

#### `src/store/provisioning.rs` (~530 lignes)

**Rôle.** Turning a pinned reference into a built store path: the daemonless nix invocation, the four provision entry points, the expression-stamp short-circuit, and marked-output selection.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/store.rs:941-980 (nix_command 946, btrfs_nix_config 974) PLUS src/store.rs:1603-2090 — provision (1617), provision_unfree (1656), provision_command (1682), provision_licensed (1724), CHANNEL_SOURCE_ROOT (1766), root_channel_source (1786), channel_source_path (1826), source_path_from_metadata (1844), provision_flake (1876), provision_expr (1947), expr_stamp_path (2004), expr_digest (2013), reuse_built_expr (2026), write_expr_stamp (2046), select_marked_output (2068). Also `use sha2::{Digest, Sha256}` from store.rs:12. Tests that move: store.rs:2134, 2401, 2410, 2430, 2461, 2479, 2486, 2559, 2620, 2652, 2673, 3657, 3675, and the whole `mod provision_tests` (3724-3832).

**Risque.** NAME COLLISION IS THE REAL HAZARD, and the repo already documents it: mise.toml:96-97 says rustdoc under `-D warnings` fails on an ambiguous link and cites `load` being "both a module and a function". `store::provision` IS a function, so the module must NOT be named `provision.rs` — [`provision`] appears 7 times in these docs (1634, 1719, 1771, 1858, 1903, 1930, 1974) and each would become ambiguous. Hence `provisioning.rs`. Zero visibility changes otherwise: nix_command, provision, provision_unfree, provision_flake, provision_expr, root_channel_source are `pub(crate)`, and the cross-boundary helpers it calls (ensure, physical_path, resolve_nix_store) are `pub(crate)` too. Two links to fix: 1841 → [`super::channel::revision_from_metadata`]; 2024 → [`crate::store::physical_path`]. In-file links ([`btrfs_nix_config`] 945, [`provision_command`] 1644, [`provision_licensed`] 1677, [`provision_unfree`] 1615/1723, [`provision_expr`] 1750/1867, [`root_channel_source`] 1765, [`select_marked_output`] 2025) stay. Outgoing: crate::storage::on_btrfs (950), crate::config::is_bare_nix_attr (1671) — unchanged; sandbox/gc.rs:95's [`provision_expr`](crate::store::provision_expr) survives via re-export. No cycle: depends on layout.rs + engine.rs, neither of which depends back.

#### `src/storage/fskind.rs` (~140 lignes)

**Rôle.** What kind of filesystem a path sits on. Consulted by two callers that have nothing to do with volumes, so it should not require reading the volume module to find.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/storage.rs:1287-1421 — FsKind (1289) with its impl (1307), fs_kind (1350), fs_kind_of_nearest (1375), nearest_existing (1382), on_btrfs (1396), kernel_supports_btrfs (1407). FsKind, fs_kind, fs_kind_of_nearest, on_btrfs are already `pub(crate)`; re-export them from `storage/mod.rs` so store.rs:950 (`crate::storage::on_btrfs`), sandbox/launch.rs and cli/doctor.rs (`storage::fs_kind`) need no edit. In-file links [`FsKind`] and [`FsKind::is_cow`] stay in-file. No cycle: this file depends on nothing else in the crate.

#### `src/storage/copy.rs` (~170 lignes)

**Rôle.** A hardlink-preserving, mode- and mtime-faithful tree copier plus its verification census. A general utility with one caller (the data-directory migration in cli/storage.rs), currently buried in the middle of the volume module.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/storage.rs:759-926 — Census (767), census (782), copy_tree (827), set_mtime (894). Zero visibility changes, zero intra-doc links crossing the range in either direction. `storage/mod.rs` re-exports Census/census/copy_tree so cli/storage.rs is untouched. No cycle: depends only on std and libc.

#### `src/storage/mkfs.rs` (~160 lignes)

**Rôle.** Finding or provisioning `mkfs.btrfs` and building the hardened invocation that runs it. Its value is dependency hygiene: this is the ONLY place storage reaches back into store or into sandbox, so isolating it turns a diffuse store↔storage cycle into one named file.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/storage.rs:943-1098 — tool (951), Mkfs (962) + impl origin (975), resolve_mkfs (989), mkfs_command (1024). `run` (1100) does NOT move: it is shared with init (1177), up (1207, 1213) and down (1253, 1260, 1264). Tests that move: storage.rs:2231 (a_provisioned_mkfs_runs_hardened_with_only_what_it_needs).

**Risque.** One widening: `tool` (951) is called from up (1191) and down (1248), which stay behind, so it must move here as `pub(super) fn tool` (or stay in mod.rs and be called as `super::tool` — grep confirms exactly three call sites: 951 def, 1191, 1248). `Mkfs` and `resolve_mkfs` are `pub(crate)`; re-export so cli/storage.rs is untouched. Outgoing deps concentrate here: crate::store::{Layout, resolve_nix, resolve_bwrap, LockTarget, revision_of, provision} at 995-1015, and crate::sandbox::seccomp::{memfds, argv_prefix} at 1041-1043. CYCLE NOTE: this does not REMOVE the store↔storage cycle — Rust permits it within a crate and it compiles today — it makes it legible and one file wide. If you want it gone, the follow-up is moving resolve_mkfs into src/cli/storage.rs, its only caller; I stop short of proposing that because it would drag mkfs_command's seccomp assembly into the CLI layer.

DOCS-COVERAGE GUARD: src/docs_coverage.rs:891 carries `("config/view.rs", "NetworkView")` in UNDOCUMENTED_MODULE_ITEMS; NetworkView stays behind, so that key must be rewritten to `("config/view/mod.rs", "NetworkView")` or the guard fails BOTH ways at once — undocumented (new path unlisted) and stale (docs_coverage.rs:1081-1090 asserts every listed entry still matches). Intra-doc: view.rs:1571 says "Pure data gathering, like [`build`]" — `build` stays in mod.rs, so that becomes [`super::build`]. `view/mod.rs` re-exports AppView, AppDetailView, build_app_detail (used from cli/config.rs and main.rs). #### `src/config/manage/rules.rs` (~600 lignes)

**Rôle.** The `[network]` and `[proc]` posture matrix — the one part of the management engine that is not generic dotted-key editing but a rule system with its own bootstrap, promotion and inertness rules.
