ManageError, Written, AddOutcome, RemoveOutcome, EgressList, ProcList and every public verb are already `pub(crate)`; re-export from `manage/mod.rs` so the 32 references in cli/net.rs, 24 in main.rs, 20 in cli/proc.rs, 20 in cli/config.rs, 11 in cli/app.rs and 2 in cli/completion.rs need no edit. Intra-doc: [`remove_rule_from`] (3 refs), [`egress_rules_in`], [`remove_egress_rule`], [`remove_proc_rule`], [`add_egress_rule`], [`add_proc_rule`], [`AddOutcome`], [`RemoveOutcome::NotPresent`] all stay in-file; [`ManageError::DenyNeedsPosture`], [`::NonFilteringPosture`], [`::NotScalar`] become [`super::ManageError::…`]. TEST-HELPER COST: the inline test module's `doc_at(dir, body)` helper (manage.rs:1602) is used by both halves — hoist it into `manage/mod.rs` as a `#[cfg(test)] pub(super) fn` rather than duplicating it. No cycle: rules.rs depends on mod.rs only.

### Autres observations

TWO ACTUAL DEFECTS, both a doc comment accidentally pasted onto itself on one line (`grep -n '^///.*///'` finds exactly these two across the five files):
 * src/storage.rs:464 — `/// Report where the volume stands, without changing anything./// Report where the volume stands, without changing anything.` The duplicate renders inside the first line of `state`'s documentation.
 * src/config/view.rs:1013 — `/// Assemble the view restricted to one configuration \`source\`/// Assemble the view restricted to one configuration \`source\` — the single-source ...` Same shape, on `build_scoped`.
Both are one-line fixes, independent of any split.

CODE THAT BELONGS IN AN EXISTING MODULE:
 * src/config/manage.rs:1340-1425 (`write_text`, `write_restricted`, `create_restricted`) is the WRITE half of the gate whose READ half is src/config/safety.rs (176 lines: `verdict` 22, `check_safe_file` 51, `read_safe_bytes` 73). They share one threat model and each says so in prose — create_restricted's doc (manage.rs:1398-1409) argues about O_NOFOLLOW and unlink-then-create against in-cage code that can pre-create a temp name in a project tree; safety.rs's header argues about fstat-on-the-open-descriptor closing a rename window. Moving the three into safety.rs would put the whole config-file trust boundary in one ~260-line module. HONEST COST: `write_text` returns `Result<(), ManageError>`, so the move needs its signature changed to `io::Result<()>` with mapping at its two callers (`write_doc` at manage.rs:1326, and cli/bundle.rs), plus renaming `manage::write_text` → `safety::write_text` at that CLI site. That signature change is why this is an observation rather than a proposed module.
 * src/sandbox/projectstore.rs:434-457 (`supports_reflink`, `reflink_verdict`) is a FILESYSTEM CAPABILITY PROBE, not a project-store concern. It has been re-exported out of the module (src/sandbox/mod.rs:172) precisely so two non-sandbox callers can reach it: src/storage.rs:1483 (`Preflight::probe`) and src/cli/store.rs:151. Moving the pair to `src/storage/fskind.rs` beside FsKind/on_btrfs removes the storage→sandbox edge entirely — projectstore.rs then calls `crate::storage::supports_reflink` at its line 250, the direction the rest of the crate already runs. Cost: delete src/sandbox/mod.rs:172; cli/store.rs:151 changes `sandbox::supports_reflink` → `storage::supports_reflink`; projectstore.rs's [`reflink_verdict`] link (in supports_reflink's doc, ~440) becomes [`crate::storage::reflink_verdict`]; and `reflink_verdict` uses `unique()` (projectstore.rs:458, backed by the module-local `SEQ` at :77) for its probe names, so the moved function needs its own pid+AtomicU64 generator rather than sharing projectstore's.

DEAD / NEAR-DEAD CODE: none found. `embedded_proc_shim` (store.rs:485) is `#[cfg(test)]`-only by design and its doc says why. `ImageLock`'s field (storage.rs:927) and `SharedGcLock`'s (projectstore.rs:128) both carry `#[allow(dead_code)]` with the reason written out (held only for Drop).

A NEAR-DUPLICATE WORTH LEAVING ALONE: `store::host_exec_verdict` (store.rs:641) and `config::safety::verdict` (safety.rs:22) are the same shape — a pure (file_uid, mode, euid) → Result predicate — and differ in exactly one branch (host_exec_verdict accepts uid 0, safety::verdict does not). store.rs:629-640 already documents that difference and why. Collapsing them into one parameterised predicate would hide a security-relevant asymmetry behind a boolean; do not.

GUARD MECHANICS THAT WILL BITE ANY OF THESE MOVES (all in src/docs_coverage.rs):
 * `UNDOCUMENTED_MODULE_ITEMS` (docs_coverage.rs:856-941) is keyed on (src-relative path, item name), and the test at :1081-1090 fails on a STALE entry as well as on a missing doc. Three entries in this scope are path-sensitive: `("storage.rs", "lock_image")` at :941, `("config/view.rs", "NetworkView")` at :891, `("sandbox/projectstore.rs", "acquire_shared_gc_lock")` at :928. Turning storage.rs or view.rs into a directory changes the first two keys to `storage/mod.rs` and `config/view/mod.rs`. `module_level_items` (:960) strips the `src/` prefix and skips only files named `tests.rs`/`*_tests.rs` — `mod.rs` is scanned like any other file.
 * The item regex at docs_coverage.rs:965 matches `fn|struct|enum|trait|union` only, so a bare `mod layout;` declaration needs no doc comment — and per mise.toml:97-98 it must NOT carry an outer `///` block, because that merges with the module's own header and moves the whole block's link resolution into the declaring module's scope. Give each new file an inner `//!` header instead; that is also what every existing multi-file module here (src/sandbox/*.rs, src/config/*.rs) does, and CLAUDE.md's documentation rule expects it.
 * `mise run rustdoc` is `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc --no-deps --document-private-items` (mise.toml:102), so links to PRIVATE items across the new boundaries do resolve — provided the path is spelled out (`super::channel::revision_from_metadata`) and the module is declared `pub(crate) mod`. The failure mode that will actually catch you is AMBIGUITY, which mise.toml:96 names outright: never give a new module the same name as an item re-exported beside it. That is the entire reason `src/store/provisioning.rs` is not called `provision.rs`.
 * None of these moves touches the docs-site guards (`every_cli_verb_has_a_reference_page`, `every_config_field_is_shown_in_the_guide`, `every_shipped_app_profile_is_in_the_catalogue`): no verb, config field or profile changes. And tests/ is CLI-level (it invokes the binary), so no integration test names a Rust path — `grep 'manage::\|view::' src/config/tests.rs` returns 0 too.

WHAT I DID NOT PROPOSE, AND WHY: splitting src/config/view.rs by "types in one file, projectors in another" (the file deliberately places each view struct next to the function that builds it); splitting the `build_scoped` + `ConfigView` pair (one struct literal populating one type — genuinely one thing); splitting src/sandbox/projectstore.rs at all; splitting the reachability witness out of store/channel.rs (it shares `valid_revision` with the lock reader and is called from `resolve_source_rev` at store.rs:1368); extracting storage.rs's btrfs space accounting (547-758) — it is coherent and its ioctl SAFETY argument spans `reported_count` and `space`, which should not be separated; and merging store.rs with storage.rs to break their cycle.

---

## `test-suite` — tests/run.rs (9373), src/config/tests.rs (11317), src/sandbox/proxy/tests.rs (10357), tests/config.rs (5481), tests/net.rs (2816)

**Verdict : **Découpage utile****

### Cartographie actuelle

Five test files, 39,344 lines, 818 `#[test]`s. Two are `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` siblings of production modules (declared at src/config/mod.rs:5840-5841 and src/sandbox/proxy/mod.rs:2058-2059); three are integration suites under tests/, each its own binary.

**tests/run.rs (9,373 lines, 93 tests)** — the cage e2e. Scaffolding 1-157: `sbx()` 13-29 (pins XDG_CONFIG_HOME to a shared `fixture_root().join("isolated-config")`, comment at 14-18), `include!("../src/testroot.rs")` 32, `KillOnDrop` 34-47 (used only at 4026/4198/4231), `TmpDir` 49-84 with `TAG_MAX` 54, `force_remove` 86-104, `run_in` 106-117, `app_in` 119-129, `app_in_args` 131-144, `sbx_in` 146-157. Then tests interleaved with helpers defined wherever the first user happened to sit: `cache_reachable` fn 1115 (first used 279), `github_api_has_quota` 1138, `quota_remaining` 1158, `grpcb_in_reachable` 1197, `postman_echo_reachable` 1210, `stage_signer` 1223, `comm_of`/`comm_settles` 1935/1943, `transient_fetch_failure` 2043 (first used 6080), `wayland_socket` 2060, `read_loopback_until`/`free_loopback_port` 3730/3752, `mise_installs_entries` 5562, `per_project_app_mise_installs` 5579, `project_home_mise_shim` 5813, `project_package_out_link_target` 6250, `project_store_dir` 6364, `project_home_mise_installed` 6378 (first used 5327), `describe_mise_pools` 6424 (first used 5329), `in_store` 6470, `host_serves_notifications` 7999 (first and only use 2677 — 5,300 lines earlier), `cage_scope_tasks_max` 8031, `cage_userns_ready` 8068, `touch_under`/`sbx_data` 8561/8568. Topic runs: launch semantics 159-1111; signers 1113-1931; process model 1933-2035; GUI/D-Bus/portal/fonts/catrust 2037-2946; argv0+zoneinfo 2948-3150; egress/proxy/net-logs/forward/splice 3152-4672; toolchain and backends 4674-6023; flakes/store/gc 6026-6783; secrets 6786-7081; limits/seccomp/devices/gpu/audio 7093-7990; attach/session 7999-8557; `sbx app rm` 8561-8813; gc prune/upgrade hints 8822-8963; fs masks 8966-9253; brokers 9260-9373.

**src/config/tests.rs (11,317 lines, 341 tests)** — flat, no submodules. One test (9-58) sits above the fixtures. Fixture builders are scattered over eight sites: 60-189 (`validate_network` shim, `raw`, `declare_groups`, `net_table_defaults`, `net_field`, `make_groups`), 582-824 (`ro_bind`…`a_header_secret`), 2382-2500, 2793-2806, 3988-3995, 4054-4067, 4160-4165, 4413-4419, 4984-5003, 5399-5614, 5913-5924, 6506-6628, 7088-7200, 7363-7383, 7515-7521, 8328-8429, 8682-8808, 9740-9746. `resolve_no_plugins` — the file's central fixture, 294 call sites — is defined at 7372 with 187 of those uses above it. Topic runs: egress groups 191-580; app layering 826-1041; network table 1043-2180; app-vs-untrusted 2183-2380; ssh_agent+brokers 2382-2792; app limits/origins/proc 2793-3310; `validate_profile` 3311-3492; `merge_app` 3493-3986; env/binds/open/services 3988-4799; packages and backends 4800-6069; postures 6070-7086; secrets 7088-7358; timezone 7385-7508; one-shot overrides 7510-8326 (banner at 7510) wedged between the two secret runs; terse keys and resolver/signer plugins 8328-10167 (banners 8559, 9737); unknown-key + trust-gate sweeps 10168-10796 with their hand-written consts `EVERY_GATED_FIELD` 10368, `REFUSAL_REASONS` 10393, `GATED_REFUSALS` 10405, `TRUSTED_APP` 10481, `OVERRIDING_PROJECT` 10491, `TRUSTED_OVERRIDE_REFUSALS` 10507; notify 10797-11048 (banner 10793); plaintext sources 11050-11317 (banner 11050).

**src/sandbox/proxy/tests.rs (10,357 lines, 186 tests)** — better ordered. A real shared harness at 99-639 (`policy`, `spawn_upstream`, `spawn_upstream_idle`, `spawn_upstream_capturing`, `through_proxy`, `close_and_drain`, `read_one_response`, `through_proxy_clean_close`, `spawn_ws_upstream`, `read_head_until_blank`, `through_proxy_websocket`, `read_until_blank`, `spawn_plain_upstream`, `through_cleartext`) plus `far_deadline` 18. Only two banner comments in 10k lines: 8588 (L4 splice) and 8807 (traffic capture). Topic runs: byte counters 23-96; CA/leaf/handshake 641-708; core relay and mute 710-1016; websocket 1018-1457; cleartext plane 1460-1732; absolute-form https forward 1734-2384; response framing and head reading 2386-2900; keepalive/pool/tunnels 2902-4463; notice/403/log redaction 4465-4620; head deadlines 4622-5007; bodies, dechunk, budget 5009-5738; framing refusals 5740-5990; ask/park and session overlays 5992-6501; verdict/SSRF/builtins 6503-6917; injections 6919-7107; redactions and stat buckets 7109-7583; cross-plane sweep 7701-7772; refresh and observed credentials 7774-8142; response masking and `pump_redacting` 8145-8586; L4 splice 8588-8805; traffic capture 8807-10080; cross-plane invariants 10082-10357. Same late-definition pattern: `digesting_injection` defined 5076 first used 1806; `injection` defined 6919 first used 1167; `answer_when_parked` defined 6395 first used 2128; `run_with_injections_and_redactions` defined 7585 first used 5106; `capturing_ctx`/`one_capture` defined 8811/8846 first used 5479/5502. Eleven redundant `#[cfg(test)]` attributes (8810-9873) on helpers already inside a cfg'd module.

**tests/config.rs (5,481 lines, 132 tests)** — the only suite in scope with a proper fixture object: `TmpDir` 16-49, `force_remove` 51-68, `struct Fixture` 73-79 (proj/config_home/state_home/data_home/bind_dir) and `impl` 81-190 (`write_global`, `write_project`, `write_mise`, `write_profile`, `write_plugin`, `write_plugin_exec`, `source_plugin`, `bind_target`, `sbx`, `sbx_in`, `run`, `run_in`). Runs: `config show`/JSON 195-488; mise file 491-553; layering and binds 556-1049; network/trust gates 1052-1352; **cage launches 1355-1711** (6 tests, the only `skip_incapable!` sites bar one); **plugin surface 1714-2193** (10 tests + `git_available`/`git_in` 2083-2119); apps and profiles 2196-3499; catalogue 3502-3622; bundles 3633-4087; `config get/set/unset/path/edit` 4148-4520; key-write trust 4523-4716; postures 4719-5142; fs 5145-5235; views 5238-5481.

**tests/net.rs (2,816 lines, 66 tests)** — the smallest and cleanest: zero skip macros, entirely host-side. `TmpDir` 13-33, `struct Fixture` 37-42 + impl 44-98 (`write_global`, `write_profile`, `write_project`, `run`, `run_without_a_trust_store`). Runs: rules 101-296; allow/deny/mute writes 299-793; groups 796-1131; app-scoped writes 1134-1359; catch-all and ask 1362-1489; pending 1492-2375; `test net` 2380-2766; session flags 2773-2816. Nine sites hand-roll a fake live session (control socket at 1643/1741/1853/2046/2126/2171/2204/2269/2330, session registry record at 1712-1737/1825-1850/2099-2121/2243-2264, `/proc/<pid>/stat` field-22 parsed inline at 1716-1720, 1830-1834, 2104-2108, 2248-2252).

### Justification

The duplication is measurable and the split is mechanical, but the two are separable and only one of them is urgent.

**The fixture duplication is the real finding, and it is bigger than the five files in scope.** `struct TmpDir` is declared in 21 files (src/testutil.rs plus every suite under tests/); `force_remove` is copy-pasted verbatim in 11 suites (tests/attach.rs:56, config.rs:53, detach.rs:59, doctor.rs:50, fs.rs:40, proc.rs:41, run.rs:88, sessions.rs:58, shell.rs:54, stop.rs:57, upgrade.rs:43); the `/proc/<pid>/stat` field-22 start-tick parser exists nine times under tests/ under four different names (app.rs:569 `start_ticks`, fs.rs:107 / logs.rs:50 / proc.rs:216 `read_start_ticks`, net.rs:1716/1830/2104/2248 inline, run.rs:8781 inline) against the one production copy at src/session.rs:752; and five suites (app.rs:36, config.rs:73, net.rs:37, path.rs:39, projects.rs:52) declare a near-identical `Fixture { proj, config_home, state_home, data_home }` whose `sbx()` sets the same three XDG variables plus `LC_ALL=C.UTF-8` / `env_remove("LANG")`. Inside tests/run.rs alone the launch preamble is written out by hand 76 times (`join(".sbx.toml")`), the capability probe 65 times (`run_in(project.path(), data.path(), &["true"])` followed by an eight-line `skip_incapable!` block differing only in the message), and the trust step 58 times (`&["trust", ".sbx.toml"]` plus its assertion). tests/common/mod.rs already exists, is `#[macro_use] mod common;`-ed by 16 suites, and carries `#![allow(dead_code)]` at line 12 precisely so a helper only one suite needs is not a warning in the others — the home is built and empty.

**That duplication is where the coverage gap actually lives.** Because the probe is retyped rather than called, it drifts. Two tests provision `nix:` packages in their *trusted* launch and carry no reachability gate at all: `a_trusted_audio_posture_binds_the_pulseaudio_socket_into_the_cage` (tests/run.rs:7495-7602, declares `alsautils = "nix:alsa-utils"` at 7516) and `a_trusted_audio_posture_equips_a_python_portaudio_tool` (7616-7746, declares `python312` and `uv` at 7641). Every comparable provisioning e2e in the file calls `cache_reachable()` and `skip_unreachable!`s; these two go red on a capable but offline host where the other 46 skip. A single `probe_or_skip!` helper makes that impossible by construction. The second gap is one of visibility: 83 of the 93 tests in tests/run.rs are gated, so on a host without userns/bwrap/nix the whole 9,373-line binary reports green having run ten tests — `run_detach_without_a_command_is_a_usage_error` (160-172), `a_malformed_one_shot_override_is_a_hard_error_and_does_not_launch` (333-378), `app_run_treats_a_subcommand_verb_as_an_app_name` (888-922), `the_quota_probe_reads_the_budget_that_governs_a_tool_install` (1178-1193) and the six `sbx_app_rm_*` tests (8577-8813). Nothing says which ten. The same shape in src/config/tests.rs: the field-by-field trust sweeps that a contributor adding a gated field must extend are driven by hand-written consts at 10368-10520, buried at line 10,400 of an 11,317-line file with no banner above them until 10793.

**The splits themselves buy less, and I want to be honest about that: not one of them adds a line of coverage.** src/sandbox/proxy/tests.rs is the weakest case — it is genuinely well ordered, it has one real shared harness at 99-639 rather than eight scattered ones, and `cargo test --bin sbx sandbox::proxy::tests::<filter>` already targets it. I propose splitting it only because its children map one-for-one onto the production submodules they exercise (ca.rs, cleartext.rs, forward.rs, tunnel.rs, websocket.rs, splice.rs, capture.rs, inject.rs, ssrf.rs, wire.rs are all real files beside it), so the split is a rename rather than a judgement, and because the file is now five times the size of the mod.rs it tests. src/config/tests.rs is the stronger case only because its clusters are not contiguous (secrets 7088-7358, overrides 7510-8326, secrets again 8328-10167) and no cut is possible without re-sequencing.

**tests/run.rs is the one where the split changes what a developer can do.** CLAUDE.md's own rule is "ALWAYS prefer targeted tests over the full suite", and today the finest grain available below `cargo test --test run` is a name filter — you cannot ask for "the egress e2es" or "the desktop e2es". .github/workflows/ci.yml:211-215 already runs `cargo test --test "$t"` per changed suite, so smaller suites give CI finer granularity for free. The cost is three more test binaries linking the full crate; cargo runs test targets one at a time (mise.toml:151 relies on this), so nothing about store contention or wall-clock on the cage job changes.

**tests/net.rs should be left alone.** 2,816 lines, 66 tests, zero skip macros, one coherent verb family, and it is the only suite in scope that already reads as a designed fixture. It should adopt the shared `Project` and the shared fake-session helper and stop there.

### Découpage proposé

#### `tests/common/mod.rs (rework, not a new file)` (~130 lignes)

**Rôle.** Turn the existing sweep-helper module into the shared test-support root for every integration suite. It is already `#[macro_use] mod common;`-ed by 16 suites and already carries `#![allow(dead_code)]` (line 12) for exactly this case.

**Ce qui bouge.** Nothing moves out. Added: `include!("../../src/testroot.rs")` (so `fixture_root()` has one definition per binary instead of 20), a `pub fn tmp_root() -> std::path::PathBuf { fixture_root() }` wrapper because the included `fn fixture_root` is private and cannot be `pub use`d, and the declarations `pub mod fixture; pub mod cage; pub mod session;`. The existing `walk`/`page_paths`/`oracle`/`sbx`/`stdout_of` (lines 23-110) stay put. The eight suites that do not yet include it — tests/app.rs, argv.rs, color.rs, logs.rs, net.rs, path.rs, session_logs.rs, trust.rs — gain `#[macro_use] mod common;` and drop their own `include!("../src/testroot.rs")`.

**Risque.** Ordering is load-bearing: `include!("../../src/testskip.rs")` at line 18 defines `macro_rules! skip_incapable` / `skip_unreachable`, which are textually scoped, so the three `pub mod` declarations MUST come after it or the submodules cannot use the macros. Both `include!`s must precede them for the same reason. No cyclic risk (tests/common depends on nothing). No rustdoc exposure — `tests/` is not documented by `cargo doc --no-deps`. src/docs_coverage.rs's `crate_and_test_sources()` (line 1108) does walk `tests/`, so the `a_paragraph_break_inside_a_doc_comment_is_written_as_one` guard applies to the new doc comments; carry blocks verbatim rather than re-wrapping them.

#### `tests/common/fixture.rs` (~240 lignes)

**Rôle.** The one temp-directory type and the one four-home project fixture, replacing 21 `struct TmpDir` declarations, 11 copies of `force_remove`, and five near-identical `struct Fixture`es.

**Ce qui bouge.** `TmpDir` + `TAG_MAX` + `impl Drop` from tests/run.rs:49-84 (the tagged, length-budgeted variant — it is the strictest, because of the 108-byte `sun_path` cap the comment at 60-69 documents, so it is the one that must win); `force_remove` from tests/run.rs:86-104 (identical text to config.rs:51-68 and the nine others). A `pub struct Project` merged from tests/config.rs:73-190 and tests/net.rs:37-98: fields `proj`, `config_home`, `state_home`, `data_home`, `bind_dir`; methods `write_global`, `write_project`, `write_mise`, `write_profile`, `write_plugin`, `write_plugin_exec`, `source_plugin`, `bind_target`, `sbx`, `sbx_in`, `run`, `run_in`, `run_without_a_trust_store` (the last from net.rs:82-98). tests/config.rs and tests/net.rs then keep an alias `use common::fixture::Project as Fixture;` so their 198 call sites are untouched; tests/app.rs:36-64, path.rs:39-69 and projects.rs:52-80 collapse onto it too.

**Risque.** **This breaks a guard, and the guard must be re-based rather than relaxed.** src/testutil.rs:517-607 `every_fixture_root_is_the_shared_one` asserts `declaring >= 20` (line 604-607, message "only {declaring} files declare a fixture directory — has the sweep gone stale?"), where `declaring` counts files containing the literal `struct TmpDir`. There are exactly 21 today; after this consolidation there are 2 (src/testutil.rs and tests/common/fixture.rs) and the sweep fails on its own staleness precondition. The property it protects — no suite roots its fixtures anywhere but `fixture_root()` — is unchanged and in fact becomes trivially true; what has to change is the needle. Re-base it to count files that *reach* a fixture tree (`TmpDir::new(` / `Project::new(`), keep the existing rule that any file declaring `struct TmpDir` must contain `fixture_root()`, keep the `hardcoded` half (562-573) untouched, and rewrite the doc block at 493-516 to say what it now guards. Do not lower the number and leave the prose. Second risk: tests/config.rs's `TmpDir::new()` takes no tag while run.rs's takes one, so every call site in config.rs/net.rs/app.rs/path.rs/projects.rs needs a tag argument added — mechanical, and the compiler names every site. Third: tests/net.rs:29-32 uses `remove_dir_all` where everyone else uses `force_remove`; adopting `force_remove` there is strictly safer (it handles a 0555 nix store) and changes nothing net.rs relies on.

#### `tests/common/cage.rs` (~420 lignes)

**Rôle.** The launch preamble and the host/remote reachability probes — the code that decides whether an e2e runs, said once. This is the file that closes the gate-drift gap.

**Ce qui bouge.** From tests/run.rs: `sbx()` 13-29 (the `LC_ALL`/`LANG` and isolated-`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` builder), `run_in` 106-117, `app_in` 119-129, `app_in_args` 131-144, `sbx_in` 146-157, `KillOnDrop` 34-47, `cache_reachable` 1113-1123, `github_api_has_quota` 1125-1151, `quota_remaining` 1153-1175, `grpcb_in_reachable` 1195-1205, `postman_echo_reachable` 1207-1218, `transient_fetch_failure` 2037-2055, `wayland_socket` 2057-2072, `host_serves_notifications` 7999-8029, `cage_scope_tasks_max` 8031-8066, `cage_userns_ready` 8068-8117, `comm_of` 1933-1941, `comm_settles` 1942-1952, `read_loopback_until` 3725-3748, `free_loopback_port` 3750-3756. Plus two new macros that replace hand-written text: `probe_or_skip!(project, data, "<label>")` for the 65-fold block at run.rs:181-188 and its clones, and `need_cache!("<label>")` for the 46-fold `if !cache_reachable() { skip_unreachable!(…); return; }`. The unit test `the_quota_probe_reads_the_budget_that_governs_a_tool_install` (run.rs:1177-1193) moves here with `quota_remaining`, the function it tests.

**Risque.** The new macros must be declared before the modules that use them or, being `macro_rules!`, be `#[macro_export]`-free and reached by textual order — declare them in this file and have suites `use common::cage::*;` for the fns while the skip macros keep coming through `#[macro_use] mod common;`. `sbx()` at run.rs:19 builds `fixture_root().join("isolated-config")` and its comment (14-18) argues the shared dir is race-free because "no run.rs test writes a global config there"; once four suites share the builder that sentence is false as written and must be re-scoped to "no suite in the run family writes a global config there" — the fact still holds (the profile-import test at 987-1038 sets its own `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`), but the prose is now a claim about more files. `KillOnDrop` has only three users today (run.rs:4026, 4198, 4231), all landing in tests/run_egress.rs; `#![allow(dead_code)]` from tests/common/mod.rs:12 covers the suites that do not use it. No cycle. Adopting `probe_or_skip!` at run.rs:7495-7602 and 7616-7746 is what turns those two tests from red-when-offline into skip-when-offline, so do not port them by hand.

#### `tests/common/session.rs` (~150 lignes)

**Rôle.** The fake live session — a registry record plus a bound control socket plus a one-shot answering thread — instead of nine hand-rolled copies in tests/net.rs and one in tests/run.rs.

**Ce qui bouge.** `start_ticks(pid) -> Option<u64>` lifted from tests/app.rs:569-574 (the only copy that returns `Option` rather than unwrapping), replacing the inline blocks at tests/net.rs:1716-1720, 1830-1834, 2104-2108, 2248-2252 and tests/run.rs:8778-8781 and the near-duplicates at tests/fs.rs:107-111, tests/logs.rs:50-54, tests/proc.rs:216-220. A `register_session(data_home, pid, runtime, project_path)` writing the `kind=/pid=/start=/runtime=/project=` record under `<data>/sbx/sessions/<pid>-<start_ticks>` with the hex-encoded canonical project path — the block duplicated at tests/net.rs:1711-1737, 1825-1850, 2099-2121, 2243-2264 and tests/run.rs:8770-8793. A `fake_control_socket(data_home, pid, expect_prefix, reply) -> JoinHandle<()>` for the listener+thread pattern at tests/net.rs:1643-1656, 1741-1753, 1853-1866, 2046-2060, 2126-2140, 2171-2185, 2204-2218, 2269-2283, 2330-2344.

**Risque.** The record format is production-coupled (net.rs:1709 calls it "session.rs's serializer"), so the helper's doc must name src/session.rs as the definition it mirrors, the way the existing inline comments do — a shared helper that silently drifts from the serializer is worse than nine copies that each say what they are imitating. `register_session` needs `use std::os::unix::ffi::OsStrExt` for the hex encoding, already imported by tests/app.rs:7 and used inline in net.rs. No visibility change in src/. No cycle.

#### `tests/run_desktop.rs` (~1360 lignes)

**Rôle.** Everything that needs a compositor, a session bus, a sound server or a device node — the tests whose skips are about desktop hardware rather than about the cage.

**Ce qui bouge.** From tests/run.rs: `a_gui_wayland_launch_connects_to_the_host_compositor` 2074-2144, `a_gui_isolated_cage_gets_a_dummy_interface_a_non_gui_one_does_not` 2147-2220, `a_gui_dummy_interface_opens_no_egress_the_allowlist_still_filters` 2223-2317, `a_gui_wayland_launch_provisions_fonts_the_cage_can_find` 2320-2430, `an_offscreen_gui_posture_provisions_fonts_without_exposing_a_display` 2433-2542, `a_trusted_dbus_stands_up_an_in_cage_portal` 2545-2629, `a_trusted_in_cage_notifications_relay_attaches_and_forwards` 2632-2759, `a_keyfile_rewrite_makes_the_in_cage_portal_re_emit_setting_changed` 2762-2865, `catrust_purges_stale_cas_so_the_nss_db_never_accumulates` 2868-2946, `a_gui_wayland_launch_composes_with_a_network_allowlist` 4499-4603, `a_trusted_gpu_posture_grants_the_render_node_and_sys_to_the_cage` 7399-7483, `a_trusted_audio_posture_binds_the_pulseaudio_socket_into_the_cage` 7495-7602, `a_trusted_audio_posture_equips_a_python_portaudio_tool` 7616-7746, `a_synthetic_xdg_open_surfaces_the_url_and_exits_zero` 5164-5197.

**Risque.** **Must be added to two hardcoded suite lists in the same commit**: mise.toml:123 and .github/workflows/cage.yml:287 both read `for suite in attach completion config detach doctor fs netns plugins proc projects run sessions shell stop upgrade;`. src/testutil.rs:711-746 `the_cage_suites_are_the_same_list_in_the_task_and_the_workflow` asserts the two lines are character-for-character the same list, so a one-sided edit fails loudly — the guard protects this move rather than being broken by it, and its `task.len() >= 10` floor (742) only rises. `wayland_socket` and `host_serves_notifications` come from tests/common/cage.rs, not from a local copy. Note that `a_trusted_devices_grant_binds_a_host_device_into_the_cage` (7316-7388) deliberately stays in tests/run.rs: it is a bind-policy test that happens to use `/dev/null`, not a desktop-hardware test. No visibility change anywhere in src/.

#### `tests/run_egress.rs` (~2350 lignes)

**Rôle.** The proxy in the loop: allowlist, MITM, HTTP/2, signers, injected credentials, cleartext, forward, splice, net logs. These are the tests that need both a cage and a reachable network, and they share `stage_signer`, the reachability probes and `KillOnDrop`.

**Ce qui bouge.** From tests/run.rs: `stage_signer` 1220-1257 and the three signer e2es 1283-1641, 1664-1791, 1810-1931; `a_network_allowlist_filters_egress_through_the_proxy` 3153-3305, `a_designated_http2_host_is_man_in_the_middled_as_http2` 3308-3411, `a_configured_secret_injects_and_tripwires_on_the_http2_path` 3414-3521, `a_secret_is_injected_masked_and_stripped_on_the_http2_grpc_path` 3524-3617, `net_learn_synthesizes_a_rule_for_a_refused_host_and_writes_it` 3620-3723, `a_forward_bridges_a_host_loopback_port_into_the_cage` 3758-3866, `a_cleartext_http_rule_forwards_plaintext_egress_through_the_proxy` 3869-3974, `sbx_net_logs_reads_a_running_sessions_live_egress` 3977-4132, `sbx_net_logs_follow_streams_a_running_sessions_egress` 4135-4287, `a_tcp_rule_splices_a_raw_stream_through_the_cage` 4290-4379, `a_network_allow_mode_serves_filtered_egress_through_the_proxy` 4382-4496, `a_shared_network_launch_trusts_sbx_own_cacert` 4606-4672, `an_outbound_secret_is_refused_at_the_proxy` 6972-7081, `the_cage_resolves_localhost_via_a_synthetic_hosts_file` 250-330.

**Risque.** Same two-place cage-list edit as run_desktop.rs, protected by the same guard. `stage_signer` (1220-1257) has all four of its callers here (1334, 1344, 1351, 1358, 1368, 1693, 1844), so it moves whole rather than to common. `read_loopback_until`/`free_loopback_port` come from tests/common/cage.rs because the forward e2e is their only user today but tests/detach.rs and tests/sessions.rs poll loopback the same way. No src/ visibility change; no rustdoc exposure.

#### `tests/run_tools.rs` (~2400 lignes)

**Rôle.** Provisioning: the curated base set, shebang resolution, every package backend and its resolve command, mise pools, flake builds, the store, gc and upgrade. One suite whose every test wants a warm binary cache.

**Ce qui bouge.** From tests/run.rs: `the_curated_base_tools_run_in_the_cage` 4675-4721, `a_usr_bin_env_shebang_resolves_in_the_cage` 4724-4797, the three resolve-command e2es and their upgrade twins 4800-5161, `a_bin_bash_shebang_resolves_in_the_cage` 5200-5250, the mise self-equip and auto-equip family 5253-5556, `mise_installs_entries` 5558-5573 and `per_project_app_mise_installs` 5574-5598 with the two pool-split tests 5599-5806, `project_home_mise_shim` 5808-5830 with the two mise upgrade tests 5832-6023, the flake family 6026-6361 with `project_package_out_link_target` 6247-6256, `project_store_dir` 6362-6376, `project_home_mise_installed` 6377-6422, `describe_mise_pools` 6423-6468 and `in_store` 6469-6476, `sbx_gc_keeps_a_current_flake_build_and_reclaims_a_rolled_away_one` 6478-6667, `sbx_projects_rm_dead_reaps_a_deleted_projects_tree` 6670-6783, `gc_prune_drops_a_superseded_seed_root_and_keeps_the_current_base` 8822-8902, `upgrade_hints_at_reclaimable_superseded_builds` 8909-8963. Plus, from tests/config.rs, the three nixpkgs-pin launches `upgrade_in_a_trusted_pinned_project_rolls_the_per_project_lock` 1583-1615, `upgrade_with_an_untrusted_pin_falls_back_to_the_global_lock` 1618-1656 with its `has_per_project_nixpkgs_lock` helper 1658-1670, and `a_trusted_pin_to_a_different_channel_runs_a_tool_from_that_channel` 1672-1711.

**Risque.** Same two-place cage-list edit, same guard. `project_home_mise_installed` and `describe_mise_pools` are currently defined at 6378/6424 but first used at 5327/5329, so the move also fixes a definition-after-use that made the mise-allowlist test unreadable — but it means the compiler is the only thing verifying the move is complete; run `cargo test --test run_tools --no-run` before anything else. The three tests taken from tests/config.rs use that suite's `Fixture` (`fx.write_global`, `fx.run`), which the shared `Project` in tests/common/fixture.rs supplies verbatim, so they port with an alias rather than a rewrite. Taking them out, together with the plugin-store move described in other_observations, leaves tests/config.rs with zero `skip_incapable!` sites, so `config` must then be **removed** from both suite lists — again, one edit each, and the guard refuses a one-sided one.

#### `src/config/tests/ (directory; src/config/tests.rs stays as the module root)` (~700 lignes)

**Rôle.** Give the 341-test resolution suite the same topic axis the engine has, and put the scattered fixture builders in one place so `resolve_no_plugins` is not defined 7,000 lines below its first caller.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/config/tests.rs KEEPS its name and role as the root — this matters, because src/docs_coverage.rs:997 skips a file named exactly `tests.rs`, so the eight currently-undocumented helpers (`raw` 79, `raw_seccomp` 716, `with_broker` 2426, `pkg` 3988, `get` 3992, `raw_flakes` 4984, `raw_fs` 6617, `raw_secret` 7088) stay outside the doc guard. Into it go the `use` block 1-7, the `validate_network` shim 60-77, and every fixture builder: 79-189, 582-824, 2382-2500, 2793-2806, 3988-3995, 4054-4067, 4160-4165, 4413-4419, 4984-5003, 5399-5414, 5539-5550, 5589-5614, 5913-5924, 6506-6517, 6617-6628, 7088-7200, 7363-7383, 7515-7521, 8328-8429, 8682-8808, 9740-9746 — roughly 700 lines — plus `mod groups; mod apps; mod network; mod postures; mod binds_env; mod packages; mod secrets; mod overrides; mod trust_matrix;`. Children: `groups.rs` ← 191-580; `apps.rs` ← 826-1041, 2183-2380, 2793-3310, 3311-3492, 3493-3986; `network.rs` ← 1043-2180; `postures.rs` ← 2502-2792, 6070-7086, 10797-11048; `binds_env.rs` ← 3997-4052, 4069-4412, 4421-4799; `packages.rs` ← 9-58, 4800-4983, 5005-6069, 11050-11317; `secrets.rs` ← 7202-7358, 8431-10167; `overrides.rs` ← 7385-7508, 7523-8326; `trust_matrix.rs` ← 10168-10796 including the consts `EVERY_GATED_FIELD` 10368, `REFUSAL_REASONS` 10393, `GATED_REFUSALS` 10405, `TRUSTED_APP` 10481, `OVERRIDING_PROJECT` 10491, `TRUSTED_OVERRIDE_REFUSALS` 10507.

**Risque.** **The `validate_network` shim is the sharp edge and it decides the whole shape.** src/config/tests.rs:60-77 deliberately shadows the glob-imported real `validate_network` so 46 bare three-argument calls read as before. Keeping it in the root means each child's `use super::*;` picks up the shim as an *item* (items beat glob imports), so the shadowing keeps working automatically — and the four sites that want the real four-argument function, which today write `super::validate_network`, must become `super::super::validate_network` or `crate::config::validate_network`. Get that wrong and the code compiles against the wrong function silently, which is the one failure mode here the compiler will not catch; grep for `super::validate_network` before and after. **Visibility: none needed.** Each child is a descendant of `config`, so `config`'s private items — including the `#[cfg(test)] use secrets::{validate_header_shape, validate_host_secret, validate_secret_target};` at mod.rs:54-62 and `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate) use schema::RawCmd;` at mod.rs:40 — remain reachable, and a child's `use super::*;` re-globs both the root's fixtures and (transitively, through the root's own `use super::*;`) `config`'s items. **Doc guard:** the nine children are NOT named `tests.rs`, so src/docs_coverage.rs's `module_level_items()` (960-1057) will scan them; every helper they carry needs a `///`. Keeping all shared builders in the root reduces that to the handful of single-topic helpers, and each of those already has a doc block. Do not add entries to `UNDOCUMENTED_MODULE_ITEMS` — the staleness check at 1082-1092 would then require pruning them later. **Intra-doc:** src/config/catalogue_tests.rs:4 links `[`super::tests`]`; the module name is unchanged so it still resolves (and `cargo doc` never enables `cfg(test)` anyway, so no `mise run rustdoc` exposure either way). Test-filter paths change from `config::tests::<name>` to `config::tests::<topic>::<name>`; nothing in mise.toml or the workflows filters on a config test name (the only name-filter in the tree is `sandbox::cgroup::tests::a_dollar_argument_reaches_the_program_verbatim`, ci.yml:202/210 and mise.toml). No cycle: children depend only on the root and on `config`.

#### `src/sandbox/proxy/tests/ (directory; src/sandbox/proxy/tests.rs stays as the module root)` (~760 lignes)

**Rôle.** Name each test cluster after the production submodule it exercises, so the 16 flat children of src/sandbox/proxy/ each have an obvious test file — and so the shared harness stops being 640 lines a reader must scroll past to reach any of it.

**Ce qui bouge.** src/sandbox/proxy/tests.rs keeps its name (again, so docs_coverage.rs:997 keeps skipping it) and holds: the `use` block 1-15, `far_deadline` 18-20, the whole harness 99-639, and the six helpers currently stranded far from their first use — `bulk_payload` 1187-1192, `digesting_injection`/`digesting_injection_for` 5074-5102 (first used at 1806), `answer_when_parked` 6395-6420 (first used at 2128), `injection` 6917-6925 (first used at 1167), `contains_subslice` 7107-7115, `run_with_injections_and_redactions` 7585-7630 (first used at 5106) — plus the `mod` declarations. Children: `ca.rs` ← 23-96, 641-708; `relay.rs` ← 710-1016, 2386-2900, 4465-4620; `websocket.rs` ← 1018-1457 (with `spawn_ws_upstream_bulk` 1194, `through_proxy_ws_bytes` 1238, `spawn_ws_upstream_burst_then_idle` 1325, `through_proxy_ws_read_exact` 1373); `cleartext.rs` ← 1460-1732; `forward.rs` ← 1734-2384; `tunnel.rs` ← 2902-4463 (with `spawn_keepalive_upstream` 2902, `through_proxy_repeatedly` 2960, `reuse_ctx` 3018, `reuse_ctx_allowing` 3028, `spawn_upstream_that_resets_after_one` 3059, `through_one_tunnel` 3399, `through_one_tunnel_paused` 3422, `spawn_counting_forwarder` 3735, `spawn_upstream_per_connection` 4094); `wire.rs` ← 4622-5007, 5009-5738, 5740-5990; `verdict.rs` ← 5992-6917; `inject.rs` ← 6927-7583, 7632-8586 (with `run_with_injections` 6927, `run_with_redactions` 7144, `run_with_refresh` 7632, `run_reflecting` 8145); `splice.rs` ← 8588-8805 (with `spawn_raw_echo` 8592, `loopback_ctx` 8615, `through_proxy_raw` 8627, `splice_connect_reply` 8666); `capture.rs` ← 8807-10080 (with `capturing_ctx` 8811, `one_capture` 8846, `capturing_ws_ctx` 9425, `spawn_leaking_ws_upstream` 9450, `spawn_listening_ws_upstream` 9502, `ws_send_from_cage` 9552, `ws_frame` 9612, `spawn_frame_ws_upstream` 9630, `through_proxy_ws_frames` 9678, `spawn_held_ws_upstream` 9874); `planes.rs` ← 7701-7772 and 10082-10357.

**Risque.** **Respect the deliberate seam in `planes.rs`.** `every_inspected_plane_refuses_the_same_framing_with_the_same_reason` (7701), `a_refusal_says_the_same_thing_whichever_plane_answers_it` (10120), `a_request_head_carrying_a_control_byte_is_refused_on_every_plane` (10164) and `each_inspected_plane_keeps_its_own_scheme_proto_and_chunked_rule` (10347) exist precisely because the check "was written out three times before, and the copies drifted twice" (7691-7694). They must land in one file, with their prose verbatim — including the paragraph at 10156-10159 explaining why the HTTP/2 plane is excluded and pointing at `wire::a_head_carrying_a_byte_another_parser_could_break_a_line_on_is_refused`. Filing any of them under the plane it happens to test first would recreate the drift the sweep exists to prevent. **Visibility: none needed** — `mod ca;` … `mod wire;` at proxy/mod.rs:182-196 are private, but a child of `proxy::tests` is a descendant of `proxy`, so the root's existing `use super::ca::CertResolver;` and `use super::splice::{SpliceGuard, splice_copy};` (lines 1-2) stay in the root and reach the children by glob. The four `super::`-qualified call sites in the moved text — `super::Plane::…` at 10349-10356, `super::dns::Resolver` at 10165, `super::validate_*`-style paths — become `super::super::`; `rg '\bsuper::' src/sandbox/proxy/tests.rs` finds every one. **Doc guard:** the twelve children are scanned by `module_level_items()`; only two helpers in the whole file lack a `///` (`digesting_injection` 5076, `digesting_injection_for` 5080) and both stay in the root, so the cost is zero — but note the eleven redundant `#[cfg(test)]` attributes at 8810, 8845, 9424, 9449, 9496, 9501, 9551, 9611, 9629, 9677, 9873, which are no-ops inside an already-cfg'd module and which currently *also* exempt those helpers from the doc guard; drop them and document the helpers instead of carrying an accidental exemption into the new files. Test-filter paths gain a segment; nothing filters on them. No cycle.

### Autres observations

**Two clusters in tests/config.rs are filed under the wrong verb, and moving them is cheaper than any split.** (1) The plugin surface at 1714-2193 — `a_registered_resolver_plugin_scheme_is_honored_in_a_secret` 1714-1738, `an_unregistered_resolver_scheme_drops_the_secret_with_a_warning` 1741-1763, `plugins_list_shows_installed_plugins_builtins_and_drop_warnings` 1766-1827, `plugins_list_flags_a_non_runnable_executable` 1830-1848, `plugins_info_reports_builtin_unknown_and_a_plugin` 1851-1893, `plugins_info_names_the_writable_state_grant_and_its_location` 1899-1919, `plugins_info_explains_a_dropped_conflicting_scheme` 1922-1961, `plugins_install_then_list_then_remove_through_the_binary` 1964-2027, `plugins_install_refuses_a_colliding_scheme_through_the_binary` 2030-2064, `plugins_install_refuses_a_path_that_is_not_a_plugin` 2067-2079, and `publish_then_add_and_install_through_a_real_clone` 2120-2193 — belongs in tests/plugins.rs, whose header (1-8) already claims exactly this territory. The move also collapses a duplicated helper: tests/config.rs:2083-2107 `git_available`/`git_in` and tests/plugins.rs:174-188 `git_run` are the same function written twice with different identity plumbing (`-c user.email` vs `GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL`), one asserting on `.status()` and one on `.output().status`. (2) The three launch tests at 1355-1399, 1436-1479 and 1482-1517 are cage e2es wearing a config suite's clothes. With those and the nixpkgs-pin trio moved out (see tests/run_tools.rs), tests/config.rs carries no `skip_incapable!` at all and must come off the cage lists at mise.toml:123 and .github/workflows/cage.yml:287 — 5,481 lines the nightly cage job currently pays for to run six tests.

**`sbx_app_rm_*` does not belong in a cage suite either.** tests/run.rs:8577-8630, 8633-8689, 8692-8722, 8725-8743, 8746-8764, 8767-8813 (253 lines, six tests) never launch anything — they write app homes, run `sbx app rm --purge`, and assert on the listing — and they carry no skip macro. They belong in tests/app.rs, which is already the `sbx app` surface and already fabricates app homes (`fixture_with_a_leftover` at 471-489). Their two local helpers `touch_under` 8561-8566 and `sbx_data` 8568-8575 move with them.

**The redundant `#[cfg(test)]` attributes in src/sandbox/proxy/tests.rs are load-bearing by accident.** The eleven at 8810, 8845, 9424, 9449, 9496, 9501, 9551, 9611, 9629, 9677 and 9873 do nothing at compile time (the whole module is already `#[cfg(test)]` per proxy/mod.rs:2058-2059) but they *do* make `module_level_items()` (src/docs_coverage.rs:1017-1030) skip those items. That is invisible today because the file is skipped whole by name; it becomes visible the moment any of them moves into a file not called `tests.rs`. Delete them and let the doc guard see the helpers — all eleven already carry `///` blocks, so nothing has to be written.

**One near-duplicate worth collapsing while the fixtures are being touched.** src/config/tests.rs has two parallel families of app-scoped raw builders — `raw_app` 746-799 / `raw_with_app` 802-807 against `app_raw_ssh_agent` 2382-2391 and `app_raw_limits` 2793-2806 — which exist only because the second pair was added 1,600 and 2,000 lines away from the first. Once all builders sit in the root `tests.rs` the overlap is visible and `app_raw_limits` collapses into a `raw_limits`-shaped call. I would not go further than that: `raw_network` 619-624, `raw_network_table` 627-653, `raw_network_allow` 656-658 and `net_field` 171-178 look collapsible and are not — each produces a different `NetworkField` shape, and `net_table_defaults` 145-168 carries an explicit argument (comment at 141-144) that `NetworkTable` derives no `Default` on purpose so the compiler refuses the next schema addition until every site says what it does with it. That is a guard living in a fixture; do not generalise it away.

**Do the work in this order, and stop after step 2 if the appetite runs out.** (1) tests/common/{fixture,cage,session}.rs plus the src/testutil.rs:604-607 guard re-base — this is where every measured duplication is, it touches no cage list, and it is the step that closes the audio-test gate gap at tests/run.rs:7495 and 7616. (2) the misfiled-cluster moves above — pure `git mv`-of-text, no new binaries, and they shrink the cage job. (3) the tests/run.rs split. (4) src/config/tests/. (5) src/sandbox/proxy/tests/, which is the least urgent of the five and the one I would happily see deferred indefinitely: it is 10,357 lines of genuinely well-ordered tests behind one real harness, and the only thing wrong with it is its size.

---
